**[FL-20260926-1245-oo] [owner-onboarding] [feature] [property-types] [bugfix] Owner self-serve onboarding link + owner billing address in the Hub + commercial property type + Add Property "fields persist" fix — branch `feat/owner-self-onboarding`, staging-verified, PR to `main` open for Brett's go/no-go, NOT merged.**

Full design/decisions/verification: `context/OWNER_SELF_ONBOARDING_BUILD_BRIEF_v1.0.md`. Brett's ask: a link he sends a new owner so they enter everything needed to bill them and service their tenants (name, business-or-name choice, contact phone, billing email, billing address, PIN, properties with the same fields as Add Property, units), landing in the Sheet/Hub with no manual entry; billing-address fields on Owners; commercial type with sub-types retail/mixed use/industrial/office; and the Add Property form no longer carrying the previous property's values.

worker.js: `OWNER SELF-SERVE ONBOARDING` block — pure `ownerOnboardValidate` / `ownerOnboardPinCheck` / `ownerOnboardInferMarket`, single-use invite tokens in new `Owner_Invites` tab (`ownerOnboardInviteCreate`/`Invites`/`InviteRevoke` admin; `ownerOnboardInfo`/`CheckPin`/`Submit` public via new PUBLIC_PATHS entries, each self-verifying the token — same pattern as `/vendor-setup/*`, `/wo/shared/*`). Submit: validate → PIN + phone uniqueness (before claiming, so a fixable problem never burns the link) → claim invite pending→processing→used (nonce re-read to win any race) → write Owners (via `addOwnerWithQBSync`), Properties, Units, Tenants, Keys → set Needs_Review + warnings → SMS `admin_phone`. Existing address never duplicated (unowned → linked; owned by another → skipped + flagged). New properties/tenants `SMS_Enabled=FALSE`; owner `SMS_Enabled` follows the consent checkbox; consent stored with timestamp/IP/version. `/owner-onboard/invites` (contains live links) added to the prod read-only token's deny-list `HUB_PROD_RO_EXCLUDE_PATHS`; invite create/revoke get TEST- guards in `hubTestWriteAllowed`. `/owner/add`+`/owner/update` now `ensureColumns` the `Billing_*` columns; `/property/add`+`propertyUpdate` ensure `Commercial_Subtype`. `BUILD_VERSION` → `2026-09-26.1-owner-self-onboarding`.

owner-onboard.html (new, public, mobile-first 3-step form): billing choice must be selected; PIN = platform format 3 letters + 5 digits (owner login + PIN sweep + selftest enforce it) with live checklist/availability/Suggest; house/rowhome unit count locked at 1, multi/condo/commercial required (multi 2+); commercial sub-type; owner lists only the unit(s) they want serviced; unit = occupied (tenant name + phone) / vacant (lockbox+code or no-lockbox+note) / "provide later" (never enough on its own); SMS consent checkbox unchecked by default with STOP/HELP/not-a-condition copy + links to bmoremanagement.com/sms-terms and /sms-privacy; draft autosave (never the PIN).

index.html: Billing section in Add/Edit Owner; Owners → "📨 Owner Onboarding Link" modal (create/copy/list/revoke + review warnings); Add/Edit Property gain Commercial + sub-type (`EP_MULTI_TYPES` now includes commercial); **Add Property fix:** `openAddPropertyModal` never cleared its inputs — new `apResetForm()` resets every field each open; Edit Property no longer silently shrinks an owner-onboarded property's owner-reported `Unit_Count` to the number of listed units.

Verified: `test/owner-onboarding.test.mjs` 86/86; `test/manual-verify-owner-onboard-ui.mjs` 52/52 (390px, mocked Worker); `test/manual-verify-hub-owner-property-ui.mjs` 54/54 against the real index.html (reopen test confirmed FAILING on the pre-fix file); full existing suite unchanged (2 pre-existing failures on `main`: `bill-to-note.test.mjs`, `receipt-attach-only.test.mjs`); live on `maintenance-hub-staging` with real read-back of Owners/Properties/Units/Tenants/Keys/Owner_Invites — invalid submit 422 without burning the link, full multi-property submit, replay 403, revoke-used 409, duplicate phone 409, owned-by-other address skipped + flagged. NOT exercised live: the unowned-property → link path (staging test token can't edit non-TEST rows), QuickBooks customer creation + admin SMS (stubbed on staging), `SMS_Consent_IP` (blank via service binding; real browser requests carry CF-Connecting-IP). Also note: `staging` had diverged from `main` (one conflicting devlog init line); brought current via PR #71/#72 with that line resolving to call both `loadVendorAccessRequests()` and `loadAlertFlags()`.

Follow-up, same day (Brett's review): (1) **PIN Suggest** on owner-onboard.html now builds our normal PIN — 3 random letters + the last 5 digits of the owner's phone (falls back to random digits when there's no phone yet or the last 5 would be a trivial run the PIN rules reject); if that PIN is taken it quietly retries with fresh letters (up to 5); the required format (3 letters + 5 digits, not a simple run, unique) is unchanged. (2) **Owner dropdowns:** `ownerLabel`/`ownerOptionsHtml` in index.html — business name first, else the person's name, never blank (falls back to Bill-To name, then `Owner #ID`); the contact's name is appended only where two active owners share a business name (e.g. the two Goldszmidt Properties contacts); active owners only (an inactive current owner stays selectable when editing); sorted. Replaces eight hand-rolled builders (Work Orders + Tenants filters, Add/Edit Property, bulk assign, master key, template, bulk access) — the old Add Property one rendered "undefined undefined" for company-only owners like Ridge Co. (3) **QuickBooks owner-customer email (real bug, found by checking live QuickBooks read-only via `/qb/entities`):** `qbFindOrCreateCustomer` set `PrimaryEmailAddr` only from `Billing_Email`, while its own look-before-create used `Billing_Email || Email` — so owners added with just the plain Email (Nirnay Pradhan → QB customer 460, Rei/Rooty Enterprises → 459) got QuickBooks customers with NO email and couldn't have invoices emailed. Now falls back to `Email`. `test/qb-owner-customer-email.test.mjs` (9 assertions, real function with stubbed QuickBooks) reproduces the bug on `main` and passes on the fix. **Existing customers 459/460 are NOT retroactively fixed** — run the Hub's "Fix QB emails" page (`/qb/backfill-emails`, a QuickBooks write, so Brett's own run). Tests: `owner-onboarding` 86, `qb-owner-customer-email` 9, `manual-verify-owner-onboard-ui` 60, `manual-verify-hub-owner-property-ui` 68 — all passing.

Second follow-up, same day: **Owners page "Set PIN" (backfill for owners who never set one).** New `🔑 Set PIN for Selected` button in the Owners action bar (tick one owner or many) plus `☑ Select owners with no PIN`. Opens an editable preview: each owner gets a proposed PIN (3 random letters + last 5 of their phone; random digits if no phone), live rule check (3 letters + 5 digits, no simple run, no duplicate in the list), Save writes only valid rows. One owner selected → replacing an existing PIN is offered; several → owners who already have a PIN are kept unless "replace existing" is ticked. Afterwards "Text these PINs" hands the saved owners to the existing send-PIN dialog. Worker: `POST /owner/pin-suggest` (proposes, writes nothing) and `POST /owner/set-pins` (validates via `ownerOnboardPinCheck`, refuses a PIN held by any other active Owner/Owner_Users/Vendor/Tenant login or repeated in the same batch, per-row results so one bad row never blocks the rest, max 200); both admin-only, and on the staging test token `set-pins` only reaches TEST- owners. Replaces the unvalidated legacy `/owner/set-pin` for this UI. Build `2026-09-26.2-owner-set-pin`. Tests: `owner-pin-backfill` 36, `manual-verify-owner-set-pin-ui` 33. Live on staging (TEST owner 25): suggest → set → read-back; weak-run PIN refused; another login's PIN (PHX58842) refused; non-TEST owner → 403.

**[FL-20260924-1333-vs] [vendor-billing] [feature] Vendor Standalone Billing (no-WO bills) + Self-Serve Work Orders ("One-Off Job") — feature branch + PR open, NOT merged.**

Per `context/VENDOR_STANDALONE_BILLING_AND_SELFSERVE_WO_BUILD_BRIEF_v1.0.md` (§2–§7). Two independent vendor-facing capabilities, both gated behind new per-vendor flags (default off for every existing vendor): (A) a vendor with `Can_Bill_No_WO=TRUE` can submit a bill for work that never had a Work Order (tenant treats, small extras) via a new "Submit a Bill" home-screen button in vendor.html, scoped to a `Billing_Property_Access` CSV list on their own Vendors row, with an in-app "request access to another property" flow that SMSes Brett and creates a `Vendor_Access_Requests` row for admin approval; (B) a vendor with `Can_Create_Own_WO=TRUE` can log their own Work Order via a new "Log a One-Off Job" button, self-assigned, with a mandatory `Approval_Source` attestation (owner/brett/other-with-required-note) and anti-overuse copy shown every time.

New worker.js: pure helpers `validateApprovalSource`, `vendorHasBillingPropertyAccess`, `computeStandaloneBillTotals` (unit-tested, see below); `addVendorBillStandalone` (writes a `Vendor_Bills` row with blank `WO_ID`, new `Property_ID`/`Bill_To`/`Standalone` columns, server-side re-checks `Can_Bill_No_WO` + billing-property access rather than trusting the client); `vendorRequestPropertyAccess` / `listVendorAccessRequests` / `vendorAccessRequestApprove` (new `Vendor_Access_Requests` tab); `workorderSelfServe` (wraps `createWorkOrder` + `assignVendor(...,{notify:false})`, forces `Vendor_ID` server-side to the caller's own id, new WO columns `Created_By_Vendor`/`Approval_Source`/`Approval_Note`); `vendorSetCanBillNoWO`/`vendorSetCanCreateOwnWO`/`vendorSetBillingPropertyAccess` admin toggles (exact `In_House` pattern). New endpoints: `POST /vendor-bill/add-standalone`, `POST /vendor/request-property-access`, `GET /vendor-access-requests`, `POST /vendor-access-requests/approve`, `POST /workorder/self-serve`, `POST /vendor/set-can-bill-no-wo`, `POST /vendor/set-can-create-own-wo`, `POST /vendor/set-billing-property-access`. New Vendors columns: `Can_Bill_No_WO`, `Can_Create_Own_WO`, `Billing_Property_Access`. New Vendor_Bills columns: `Property_ID`, `Bill_To`, `Standalone`. New Work_Orders columns: `Created_By_Vendor`, `Approval_Source`, `Approval_Note`.

QuickBooks send path risk (deliberately designed around, not discovered late): standalone bills all share a blank `WO_ID`, and the existing `qbGroupOpenRows` groups Invoice_Review rows by `WO_ID` — left alone, that would have silently merged unrelated standalone invoices into one QuickBooks send. Fixed by fully isolating a new `qbSendStandaloneInvoice(env, body, ctx)` function, dispatched from `qbSendInvoice` immediately on detecting `billRow.Standalone==='TRUE'` — before `wo`/`prop`/`owner` resolution and before `qbGroupOpenRows` ever runs. It resolves owner/customer straight from `Vendor_Bills.Property_ID` (mirroring `scopeSigResolveParties()`), and branches on `Bill_To` (owner invoice + vendor bill, vs. Ridge Co's-own-cost bill only, no invoice). `approveInvoiceReview` and Review Bills' `irCalc()` money math needed zero changes — both already tolerate a bill with no `wo_id` and are generic over `Bill_Type`/`Receipts_JSON`, which reduced scope versus a naive reading of the brief.

index.html: two new inline checkbox columns on the Vendors table ("No-WO Bill" / "One-Off Job") + matching bulk-action buttons; a property multi-select grid on the Edit Vendor modal for `Billing_Property_Access` (mirrors the existing Trades checkbox-grid); `renderIRCard` gets a standalone-bill display branch (resolves address from `Property_ID` since there's no WO, shows a "STANDALONE BILL — NO WO" badge + Bill_To label, and a dedicated info box instead of the "Work order not found" warning); Dev Log gets a new "Vendor Access Requests" section (Approve once / Approve ongoing / Deny) and both the Dev Log nav-tab badge and a Dashboard pending-count tile read from the same `GET /vendor-access-requests?status=pending` call so the two counts can't drift apart.

vendor.html: home-screen buttons shown per session flag (`can_bill_no_wo`/`can_create_own_wo`, both default hidden so an old cached session never shows either by accident); three new modals (Submit a Bill, Request Property Access, Log a One-Off Job) with repeatable line items reusing the existing Receipts_JSON reimburse/company-card pay-mode shape and a photo-upload-per-line-item flow; Spanish translations added for every new string.

Judgment calls: added `/properties` to the vendor role's endpoint allow-list (read-only, needed so vendor.html can populate the three new property pickers — other roles already get analogous property-list access); `notify:false` on the self-serve WO's self-assignment so a vendor logging their own job doesn't SMS themselves; the "Log a One-Off Job" property picker is deliberately NOT restricted to `Billing_Property_Access` (any active property), since that flag is specific to standalone billing.

Verified: `node --check` clean on worker.js and on every extracted `<script>` block in both index.html (5 blocks) and vendor.html (11 blocks); full read-back diff of all three files against the branch confirmed byte-identical (worker.js, vendor.html) or functionally identical (index.html — one harmless line-ordering difference) to what was authored; new `test/vendor-standalone-billing-selfserve.test.mjs` (21 pure-function tests, source-sliced straight from worker.js, no Sheets/QuickBooks credentials used or needed) — 21/21 pass. **Not verified — needs Brett:** a live Sheets write (new columns are lazy-provisioned via `ensureColumns` on first real write, never exercised here); a real QuickBooks send through `qbSendStandaloneInvoice` (owner-invoice and Ridge-Co-cost branches both); a real SMS via `vendorRequestPropertyAccess`; staging smoke test via `hub_test_get`/`hub_test_post` — attempted, 401'd on the new paths as expected, because maintenance-hub-staging only runs code synced from the `staging` branch, not this feature branch (confirmed staging itself is reachable — `/vendors` returned real data); `brett332/gh-broker` needs no update — its own path allow-lists were removed in the Sep 24 Allow-list Simplification build, so it forwards new paths straight through to worker.js's own gate with no separate change required. Branch `feature/vendor-standalone-billing-selfserve-wo-v2`, PR opened against `main`, not merged (GATED under `AUTONOMY_GUARDRAILS_v1.0.md` — money/QuickBooks + vendor-PII-adjacent).

**[FL-20260924-1815-as] [security] [gh-broker] Allow-list simplification, all 3 planned changes shipped (Brett, Sep 23: "I really want to be giving you more commands rather than you sending me back text that basically says I can't do what you want").**

Full design/history: `context/ALLOWLIST_SIMPLIFICATION_BUILD_BRIEF_v1.0.md` (now marked shipped). Change 1 — removed `brett332/gh-broker`'s redundant client-side path-allowlist arrays (`HUB_TEST_READ_PATHS`/`HUB_TEST_WRITE_PATHS`/`HUB_PROD_RO_READ_PATHS`/`HUB_PROD_WRITE_PATHS`/`HUB_PROD_QB_RO_PATHS`) — worker.js was always the real enforcement point, this copy only produced a friendlier early rejection and regularly drifted stale; direct commit to gh-broker `main` (no security boundary changed). Change 2 — broadened `HUB_TEST_TOKEN` (staging-only, gated by `isStaging()`) to any path/method, after confirming `hubTestWriteAllowed` default-denies (final fallthrough `return false`) so every write still resolves to a `TEST-`-marked record regardless of path. Change 3 — inverted `HUB_PROD_RO_TOKEN` from a ~12-path allow-list to an 8-path deny-list (`HUB_PROD_RO_EXCLUDE_PATHS`) after reading every one of worker.js's ~111 GET handlers by hand; excluded: `/notifications/pending` (live SMS), `/gmail/test`, `/gmail/callback`, `/qb/setup-trades`, `/daily-digest`, `/tenant-by-pin`, `/owner-by-pin`, `/vendor-by-pin` (each a confirmed real side effect). Changes 2+3 shipped as PR #55 (Ridge-Co/RidgeCo, sha `f1e23456bbab060d28e827e85a9af207f99ae835`), left open for Brett's own merge per PAT-033/AUTONOMY_GUARDRAILS_v1.0 — never auto-merged; Brett merged both PR #54 and #55 together ("go with pr 54 and pr 55"). Verified live on staging (`hub_test_get`) and production (`hub_prod_get`) against previously-blocked paths post-merge.
**[FL-20260924-1136-rb] [review-bills] [bugfix] [money-adjacent] Review Bills "Approve & send to QuickBooks" Cancel is now a true no-op — real incident: Eddie Smith's bill vanished from Review Bills after Cancel (CAP-036 #15).**

Root cause: `invBillThisJob` (index.html) is one button that does two real things in sequence — (1) `POST /invoice-review/approve`, a genuine, immediate, server-side state change (Vendor_Bills `Status` → `'reviewed'`, which is exactly what Review Bills' own `?status=submitted` query excludes), then (2) opens the "confirm send to QuickBooks" preview modal for the user to actually create the invoice/bill in QuickBooks. The modal's Cancel button only ever did `closeModal('modal-qb-send')` — a true no-op for step (2), but it did nothing about step (1), which had already committed before Cancel was ever visible. So pressing Cancel left the bill permanently approved-and-unsent, off Review Bills for good, recoverable only via "Withdraw approval" on the Send to QuickBooks page — a path Brett had no reason to know about.

Fix: `previewQBSend(id, justApproved)` now tracks whether THIS specific call is the one that just approved the bill (passed `true` only from `invBillThisJob`'s fresh-approval path — not from the "already approved" fast path, not from the Send-to-QB queue's own Preview & Send button, not from `sendWOToQuickBooks`'s fallback lookup). The modal's Cancel button now calls `cancelQBSend()`: if this modal opening followed a fresh approval, Cancel also POSTs `/invoice-review/unapprove` for that same id (the existing, pre-built, already-tested endpoint that already handles this for the Send-to-QB page's own "Withdraw approval" button — same guarantees: refuses once the bill is genuinely in QuickBooks, restores Vendor_Bills to `'submitted'`, read-back verified) — so Cancel is now a true no-op for the WHOLE action and the bill reappears in Review Bills untouched. If the bill was already approved before this click, Cancel leaves that approval alone exactly as before (regression-safety case).

Verified: two headless Playwright cases (`test/manual-verify-review-bills-cancel-noop-ui.mjs`) against the real shipped index.html — Case 1 (fresh-approval path): Cancel calls `/invoice-review/unapprove` with the right id, never calls the real (non-preview) `/qb/send-invoice`, and the user is told the bill is back in Review Bills. Case 2 (pre-existing approval): Cancel calls neither endpoint. Both cases confirmed to FAIL against the pre-fix code (proving the tests actually catch the regression) and PASS (8/8) against the fix.

**Production finding (read-only, not acted on):** Eddie Smith's WO-1118 bill (Vendor_Bills row ID 56, 2930 St Paul St — HVAC condensate leak/dehumidifier, vendor cost $225, customer total $608.65) is `Status='reviewed'` with `Reviewed_Date = 2026-09-24` (today) but has **no matching bill or invoice in QuickBooks at all** (confirmed via `hub_prod_qb_query` `/qb/vendor-reconcile` — vendor_qbo_id 294's QB bill list has no WO-1118 entry, while every one of Eddie's other 10 "reviewed" bills does). This is exactly the stuck state the Cancel bug produces and is very likely THE bill from the real incident. It is fully recoverable — it already shows on the "Send to QuickBooks" page (approved, awaiting send) and can be sent normally, or withdrawn back to Review Bills to reprice — but no write was made to it; that's Brett's call. A sweep of every other vendor with a "reviewed" bill (Alex Busey, Allen George, Andreas Cleaning, Cesar Diaz, Michelle Dykes, Oscar Padilla, Robert Whitley, Towson, Juana Gomez — 91 other reviewed bills total) found every single one already has a matching QuickBooks bill — Eddie Smith's WO-1118 is the only one currently stuck. (Brett Lambert's own 30 in-house reviewed jobs use a separate no-vendor-bill/customer-invoice-only path this audit's tool couldn't check QuickBooks-invoice presence for — flagging as an unchecked gap, not a finding.)

PR: https://github.com/Ridge-Co/RidgeCo/pull/56 (not yet merged — Brett reviews per PAT-033). Files: index.html, test/manual-verify-review-bills-cancel-noop-ui.mjs.

**[FL-20260924-1130-pn] [messaging] [bugfix] Property Notice modal now updates owner/property info reliably when switching properties (CAP-036 #9).**

Root cause: `propertyNoticePreviewNow()` (index.html) had no stale-response guard. Opening the modal already fires its own preview request (twice, chained — the first blank-message call auto-fills the SMS template text, which then re-triggers a second real preview call), and if the user switched to a different property while that first property's request was still in flight, a slower response for the property they'd already left could resolve AFTER the new property's faster response and silently overwrite it — the exact "opened on 20 East Eager Street, switching properties didn't update" behavior Brett reported. Not a missing onchange handler; the handler always fired correctly, the response ordering just wasn't guaranteed.

Fix: a monotonically-increasing request id (`_pnReqId`) is bumped on every property-select change; a response is only rendered if it's still the latest request AND the property picker still reads the property that request was for. Also added: switching the property now clears the recipients line immediately (`propertyNoticePropertyChanged()`) instead of leaving stale text visible until the debounced fetch resolves, and the owner name (already returned by the backend, `res.owner`, but never displayed) is now shown alongside the address and tenant/recipient counts.

Verified: headless Playwright pass (`test/manual-verify-property-notice-refresh-ui.mjs`) against the real shipped index.html — opens the modal on property "20 East Eager Street" (deliberately slow-responding mock), switches to "115 West 29th Street" (fast-responding mock) while the first request is still in flight, and asserts the screen shows the second property both immediately after switching and after the first (stale) response finally arrives late. Confirmed to FAIL against the pre-fix code (reproduces the exact bug) and PASS against the fix (8/8).

PR: https://github.com/Ridge-Co/RidgeCo/pull/56 (not yet merged). Files: index.html, test/manual-verify-property-notice-refresh-ui.mjs.

**[FL-20260922-2250-gc] [gemini-integration] [infra] /gemini-context: token-gated Worker endpoint replaces a fragile Google Drive source for Brett's Gemini Notebook.**

Brett wants Gemini (Notebook/Gem) grounded on the same background context Claude has. The first pass stored the snapshot as a Google Drive doc added to the Notebook as a source — but the Drive connector's `update_file` only changes title/parentId, never content, so refreshing it meant trash-and-recreate, which mints a new file ID and silently orphans the Notebook's source on every single refresh. Brett caught this before it shipped.

Fix: serve the snapshot from a stable URL on production `maintenance-hub` instead, so there's no file ID to break.
- **`GET /gemini-context?token=...`** — added to `PUBLIC_PATHS` (self-verified, same pattern as the Shareable Work Order precedent), but `geminiContext()` checks `?token=` against `env.GEMINI_CONTEXT_TOKEN` before returning anything. Query-string, not a header, because a Gemini Notebook website-source is fetched as a plain unauthenticated GET with no custom headers available. Wrong/missing token → plain 404, not 401, so the endpoint doesn't announce itself. Returns `Config.Gemini_Context_Snapshot` as `text/plain`.
- **`POST /admin/gemini-context-update`** — added to the existing `HUB_PROD_WRITE_PATHS` allow-list (reuses `HUB_PROD_WRITE_TOKEN`, no new write secret). Body `{content}`, written via the existing `setConfigKey()` helper. gh-broker's own separate `hub_prod_post` allow-list needed the same path added (PR #7) — it's a second, client-side list distinct from worker.js's own gate.
- The daily scheduled snapshot-refresh task was rewired to POST here instead of touching Drive; the Notebook's Drive-doc source was removed and replaced with the token URL as a "website" source, verified live via a real Notebook query.

`worker.js`: PR #33 (Ridge-Co/RidgeCo). `gh-broker`: PR #7 (brett332/gh-broker). Both merged Sep 22 2026.

**[FL-20260922-1620-ip] [receipt-reconciler] [ux] Reconciler actions update in place (no full-page reload); receipt-date cutoff (default 2026-07-01); ↩ Move back to Pending.**

Brett: "it drives me crazy to have to scroll down through a whole bunch of receipts to get to the one I was just working on." Every action (Confirm, expense taps, business-expense confirm, Skip, Confirm duplicate, "It's this one") used to call `setTimeout(loadQueue, …)`, which rebuilt the whole list and threw him back to the top. The fix is `finishCard(id)`: it collapses just that card, so the next receipt slides up into the same spot with the scroll position kept. The result goes to a small toast at the bottom, so warnings like "already invoiced — flagged for repair" aren't lost when the card leaves. The pending count updates without a reload. "Check duplicates" now rewrites only that card's evidence block (`evidenceHtml(r)` in `#rcN-evidence`), so anything typed or picked in the card survives. ↻ Refresh and tab switches still do a full reload.

Brett also saw 2023/2024/2025 receipts in Pending. Traced to folder scans (queued Aug 23–Sep 1 from `Receipt - …`/`Scanned_…` files), not the email pull, which never reaches before Jul 1 and contributed 0 of the 9 pre-July rows. Added a receipt-date cutoff:
- Config `receipt_recon_min_date` (yyyy-mm-dd; default/garbage → `2026-07-01`).
- `receiptReconScan` queues anything dated earlier as **skipped** with a plain note. A receipt with no readable date stays Pending and is never hidden.
- New `POST /receipt-recon/skip-before-cutoff {dry_run?}` does the one-time cleanup of the rows already queued.
- There was previously no way to un-skip, so `POST /receipt-recon/unskip {id}` was added (skipped → pending only). Skipped cards show the note and a **↩ Move back to Pending** button.

Verified: `node --check` clean.
- New `test/receipt-recon-cutoff-unskip.test.mjs` (11/11).
- `test/receipt-recon-scan-cap.test.mjs` grew to 22/22 with the cutoff cases.
- New headless `test/manual-verify-receipt-inplace-ui.mjs` (17/17, 390px). It covers: no queue reload on any action, scroll position unchanged (6080→6080), the next card landing in the same screen spot, typed text surviving both a neighbor's Skip and a duplicate check, the repair warning surviving in the toast, the cutoff note, and un-skip.
- The earlier expense UI check still passes 13/13.
- Full suite 85/85 on a fresh clone of the branch.

`BUILD_VERSION` → `2026-09-22.4-receipt-inplace-cutoff`.

**[FL-20260922-1545-ex] [receipt-reconciler] [quickbooks] [expense] One-tap expense receipts (Ridge Co / 1864 Kerns School Rd / picked property) sent to QuickBooks immediately; unreadable scan files stop retrying.**

Brett's ask: attach receipts to Ridge Co or 1864 Kerns School Rd as an expense instead of picking a work order; route them to QuickBooks for the expense record, and put them on work orders only when he chooses to. Goal: clear the backlog faster. The pieces already existed, but three problems blocked this:
1. The no-WO confirm button only appeared when the scanner classified a receipt as `company` (a "bmore" PO). Home Depot receipts with an address never got it.
2. When a `billable`-suggested receipt was confirmed with `no_wo`, `receiptReconConfirm` kept `category = billable`, leaving a billable row with nothing to bill.
3. Receipts only reached QuickBooks through the 7am `sendReceiptsToQBEmail` sweep, which is capped at 8 a day.

Fixed:
- `receipt-reconciler.html`: every pending, non-excluded card gets a green "Expense only" bar with **🧾 Ridge Co expense** (no property), **🏡 1864 Kerns School Rd** (found by address, so no hardcoded ID), and, on billable cards, **Expense to the property picked above**. One tap posts `no_wo:true`.
- `worker.js` `receiptReconConfirm`: `no_wo` now always records category `company`. After a successful expense record it calls `sendReceiptsToQBEmail(env, {ids:[newId], limit:1})`, sending **only that receipt** to QuickBooks immediately, and returns `qb_email: {sent, error}`, which the card shows ("Sent to QuickBooks", or a warning that the 7am sweep will retry).
- `sendReceiptsToQBEmail` gains an optional `ids` filter. The QB email for a property receipt now reads "Expense — property: …".
- Work-order confirms are unchanged: billable on the WO, folded into the invoice, and sent to QB by the daily sweep.

Also fixed, from the same session:
- **`receiptReconScan` retried unreadable files forever.** `recon_smoke_test.png` ("Could not process image") cost an OCR call and logged an error on every scan. It now uses the same 3-attempt Config tracker `receiptScan` already had (Config key `receipt_recon_failures`), and after that the file is skipped and listed under `stuck`.
- **The Scan button's result line was being overwritten.** `loadQueue()` replaced it with "✅ N pending" the moment the list reloaded. That means PR #21's "N more waiting, tap Scan again" note never actually stayed on screen. Caught by the new headless test. The scan result now stays in front of the pending count.

Verified: `node --check` clean on worker.js and the page script. New `test/receipt-expense-path.test.mjs` (14/14) extracts the real `receiptReconConfirm` + `sendReceiptsToQBEmail` and covers the expense category, send-only-this-one, the Kerns wording, the unchanged WO path, and the double-tap guard. `test/receipt-recon-scan-cap.test.mjs` grew to 16/16 with the unreadable-file cases. New `test/manual-verify-receipt-expense-ui.mjs` (headless Chromium, 390px phone width) passes 13/13: exact payloads per button, the refuse-with-no-property case, no sideways scroll, and the scan status line. Full suite 84/84. `BUILD_VERSION` → `2026-09-22.3-receipt-expense-path`.

**Known, not built:** the Hub OCR reads Home Depot **return** receipts as positive purchases. The Aug 24 −$111.18 refund queued as +$111.18. Skip returns by hand until refund detection is built.

**[FL-20260922-1900-rm] [receipt-reconciler] [email-intake] [apps-script] Receipt Mail → Hub — emailed receipts (Home Depot first) flow into the Reconciler scan folder via Gmail filters + Apps Script, no AI retrieval, no Cloudflare cron; plus a batch cap on `receiptReconScan`.**

Brett needed Home Depot e-receipts out of email and into billing, and wanted a documented, filter-driven path with no AI retrieval, kept off Cloudflare cron. Found live on Sep 22: brett@ has had no HD e-receipts since Jun 30. Brett confirmed they now go to info@. HD e-receipts carry an `eReceipt.pdf` attachment. Lowe's "Ready for Pick Up" emails have no prices, and Amazon "Ordered:" emails have no tax, so those two are left to approval rather than pre-approved.

Built `apps-script/receipt-mail-to-hub/` (Apps Script, installed per mailbox, brett@ and info@, sharing one sheet):
- Approved rules become real Gmail filters that apply the label `Receipts/To Hub`.
- A 10-minute trigger works only that label. It saves the PDF/photo into "Receipts and Invoices", or renders the HTML body to PDF, then relabels the thread `Sent to Hub` and writes a Log row.
- A weekly discovery adds Pending sender+subject candidates. A Monday digest asks Brett to Approve or Deny.
- Approving a rule backfills its emails back to `Backfill_Since` (2026/07/01).
- Cross-mailbox dedup uses the RFC Message-ID header. There are 3 retries before an email moves to `Receipts/Hub Error`.
- A hand-applied label works for vendor-forwarded orders.

Full design, setup and troubleshooting: `context/RECEIPT_MAIL_TO_HUB_v1.0.md`. Offline harness: 51/51, covering two mailboxes, backfill cutoff, dedup, the approve→filter→backfill cycle, deny→filter removal, the manual-tag thread rule, the retry/error path and the digest.

Worker: `receiptReconScan(env, body)` now caps new files per call. The default is 8, Hub-tap `body.max` and Config `receipt_recon_scan_batch` can change it (clamped to 1–10), and the daily cron passes 5. The response gains `remaining`, and the Reconciler's Scan button says "N more waiting, tap Scan again". Reason: each file costs about 4 subrequests, and an uncapped backfill drop would blow the per-invocation subrequest limit inside the shared daily cron. That's the same failure class as rule 155a. New `test/receipt-recon-scan-cap.test.mjs` extracts the real function (12/12); it caught a fallback bug where a negative max clamped to 1 instead of defaulting to 8. `node --check` clean on worker.js and receipt-reconciler.html's inline script. Full suite 82/82 on a fresh clone of the branch. `BUILD_VERSION` → `2026-09-22.2-receipt-scan-batch-cap`.

**Not verifiable from the build sandbox:** real Gmail filter creation and HTML→PDF rendering inside Apps Script. The first `setup()` run is the live check: its execution log prints filters created and emails queued. Backfilled receipts that were already entered by hand need the Reconciler's on-demand duplicate check before Confirm.

**[FL-20260922-1500-rm] [scope-proposal] [materials] [gmail] [billing] Ridge Co–supplied materials on scope proposals + budget-vs-actual/double-billing guard + "Reconnect Gmail" sign-in.** Two live problems from Brett (Sep 22): (1) Scope Creator had no way to include materials Ridge Co buys itself — every item needed vendor cost > $0 (frontend `itemsFullyPriced` + backend `scopeProposal` gate) and 100% of vendor cost goes onto the vendor bill / milestone `Vendor_Amount`, so he typed a fake $1 vendor cost that would have been paid to the vendor; (2) "Send estimate to owner" failed with Gmail `invalid_grant` — root cause: the OAuth consent screen was still in **Testing** (Sep 7 fix notes needed a "Test users" entry), and Google kills Testing-mode refresh tokens after 7 days. Brett published the app to **In production** himself (Sep 22) — that fixes the weekly expiry; this build removes the manual re-mint.

**Materials (per option):** new `rc_materials_cost` + `rc_materials_desc` on each variant (kept by `scopeCleanVariants` only when used — old variants keep their exact shape). Never part of `vendor_cost`, so never on a vendor bill or `Vendor_Amount`. Materials-only items (vendor cost $0) now count as priced. Per-proposal pricing mode (`Scopes.Materials_Pricing_JSON`, saved on Generate): **same markup** (option priced as one job of vendor+materials through `calcTieredEstimate`, materials share split out proportionally — no second minimum/admin fee), **at cost × card fee**, or **flat % × card fee**. `price_override` covers the labor part only when materials exist. Option `price` stays the TOTAL so view/sign subtotals are unchanged. Owner sees an "Includes materials: <desc> ($X)" line — description + their price only; `scopeProposalView` strips `rc_materials_cost` (runtime-tested, not just source-checked). Materials description goes through `findVendorPricingLeak` at generation. `scopeProposalSign` records `RC_Materials_Cost`/`RC_Materials_Price` on `Scope_Signatures` (self-provisioned via `ensureColumns`).

**Budget vs actual + double-billing guard:** `scopeCoveringSignature` = active signature on the WO's scope **with `RC_Materials_Cost > 0`**. Only those jobs are covered — proposals signed without materials (every pre-Sep-22 one) are deliberately untouched so their receipts still bill through Review Bills (blocking them would lose money; caught by the independent review pass). Covered jobs: `/receipts-billed` marks every receipt billed (index.html picker already disables them — no index.html change); Receipt Reconciler records the receipt but skips `appendReceiptToInvoiceReview`; `approveInvoiceReview` + `approveInvoiceReviewBulk` refuse covered receipts server-side (409; 503 fail-closed if the check can't read); `qbSendInvoice` preview warns if an invoice approved before signing carries receipts. Signed Proposals shows budget (cost + owner price) vs matched Ridge Co receipts (vendor-logged `Role:'vendor'` excluded), with within / over / not-bought states; overage copy says it is NOT billed and to bill it manually in QuickBooks (no change-order system exists yet).

**Reconnect Gmail:** `POST /gmail/connect-url` (admin) → Google consent (`gmail.send openid email`, offline, `prompt=consent`, `login_hint` = sender) with a signed 15-min `gmail-oauth` state → `GET /gmail/callback` (PUBLIC path, self-verifies state; a state token has no `role` so it can't pass the session gate, and no session token has scope `gmail-oauth`) → exchanges code, requires id_token email == `GMAIL_SENDER` (verified) + `gmail.send` granted + a refresh token, stores Config `GMAIL_REFRESH_TOKEN` + `GMAIL_TOKEN_UPDATED`. `gmailAccessToken` now tries Config token first, then the env secret (only `invalid_grant` falls through). `GET /config` redacts `GMAIL_REFRESH_TOKEN`. `/health` shows `gmail.config_token_set/config_token_updated` (presence/date only); new admin `GET /gmail/token-check` = no-send live refresh check. Scope Creator shows a "🔑 Reconnect Gmail" button only after a Gmail-auth send failure. **One-time Google setup:** OAuth client (Web application) must list `https://maintenance-hub.brett-2f8.workers.dev/gmail/callback` as an Authorized redirect URI.

Also fixed in passing: `scopeCommand` (natural-language "Apply" on items) reset every kept item to a single $0 option because the model never returns variants — it now always carries the saved variants back by id.

Verification: `node --check` clean; full suite 82/82 on a fresh clone of the branch; new `test/scope-rc-materials.test.mjs` (71 assertions incl. runtime `scopeProposalView` leak check, milestone vendor-side excludes materials, byte-identical no-materials pricing); new `test/manual-verify-rc-materials-ui.mjs` (35 Playwright checks at 390px across scope-creator / scope-proposal / signed-proposals, zero page errors). Independent adversarial review found 2 HIGH (over-broad block on no-materials proposals; nonexistent "change order" advice) + 4 MEDIUM + 3 LOW — all fixed before push except as noted in the PR. **Not live-verified** (no staging deploy path for a feature branch — staging only deploys from the `staging` branch): needs Brett's live pass after merge. `BUILD_VERSION` left at main's `2026-09-22.2-receipt-scan-batch-cap` (PR #21 bumped the same line; changing it here would conflict) — confirm THIS build is deployed by `/health` showing the new `gmail.config_token_set` field.
**✅ Merged + live (Sep 22 2026, PR #22 → `eaa583b`):** `/health` showed the new `gmail.*config_token*` fields right after deploy; live GitHub Pages `scope-creator.html` carries the Reconnect button + RC materials inputs. Brett added the redirect URI and reconnected — `/health` → `config_token_set:true`, `config_token_updated 2026-09-22T19:35:15.935Z`. Brett re-priced the $1-hack scope with RC materials. **Regression rule:** Gmail OAuth consent screen must stay **In production** (Testing = 7-day refresh-token death); re-auth is Scope Creator → Reconnect Gmail, never a hand-minted Cloudflare secret. Receipts on a signed proposal WITH `RC_Materials_Cost > 0` must never reach an Invoice_Review — don't widen or remove `scopeCoveringSignature`'s materials condition without re-reading this entry.

**[FL-20260922-0910-vp] [vendor-performance] [reporting] B-012 Vendor Performance dashboard — read-only, admin-only vendor scorecard, built from the Optimizer Prepare Agent's RUNG-1 SAFE-class brief.** Brett said "Build it" on the brief; PAT-024 (open the real handler before editing) caught one real drift between the brief and the live code before anything shipped: the brief's draft assumed `Vendors.Company` was the display-name column — the live `Vendors` tab actually carries both `Name` (the real display name, confirmed in `renderVendorsPage`) and `Company` (secondary/business name shown alongside it) — and assumed a single `Trade` field where the live tab uses `Trades` (comma list) with `Trade` only as a legacy fallback. Built against the verified fields: `v.Name || v.Company`, `v.Trades || v.Trade`. Every other field the brief cited (`Vendor_ID`, `Status`, `Voided`, `Active`, `Total`/`Customer_Total`, `Role`/`Entered_By_ID`/`Duration_Minutes`) matched the real handlers exactly.

New `GET /vendor-performance` (`worker.js`) — modeled on `arAging` (aggregate → roll-up → `json({...})`), added directly beside it. Aggregates `Work_Orders`+`Vendor_Bills`+`Time_Entries` by active vendor: `jobs_total`/`jobs_completed`/`jobs_open`/`jobs_open_urgent`, `completion_rate`, `avg_days_to_complete`, `total_billed`, `bill_count`, `avg_bill`, `labor_hours`, `last_activity`. `OPEN_WO_STATUSES` and the new local `DONE` set (`Complete`/`Pending Invoice`/`Invoiced`/`Paid`) both contain Complete/Pending Invoice by design — `OPEN_WO_STATUSES` means "still needs billing follow-up" (the vendor-portal sense it was built for), `DONE` means "the job itself is finished." The `open` filter's `&& !DONE.has(w.Status)` guard reconciles the two so a completed-but-unbilled job is never double-counted as both done and open — verified with a synthetic-data test (see below). One router line added after the `/ar-report/view` GET route; **not added to `PUBLIC_PATHS`**, so it stays admin-gated (`WORKER_SECRET`) by omission, same as every other admin report. `BUILD_VERSION` → `2026-09-22.1-vendor-performance-dashboard`.

New "📊 VENDOR PERFORMANCE" nav item + page in `index.html`, fetch-on-open (`showPage`'s existing lazy-dispatch pattern, same as Who To Pay/Review Bills) via `loadVendorPerformance()` → `api('GET','/vendor-performance')`. Sortable table (`sortVendorPerf(key)`/`renderVendorPerformanceTable()` — click any header, ascending default for text columns, descending for numeric), summary line, clean empty state, styled with the existing `data-table`/`badge`/`loading` classes — no new CSS.

`node --check` clean on `worker.js` and all 5 inline `<script>` blocks in `index.html`. No `WORKER_SECRET` in this build sandbox, so the real Sheets-backed test suite couldn't run against this — instead wrote and ran a standalone 20-assertion pure-logic test against synthetic data (mirroring the real function's logic exactly, not committed to the repo) covering: voided WOs excluded, inactive bills/vendors/time-entries excluded, the DONE/OPEN_WO_STATUSES overlap resolves correctly, `Customer_Total` fallback, `Trade`/`Trades` fallback, sort order, and totals math — all 20 passed. Also grep-confirmed `/vendor-performance` is genuinely absent from `PUBLIC_PATHS`, and the pushed branch's `worker.js`/`index.html` were read back from GitHub and diffed byte-for-byte against the locally verified copy before opening the PR — identical both times.

**Pushed as a branch + PR** (`feature/vendor-performance-dashboard`, PR #19: https://github.com/Ridge-Co/RidgeCo/pull/19), not direct to `main` — per PAT-033, and specifically because this same file's Sep 19-20 entries (CAP-035, Queue #24) each flagged skipping that branch-first+staging-verify step as a process miss; this build goes through it properly. **Needs Brett's live pass before merge**: `GET /vendor-performance` with the admin token → 200 + real vendor rows; same call with no token → 401; for one real vendor, confirm `jobs_total` matches `GET /vendor-workorders?vendor_id=<id>&include_closed=true` row count and `total_billed` matches that vendor's active `Vendor_Bills.Total` sum; open the Hub, tap "VENDOR PERFORMANCE," confirm it renders cleanly on a phone with no console errors and column-sort works.

**[FL-20260920-1710-sb] [test-infra] [deploy-pipeline] HUB_TEST_TOKEN staging smoke tests were silently testing dead code all day — root cause found and fixed, no code bug involved.** A day-long investigation into every `POST /workorder` (and by the same code path `/assign`/`/status`) via `hub_test_post` returning 403 "does not resolve to a TEST- record" — even against a freshly-created, independently-verified-correct `TEST-`-marked Property — chased several code-level theories (a swallowed exception in `isTestRecord()`'s try/catch, a Google Sheets API read-consistency race) across four shipped diagnostic patches, none of which changed the outcome.

The actual cause: `maintenance-hub-staging` is a separate Cloudflare Worker from production `maintenance-hub`, with its own git integration, whose Branch control is configured with `staging` (not `main`) as its **production branch**. Cloudflare's Workers Build only runs the real `Deploy command` (`npx wrangler deploy`, promotes to live traffic) for pushes to that production branch; every other branch — including `main`, where all of today's work was landing — only runs the `Version command` (`npx wrangler versions upload`), which builds and stores a version but never promotes it. The repo's `staging` branch existed but had never been kept in sync with `main` since the original Sep 19 test-infra build — it was ~457 commits behind. So every merge to `main` today built successfully as a Cloudflare "version" (confirmed via the Cloudflare API's worker-source-inspection tool, which reads the latest uploaded source regardless of what's actually live) but never reached the Worker actually serving `hub_test_*` traffic. `workers_get_worker_code` and real served behavior can silently diverge on this Worker for exactly this reason — don't trust one to imply the other.

Diagnosed by adding a `build_version` field to `/health`'s response and bumping `BUILD_VERSION`, then polling `hub_test_get('/health')` directly across multiple merges — it never changed, which was the actual tell (a live-traffic check, not a source-inspection check). Fixed with a one-time `git push origin main:staging --force` (run by Brett via a GitHub Codespace, since GH Broker's git write tools are intentionally fast-forward-only and correctly refused the non-trivial merge). Confirmed immediately via `/health` picking up the new `build_version`, then a full `/workorder` → `/assign` → `/status` write lifecycle succeeding end-to-end for the first time. The guard logic itself (`isTestRecord`/`hubTestWriteAllowed`, marker-field checks on linked Property/Tenant/Vendor) was correct the entire session — **no worker.js logic changes were actually required to fix this**, though the diagnostic `{ok, debug}` return-contract patch on the `/workorder` guard check is now live and harmless to keep (staging-only, inside the existing `_viaHubTestToken` branch). Full writeup: project doc `ridgeco-gh-broker-hub-test-infra.md`.

**Open follow-up, not yet decided:** whether to point `maintenance-hub-staging`'s production branch directly at `main` (removes the manual-sync step permanently, but changes what "staging" means) or keep `staging` as a separately-synced branch (requires remembering to sync it, as just happened). Until decided, any session running `hub_test_*` smoke tests after merging real work to `main` needs to confirm `staging` is current first — check `hub_test_get('/health')`'s behavior against what you'd expect from your own merge, don't assume a clean `workers_get_worker_code` read means your change is live.

**[FL-20260920-1700-sb] [vendor-portal] [notifications] Invoice Submitted vendor-bill status + per-audience notify controls (PR #9, open).** Brett flagged two vendor-portal bugs: Open Work Orders not sorting completed jobs to the bottom, and no interim WO status when a vendor submits a bill. **[FL-20260919-1315-sb] [ops-build-queue] [automation] Ops_Build_Queue → Start Build — one click on proposals.html fires a real Claude Code cloud session that builds a batch of greenlit/prepared items and reports each one back as done/held on its own.** Brett's ask: approve a build, press a button, have it route straight into a new Claude Code cloud session that builds it, updates the queue live, and marks the item done as its own last step — batchable (one session can build several items at once; a new session is only spun up per batch, not per item), and Reuse-Radar-sourced items treated identically to Reviewer/Product-sourced ones (confirmed they already land in the same `Ops_Build_Queue` — nothing there needed separate wiring).

Researched what's actually available rather than guessing: Anthropic's Claude Code **Routines API** (`POST https://api.anthropic.com/v1/claude_code/routines/{id}/fire`, external HTTP call, per-routine bearer token, fires a real full agentic Claude Code session with GH Broker and all, returns immediately with a session URL) is a materially better fit than the Worker-cron path `AUTONOMY_RUNG2_SAFE_CLASS_BUILD_BRIEF_v1.0.md` sketched on Sep 17 — that brief didn't know this API existed; it's still the right doc for the SAFE/GATED framing this build reuses.

- `worker.js`: `held` added to `OPS_QUEUE_STATUSES` (a failed/blocked build attempt, distinct from `dropped` = proposal rejected), new `Held_Note` column (`ensureColumns`, not `ensureTab`, per rule 37/78). `POST /ops-queue-status {id,status,note?}` — narrow `OPS_QUEUE_TOKEN`-or-`WORKER_SECRET`, added to the same allowlist `/ops-queue-prepare` lives on; structurally can only ever set `building`/`done`/`held` (never greenlit/prepared/dropped) and only from a current greenlit/prepared/building row (`validateQueueStatusReport`, pure/tested). This is the fired session's own per-item callback. `POST /ops-queue/start-build {ids:[1-20]}` — `WORKER_SECRET` only (the one write on this queue that actually spends). Validates every id via `isEligibleForStartBuild` (pure/tested): `Risk_Class==='SAFE'` fail-closed same as `opsApprove`, `Status` in `{greenlit,prepared,held}`. Marks eligible rows `building` in one batched Sheets write, builds the routine-fire `text` via `buildStartBuildRoutineText` (pure/tested — one delimited section per item, verbatim `Build_Brief` if present else a "research from scratch" instruction, plus a fixed instructions block embedding the real `OPS_QUEUE_TOKEN` for the fired session's own callback), fires the routine, returns `{fired_ids, skipped, session_id, session_url}`. **Rolls back every touched row's original `Status` on any failure path** (routine not configured, fire fails, network error) — an item can never get stuck at `building` from a fire that never actually happened. `/health` gains `routine_fire_token_set`/`routine_id_set` booleans (presence only, never values). New `test/ops-queue-build-trigger.test.mjs`, 45 assertions across the three pure helpers. `node --check` clean, full suite 86/87 (same single pre-existing unrelated `trade-map.test.mjs` failure as before — zero regressions). `BUILD_VERSION` → `2026-09-19.2`.
- `proposals.html`: the "Greenlit — build queue" cards now show a Risk_Class chip (SAFE/GATED), a Build_Brief-ready indicator, and a status-aware chip (building=amber, held=red + `Held_Note` shown inline, prepared=blue). Eligible rows (SAFE + greenlit/prepared/held) get a checkbox; GATED rows get a 🔒 in its place ("needs your own session, not auto-buildable"); an already-`building` row gets a ⏳. A new "Start Build (N)" button in the bottom bar (separated from "Approve selected", which now only shows when proposals are actually selected) POSTs the batch to `/ops-queue/start-build` and shows the returned session link + any skipped-item reasons inline. The queue was already one unified list regardless of originating lens/tag — confirmed, not changed.

**Not yet live** — needs Brett's own one-time setup, outside any repo: create a Claude Code "routine" at claude.ai/code/routines (attach the GH Broker connector + `Ridge-Co/RidgeCo`), generate its API trigger token, then set `ROUTINE_ID` + `ROUTINE_FIRE_TOKEN` as Cloudflare Worker secrets on `maintenance-hub`. Until both are set, Start Build fails clean with `routine_not_configured` (covered by the rollback-path tests) rather than silently doing nothing. Full setup steps + the routine's own saved base prompt handed to Brett directly; also in the Continuous Improvement project's `ridgeco-optimizer-prepare-ship-queue.md`. No live `WORKER_SECRET`/`ROUTINE_FIRE_TOKEN` in this build sandbox, so the actual round-trip and a real routine fire are unverified beyond static correctness + the pure-helper suite — same standing limitation as every other `Ops_Build_Queue` build in this repo.

Brett's answers this session: (1) fold into the existing Owners/Properties edit modals, no standalone settings page; (2) the admin-side `Managed_By` toggle shows the same confirm-dialog weight `owner.html`'s own `toggleManagedBy` does; (3) Brett flagging a WO as owner-managed himself DOES notify the owner, either direction.

- **Edit Owner modal**: new Tenant Work Order Submission toggle (blank/ON/OFF) + scoped property IDs + a held-contact-note textarea (shown to a tenant on any WO this owner has claimed as their own). All three save through their own dedicated endpoints (`/owner/tenant-wo-toggle`, `/owner/held-contact-note`) fired alongside the existing `/owner/update` call — not folded into that call's generic fields, since those two endpoints `ensureColumns` first and the generic update doesn't. A "currently ON for N of M properties" resolved-state line is fetched from `GET /tenant-wo-settings` (cached client-side via `loadTenantWOSettings`, refreshed on open/save) so the modal shows what the owner-overrides-property hierarchy actually resolves to, not just the raw stored value.

Same resolved-state line, flagging when an owner-level override is in play.
- **WO detail modal**: a "WHO'S HANDLING THIS JOB" section mirroring `owner.html`'s own wording, with a HAND THIS TO THE OWNER / TAKE THIS BACK FOR RIDGE CO button. `adminToggleManagedBy()` shows a confirm dialog matching `toggleManagedBy`'s weight (claiming/releasing a job is a real workflow decision, not a display preference) — the confirm text itself states the owner will be texted, so that isn't a silent side effect.

Wrapped in its own try/catch — a notify failure never undoes the WO field update that already succeeded.

New `test/tenant-wo-settings-ui.test.mjs` (26/26 — every modal field, population-from-real-record, and save-path assertion). Full suite 78/78, zero regressions. **[FL-20260916-2210-p3] [tenant-management] [security] `/workorder` tenant-submission gate now resolves the caller's real property/unit/tenant_id from their verified session, not from the client-supplied request body — closes the gap flagged at the end of today's 3-part access-control build (see `[FL-20260916-2145-w2]` below).** Per `TENANT_WO_SETTINGS_UI_AND_HARDENING_BUILD_BRIEF_v1.0` Part A: the Aug 20 2026 tenant-submission gate confirmed the *property* allowed tenant submission but never confirmed the calling tenant's own session actually belonged to the property/unit/tenant_id in the request body — a tenant with a valid session could submit a WO tagged to a different property or tenant_id than their own.

- Hoisted `callerSessionId` (the verified session's own record id) alongside the existing `callerRole`, set from `_session.id` in the same auth block.
- In the `/workorder` gate, for `callerRole === 'tenant'`: looks up the real `Tenants` row by `callerSessionId`, rejects with 403 if no active tenant matches, then **overwrites** `body.tenant_id`/`body.unit_id`/`body.property_id` from that real record (falling back to the unit's own `Property_ID` when the tenant record itself has none — mirrors `tenantByPin`'s own resolution) before `resolveTenantSubmitAccess` or `createWorkOrder` ever run. Chose overwrite over cross-check-and-403 (the brief's own "cleaner alternative") — removes the spoofing vector entirely rather than just detecting it, and is the smaller change.
- Scoped strictly to `callerRole === 'tenant'` — the admin secret and owner-session paths through `/workorder` are byte-for-byte unchanged.
- `tenant.html` needed no change — it already only ever sends the logged-in tenant's own `session.property_id`/`session.unit_id`/`session.tenant_id` (confirmed by reading `submitNewRequest` directly, not assumed).

`test/tenant-submit-request.test.mjs` extended with 8 new structural assertions (order-of-operations: the session lookup and all three overwrites happen before `resolveTenantSubmitAccess` is called; a stale/inactive session id 403s; the admin/owner path is untouched). `node --check` clean, full suite 76/76, zero regressions. Deployed, confirmed via `/version` (`2026-09-16.5`) and a fresh anonymous clone of `origin/main`.

**NOT verified live** — no live tenant PIN session was exercised from this sandbox (no Drive/Sheets credentials here). 🔴 Needs Brett's first live pass: log in to `tenant.html` as a real tenant, use browser dev tools to replay the `POST /workorder` request with a different `property_id`/`unit_id` in the body, confirm the created WO lands on the tenant's own real property/unit regardless of what was sent (overwritten, not rejected, since the fix removes the spoofing vector rather than just blocking it) — then confirm a normal, unmodified submission from the app still works exactly as before.

Human-gate: no QuickBooks/payment writes — Work_Orders/Tenants/Units reads only, one Work_Orders row created same as any normal tenant submission today.

Added a "+ SUBMIT A NEW REQUEST" button to tenant.html (shown only when the server-resolved `can_submit_wo` is true), a modal reusing the portal's own existing photo-upload plumbing, posting through the unmodified `/workorder` endpoint with `type:'tenant'`. **No worker.js changes** — the gate, the resolver, and the owner-auto-notify (`owner_job_received`, already scoped to `type==='tenant'` only) were all already correct. Set Goldszmidt's (Owner_ID 3) toggle ON via the existing `POST /owner/tenant-wo-toggle` endpoint, live, unscoped (applies to all 11 active properties). Phoenix (Owner_ID 1) and every other owner stay at the existing OFF default — no action needed there.

`test/tenant-submit-request.test.mjs` (7 assertions) — structural checks on the button's `can_submit_wo` gating, the submission always using the logged-in tenant's own session fields (never client-editable), and error surfacing on a server-side rejection. `node --check` clean on all 3 tenant.html script blocks, full suite 75/75 test files, zero regressions.

**Worth flagging, not fixed this round**: `POST /workorder`'s tenant gate (`callerRole === 'tenant'` → `resolveTenantSubmitAccess(env, body.property_id, body.unit_id)`) checks whether *that property* allows tenant submission, but doesn't cross-check that the calling tenant's session actually belongs to the `property_id`/`unit_id`/`tenant_id` in the request body — a tenant with a valid session token could theoretically submit a WO tagged to a different property/tenant than their own. This is pre-existing (part of the Aug 20 build, not introduced today) and matches the same self-reported-ID-checked-against-a-real-record pattern used elsewhere in this codebase (e.g. `ownerUpdateWO`'s `owner_id`) rather than a verified-session-identity check. Narrow risk while portal rollout is still limited to a couple of owners; worth hardening (check the session's own tenant `id`, not just the record it claims) before wider rollout.

**Still open, not part of this pass**: the owner-level `Managed_By`/"held" work-order concept (owner claims a job as their own, tenant sees it grayed out with the owner's own contact info) from the same conversation — `Managed_By` shipped on the Work_Orders/owner.html side (rule above/prior entry); the tenant-facing grayed-out display + `Owners.Held_Contact_Note` redirect text is a separate, not-yet-built piece. No admin (index.html) UI yet for managing the `Tenant_WO_Toggle` hierarchy day-to-day — set via direct API call this session; the existing `GET /tenant-wo-settings` endpoint (built Aug 20, never consumed by any page) is ready for exactly that if/when Brett wants a proper settings screen instead of asking per-owner via chat.

Human-gate: no QuickBooks/payment writes. Real customer-facing capability (tenants can now create real Work_Orders) — worth a live test with Goldszmidt before broader rollout.

**[FL-20260916-1950-m4] [twilio-sms] [vendor-payments] Vendor status/invoice nudges could stall past 'Complete' forever — satisfied-check now keys off WO status (Complete-or-later stops the status ask; a separately auto-started invoice ask persists until Invoiced-or-later) instead of a Vendor_Bills row; Cancelled/Declined still stop everything (worker.js processVendorNudges).** Brett flagged two live false-positive nudges directly: WO-1200 and WO-1201 both got a "checking in — any update on status?" text at 3:06pm ET today even though both were already `Invoiced` with a real, reviewed Vendor_Bills row on file (#90/#91). Root cause, confirmed against the live Sheet via `/message-queue`, `/vendor-requests`, `/workorders`, and `/vendor-bills` (not guessed): the old satisfied-check only recognized `wo.Status === 'Complete'` as the stopping condition for a status_update ask — once a WO moved past Complete to Invoiced, the check silently fell through and the daily nudge kept firing indefinitely regardless of billing.

Brett's new rule, applied directly: the status ask is satisfied at Complete or anything past it (`Complete`/`Pending Invoice`/`Invoiced`/`Paid`/`Closed`), independent of billing — once the WO is Complete the status question is answered. If it isn't invoiced yet at that point, the same sweep pass auto-starts a separate `invoice`-type request (skipped if one's already open for that WO+vendor) that persists specifically until the WO reaches Invoiced or later — a Vendor_Bills row existing is no longer treated as the same thing as the WO actually being invoiced, since WO-1200/1201 are exactly the case where those diverge. Cancelled/Declined already short-circuited both request types before either satisfied-check ran; unchanged.

Also per Brett: the status_update nudge text now coaches the vendor through the actual workflow ("remember to add the schedule for the job in your portal, and mark it Complete once the work's done — then you'll just need to submit your invoice") instead of a bare "any update on status?" ask.

`test/vendor-nudges.test.mjs` updated (17 assertions, was 12) — replaced the stale Vendor_Bills-row assertion with checks on the new status-set constants, the auto-started invoice chase and its dedupe guard, the Cancelled/Declined short-circuit, and the new coaching copy. `node --check` clean, full suite 65/65 passing, zero regressions.

**Not yet live-verified**: no live Sheets/Twilio-send credentials in the build sandbox for an actual end-to-end nudge cycle — the fix is grounded in real WO-1198/1199/1200/1201 data pulled from the live Worker this session, but the next scheduled sweep actually going quiet on WO-1200/1201's status ask (both are already Invoiced, so neither should even start an invoice-chase row) needs a live check.

**Also relevant, found but not yet re-confirmed this session**: the Sep 15 evening fix for the GitHub Actions sweep cadence (FL-20260915-1839-x7 — added a 5th Cloudflare Cron Trigger since the GH Actions `schedule:` trigger ran ~every 5h instead of every 15min) doesn't appear to have fully closed the gap yet, per today's real data: WO-1198/1199's 9:00am-due nudges actually fired at 11:33am (2h33m late), and WO-1200/1201's 12:16/12:20pm-due nudges fired at 3:06pm (~2h50m late) — all four pairs fired in near-simultaneous batches, consistent with an irregular sweep rather than a 15-minute cadence. Meaningfully better than the original ~5h gap, but still well short of 15 minutes. Worth Brett confirming the new Cloudflare Cron Trigger is actually registered and firing — no manual-dispatch/logs API is reachable from a session to check this directly, per that entry's own note.

Human-gate: no QuickBooks/payment writes — this only changes when an existing SMS nudge fires or stops, not any money-moving logic.

**[FL-20260916-1750-k3] [gh-broker] [infrastructure] [documentation-gap] GH Broker (the GitHub-access MCP connector, `brett332/gh-broker`) was built, debugged, and fixed today with ZERO documentation anywhere in this repo until now — closing that gap retroactively.** What actually happened, pieced together after the fact because the session that did the original build/fixes disconnected/was lost with no checkpoint saved (`context/SESSION_STATE.md` was stale at Aug 22, `CURRENT.md` and `BACKLOG.md` both had no mention of GH Broker at all as of this writing):

- **What GH Broker is**: a Cloudflare Worker (separate repo, `brett332/gh-broker`) that mints short-lived GitHub App installation tokens server-side and exposes `read_file`/`list_directory`/`commit_file`/`create_pull_request` as MCP tools — replacing the old git-clone-with-a-pasted-PAT approach for Cowork sessions. Two GitHub App installs behind one Worker (`Ridge-Co` org + `brett332` personal). Documented properly for the first time in `context/CREDENTIALS_MAP.md` v1.4 (Sep 16) — that file previously still told sessions to ask Brett to paste a PAT by default, which was already obsolete and against Brett's own standing rule.
- **Bug 1 (fixed by the lost session, evidenced only by its commit message): `read_file` decoded base64 content incorrectly for non-ASCII text.** Fixed with a proper `decodeBase64Utf8` (via `TextDecoder`). Commit `2bc1b4c` on `brett332/gh-broker`, message: "feat: add list_directory tool, fix UTF-8 decoding in read_file." No accompanying FEATURE_LOG/CURRENT.md entry was ever written for this.
- **Bug 2 (found and fixed this session): `commit_file` had the exact same class of bug on the WRITE side and was never touched by the lost session's fix.** It base64-encoded content with plain `btoa()`, which throws on any character outside Latin-1 — an em-dash, a checkmark, a curly quote, an arrow, an emoji, anything CJK. Since nearly all real documentation content (including this file's own style) uses those characters constantly, almost every real `commit_file` call was silently crashing the Worker mid-request, surfacing to the calling session only as "the connector's server isn't responding" — indistinguishable from a genuine outage. Root-caused by reading `brett332/gh-broker/src/index.ts` directly (not guessed), reproduced in isolation with Node before touching anything live (`btoa("em—dash")` throws `Invalid character`, confirmed), fixed by adding a matching `encodeBase64Utf8` and verified with a roundtrip test (ASCII/em-dash/curly-quotes/CJK/emoji) before shipping. Commit `e302e5d` on `brett332/gh-broker`. Live-tested against the actual deployed connector afterward with a string containing every one of those character classes — round-tripped byte-perfect.
- **A separate, unrelated mistake got made and fixed in the same session**: an early `commit_file` call accidentally sent a literal template-placeholder string instead of real file content, briefly leaving `context/CREDENTIALS_MAP.md` broken on `main` with garbage in it. Caught immediately via a read-back, and — once GH Broker's own write path turned out to be broken too — fixed by pushing directly via Brett's own classic PAT over git Basic-auth (his explicit in-session authorization, per the existing standing rule in `ridgeco-git-push-proxy-bug.md`), not by working around GH Broker quietly.
- **The actual root-cause finding worth remembering**: GH Broker being broken this session had nothing to do with credentials or runtime env vars (Brett had already separately identified and fixed a real missing-runtime-variable outage earlier the same day) — read and write mint tokens through the identical code path, and reads worked perfectly throughout. The two outages looked similar from the outside ("connector isn't responding") but were two unrelated root causes on two different code paths.

**Real gap this whole episode exposes**: `brett332/gh-broker` is a separate repo from `Ridge-Co/RidgeCo`, so nothing in this file's own doc-audit/nightly-check system (`scripts/doc_audit.py`, rule from Sep 15) ever sees commits to it. A tool this central to how every session gets GitHub access can go through a full build-and-two-bug-fix cycle with zero record anywhere Brett would think to look. Worth deciding whether `brett332/gh-broker` should get its own lightweight FEATURE_LOG, or whether changes to it should always also get an entry here (this entry is the first instance of the latter).

**NOT verified this session**: `list_directory` was confirmed to exist and be fully implemented server-side (read directly from `src/index.ts`) but was never actually invoked through a live MCP call this session — the session's own connector tool list was fixed before Brett's mid-conversation disconnect/reconnect, so it couldn't be exercised from here. Worth a real test from a fresh session.

Human-gate required: no QuickBooks/customer-money code touched. This is infrastructure Claude sessions use to reach GitHub, not a change to any Ridge Co customer/vendor-facing system.


**[FL-20260915-1903-s8] [twilio-sms] [welcome-messages] `welcomeSend` had the same quiet-hours-hold-treated-as-failure bug already found and fixed once today in `createVendorRequest`/`processVendorNudges` — a welcome message sent during quiet hours would falsely report "Send failed" and skip the `Welcome_Sent` write entirely (worker.js).** Found proactively while comprehensively testing the message system live (Brett: "get this whole thing live") — checked `welcomeSend` specifically because it shares the exact same `smsGatedSend` return shape as the code I'd already fixed earlier the same day, and the same mistake was there: `if (!r.sent) return json({error:...})` treated a quiet-hours hold (`r.sent:false, held_for_quiet_hours:true` — a SUCCESS, correctly queued) the same as a genuine gate-block or send failure. Two real consequences: (1) the API caller got a false "Send failed" 502 for a message that was actually working correctly; (2) because the function returned before reaching the `Welcome_Sent` write, a tenant/vendor with a legitimately queued welcome message would still show as "not yet welcomed" in the Hub's own filter (rule 169).

Fixed with the same `!r.sent && !r.held_for_quiet_hours` guard already established as the pattern. Response now reports `held_for_quiet_hours` explicitly and a clearer `sent_to` string for that case. No frontend changes needed — both `confirmSendWelcome` and `executeBulkSendWelcome` (index.html) already just check `res.success`, which now correctly reports `true` for a quiet-hours-held send.

`test/welcome-send.test.mjs` updated (13 assertions, was 11) — the existing structural test that checked for the OLD exact guard string was caught and fixed too (it would have kept "passing" against the wrong pattern otherwise), plus 2 new regression assertions confirming the old sent-only guard is gone, not left alongside the fix. `node --check` clean, full suite green.

**Not yet live-verified specifically during quiet hours** — fixed proactively via code inspection, not caught by an actual quiet-hours live test this time (tested during business hours, when the bug doesn't trigger). Worth a real check next time a welcome send happens after 7pm ET.

**[FL-20260915-1843-g2] [twilio-sms] [owner-messages] Owner Scheduled notification was permanently unreachable — Status is never literally set to 'Scheduled' anywhere in this codebase; moved the trigger from updateStatus to scheduleWO, the code path that actually handles scheduling (worker.js).** Found live-testing during Brett's "get this whole thing live" comprehensive pass: scheduled WO-1196 for today via the real `/schedule` endpoint and confirmed via `/message-queue` that ZERO messages were queued — not a gate block, not a delivery failure, nothing attempted at all. Traced it to the actual code: `scheduleWO` only ever touches `Scheduled_Date`/`Scheduled_Window`, never `Status` (except the "within 1 hour" case, which sets `In Progress`) — confirmed via a direct grep that `Status` is never assigned the literal string `'Scheduled'` anywhere in worker.js, index.html, or vendor.html. The owner-scheduled notification (both the pre-rebuild version and rule 168's rebuild of it) was gated on `body.status === 'Scheduled'` inside `updateStatus` — a condition that could never become true from any real user action in the Hub.

Fixed by moving the trigger to `scheduleWO` itself, alongside the tenant-scheduled notification that already lives there and already works correctly (tenant/owner variables restructured to be fetched once, outside the `notify_tenant` conditional, since an owner's own notify preference isn't tied to whether the tenant-notify toggle happens to be on for this particular call). Removed `Scheduled` from `updateStatus`'s `OWNER_STATUS_EVENTS` map entirely rather than leaving it as harmless dead code — leaving it would risk a future double-send if any code path ever did start posting `status:'Scheduled'`.

New `test/owner-scheduled-fix.test.mjs` (8 assertions) — includes a direct regression guard on the bug's own premise (`Status` never literally `'Scheduled'` anywhere in the file), confirms the send sits in `scheduleWO` not nested inside the `notify_tenant` block, and confirms `OWNER_STATUS_EVENTS` no longer references `Scheduled` at all. `node --check` clean, full suite green.

**Not yet live-verified after this specific fix** — found and fixed mid-comprehensive-test; next step is re-running the same `/schedule` call on WO-1196 and confirming an `owner_job_scheduled` row actually appears in `/message-queue` with real Twilio delivery.

**[FL-20260915-1839-x7] [twilio-sms] [vendor-payments] [cron-sweep] Real root cause found for "vendor nudges didn't fire": GitHub Actions' `schedule:` trigger ran roughly every 5 hours instead of every 15 minutes — added a 5th Cloudflare Cron Trigger calling `cronSweep(env)` directly as a reliable backup (worker.js `scheduled()`, wrangler.toml).** Brett: "I should have gotten nudges. I did not... run a comprehensive test so we can get this whole thing live." Investigated live, not guessed: checked `cron-sweep.yml`'s actual run history — only 3 real scheduled fires in ~15 hours (16:37, 11:46, 06:06 UTC), roughly every 5 hours against a `*/15 * * * *` schedule. This is a documented GitHub Actions limitation, not a bug in this repo's own code — `schedule:` triggers are best-effort and can be significantly delayed on lower-activity/public repos, with no SLA. Confirmed the underlying send pipeline itself was NOT broken: the 3 messages that had been quiet-hours-held since the earlier live-verification session (an `owner_job_on_hold`, a `vendor_request_photos`, a `vendor_request_invoice`) all show `status: "delivered"` on Twilio's own side once the delayed sweep finally ran at 16:37 UTC — real delivery confirmed via `/twilio/message-status`, not just a Message_Queue "sent" flag (same rigor as the rule 159a precedent, since "the Worker says sent" and "the phone actually got it" have been different things before in this codebase).

Fix: rather than trying to make GitHub Actions run more reliably (not something this codebase controls), added the sweep as a 5th Cloudflare Cron Trigger — the last available slot on the paid plan (4 were already used: daily digest, 2x optimizer review, weekly AR report). Calls `cronSweep(env)` directly inside `scheduled()`, no HTTP round-trip and no `CRON_SWEEP_TOKEN` needed for this path. The GitHub Actions workflow stays in place as a redundant second path — both call the same idempotent function (only acts on rows that are actually due), so both firing is harmless, just occasionally repeats a `checked: 0` no-op.

`node --check` clean, full suite green (no test logic changed, only the cron wiring). **Deployed, not yet confirmed actually firing on the new Cloudflare schedule** — Cloudflare Cron Triggers don't have a manual-dispatch API reachable from this session; confirmation is "check back in ~15-30 minutes for a new sweep having run" rather than a trigger-and-verify like the GitHub Actions path allowed.

## ID/tag convention (Sep 15, 2026) — read this before adding a new entry
Entries before this note keep their old `**NNN.**` sequential numbers — never renumbered, never
touched, for real. **Every new entry from here forward gets an ID instead of the next number:**

    **[FL-YYYYMMDD-HHMM-xx] [tag-one] [tag-two] Title of what happened (files/functions touched).**

- **`YYYYMMDD-HHMM`** — the date and time (24h, your local time is fine, just be consistent) you
  write the entry. Year-first so entries sort correctly as plain text.
- **`xx`** — two random base-36 characters (`0-9a-z`), picked fresh for this entry. Not
  meaningful on their own — they exist purely so two entries written in the same minute
  (two concurrent sessions is the real case this guards against) can't collide. To pick them:
  `echo $((RANDOM % 36)) $((RANDOM % 36))` in bash, or just type two characters that feel
  arbitrary — collision odds at this scale (a handful of entries per minute, worst case) are
  low enough that "arbitrary" is fine; this isn't cryptography.
- **Why not a commit SHA in the ID itself**: the entry is usually written as part of the same
  commit as the code it describes, so that commit's own SHA doesn't exist yet at write time —
  using it would mean either a two-commit dance (code, then docs) or an `--amend` that changes
  the SHA out from under the reference. Sidestepped entirely: the ID doesn't need the SHA to be
  unique (the timestamp+random already guarantees that), and tracing an ID to its real commit
  works by just **including the exact ID string in the git commit message** — `git log --grep
  "FL-20260915-1430-k7"` finds it. No self-reference problem, no two-commit requirement.
- **Tags** — `[bracketed-lowercase-hyphenated]`, as many as genuinely apply (usually 1-3): the
  subsystem/area, not the specific fix. `[twilio-sms]`, `[vendor-payments]`,
  `[receipt-reconciler]`, `[owner-messages]`. Multiple entries sharing a tag is the whole point
  — `grep '\[twilio-sms\]' FEATURE_LOG.md` should surface every entry that's ever touched that
  area, related or not. This is what actually solves "did session A and session B step on the
  same code for unrelated reasons" — not by preventing it (you can't, two sessions running at
  once will sometimes land on the same file), but by making it trivially searchable after the
  fact instead of requiring a human to remember or a Claude to guess.
- Never check what's "already taken" before writing an ID — there's nothing to collide with by
  construction. If two sessions land in the same file at the same time, they get two different
  IDs and that's the end of it; no lookup, no coordination needed.

Example: `**[FL-20260915-1430-k7] [twilio-sms] [owner-messages] Owner On-Hold now requires a
reason before the status write happens (worker.js updateStatus).**`

**[FL-20260915-1644-cz] [doc-infrastructure] [feature-log] [backlog] Documentation-completeness infrastructure: the ID/tag convention itself, a `ridgeco-validate` documentation gate, two staleness tripwires, and a nightly audit (Sep 15 2026, context/FEATURE_LOG.md + context/BACKLOG.md + scripts/doc_audit.py + .github/workflows/doc-audit.yml + context/DOC_AUDIT_LOG.md; skill edits to ridgeco-validate/brett-context, persistence to future sessions unconfirmed).** Flagged by this exact system's own first real run — a genuinely fitting way to notice the gap. Brett, after the Sep 14 documentation-gap incident: capture more as it happens, plus a scheduled audit to catch what still slips through, without requiring a manual save from every session that touched something that day.
