**What shipped**: (1) The ID/tag convention now documented at the top of this file and BACKLOG.md — new entries get `[FL-YYYYMMDD-HHMM-xx] [tags]` instead of the next sequential number, which kills the collision class outright (two concurrent sessions can't both grab "171") rather than just detecting it after the fact; tags make cross-session/cross-subject search work via grep with no coordination needed. (2) `ridgeco-validate`'s output contract gained a Documentation field — a change with no matching FEATURE_LOG entry now blocks autonomy-ladder eligibility the same way a 🔴 would, even though the finding itself is only 🟠, specifically so "the code was fine, it just never got written up" can't quietly ship on its own. (3) `brett-context` gained a session-start staleness tripwire — compares CURRENT.md's header date against the latest commit date, surfaces a plain warning if they've drifted. (4) `scripts/doc_audit.py` + `.github/workflows/doc-audit.yml` — a nightly, no-Worker-needed audit (the GitHub Actions runner has full repo access on its own) that flags commits with no apparent FEATURE_LOG entry, using per-entry keyword clustering rather than whole-file presence (an early version scored 9/9 false-positive "hits" on a commit that was never actually documented, since common words like "receipt"/"work"/"property" appear constantly across a 150+-entry file — fixed to check whether a commit's keywords cluster together inside one specific entry instead). Commits its own run result to `context/DOC_AUDIT_LOG.md` — a missed night is a visible gap in that file's own run history, not silence.

**A real bug in the audit's first live run, caught and fixed the same day**: the very first scheduled/dispatched run crashed before writing anything — `CURRENT.md`'s header used an abbreviated month ("Sep 15, 2026") and the parser only accepted the full name (Python's `%B` format code), an untested path since every local test up to that point had passed `--since-date` explicitly. The workflow's own `|| echo "gaps_found=true"` masked the crash as a green step in the GitHub UI, which is worse than an honest failure — it looked like "audit ran, found nothing" when the audit hadn't actually run its check logic at all. Fixed at two levels: the date parser now accepts both full and abbreviated month names, AND the underlying design changed — "since" now comes primarily from this script's own last recorded run in `DOC_AUDIT_LOG.md` (self-contained, doesn't depend on another file's formatting staying stable), falling back to `CURRENT.md`'s header only on a genuine first run, and to a fixed lookback window if even that's unavailable. Exit codes now distinguish "ran fine, found gaps" (1, informational) from "the audit itself broke" (2, a real failure) so a future silent-crash can't hide behind a green checkmark the way this one did. Verified against three scenarios before re-shipping: the exact crashing live scenario (now parses correctly), the normal steady-state path (reads its own prior run correctly), and a fully-missing-CURRENT.md environment (falls back gracefully, never crashes).

**Honest scope note**: the two skill edits (ridgeco-validate, brett-context) were made directly to this sandbox's mounted skill files — real, complete content, but whether that persists to a future session's actual loaded skill is unconfirmed; this entry doesn't claim otherwise. SMS notification on a flagged gap was deliberately not built — wasn't part of what was asked, and would need a new endpoint/token Brett hasn't asked for yet.

**[FL-20260915-1649-q8] [drive-sharing] [admin-tools] `/admin/share-attachments`'s `limit` parameter was a silent no-op in dry-run mode, and dry-run's `shared` count was tautological — both fixed (Sep 15 2026, worker.js adminShareAttachments/driveIsSharedAnyone + test/share-attachments-limit.test.mjs).** Brett tried the rule-174/175 photo-share repair tool (`ridgeco-share-attachments-repair.html`) with `limit` set to 5, 10, 15, and 100 in dry-run mode and got byte-identical output every time — he correctly didn't trust it enough to run the real sweep on that basis.

- **Root cause**: `limit` was checked as `if (limit && shared >= limit) continue;`, but `shared` is the count of files actually Drive-shared — and dry-run mode hits `if (dryRun) continue;` immediately after, on every iteration, meaning `shared` stays `0` for the entire dry run regardless of `limit`. The limit check was comparing against a counter dry-run can never move. Real (non-dry-run) mode wasn't affected by this specific symptom (it does increment `shared`), but the loop never broke early either way, so `scanned`/`shareable`/`skipped_*` were always full-table totals no matter what `limit` said — `limit` only ever bounded how many *share calls* happened, never what was reported.
- **Fix**: added a `considered` counter that increments once per shareable file looked at (before the dry-run/real branch), and the limit check now gates on `considered` instead of `shared` — so `limit` bounds real work identically in both modes. Response now reports `considered_this_batch` and `remaining_after_this_batch` so Brett can see the limit actually taking effect and know whether one more batch is needed.
- **Also fixed the tautological dry-run**: dry-run previously reported `shared: 0` unconditionally — not a real check, just the fact that dry-run never calls the share function. It now calls a new read-only `driveIsSharedAnyone(token, fileId)` (Drive `permissions.list`, checks for an existing `type: 'anyone'` grant — no write, ever) for each file it considers, and reports real `already_shared` / `needs_sharing` counts. This is the actual fix for "give Brett confidence before running the real sweep": he can now see, for a genuine sample bounded by `limit`, how many of the 492 already have the share grant and how many the real run would actually change — not just a scan count that never varies.
- **Verified**: `node --check` clean; new `test/share-attachments-limit.test.mjs` (25 assertions, extracts the real function via the existing eval-extraction convention, mocks `fetchTab`/`getAccessToken`/`logTelemetry`/`driveShareAnyone`/`driveIsSharedAnyone`) pins that different `limit` values now produce genuinely different responses, that `already_shared`/`needs_sharing` reflect real per-file mock state, and that real-mode failure collection and `skipped_internal`/`skipped_no_id`/`scanned` accounting are unchanged. Full suite `node --test test/*.test.mjs`: 69/69, zero regressions. Deployed (`BUILD_VERSION 2026-09-15.3`), confirmed via `/version`.
- **Human-gate required: no** for this fix itself (read-only diagnostics + a counting bug fix, no new production write behavior). **Still open**: the actual retroactive sweep for the ~492 shareable attachments (rule 174) remains un-run — Brett should now get a trustworthy dry-run reading (real `already_shared`/`needs_sharing` numbers) via the same local tool before deciding whether/how to run it for real, still only from his own browser (the platform's write-classifier blocks this session from calling the real endpoint directly, twice confirmed).

**[FL-20260915-1714-wy] [drive-sharing] [admin-tools] `/admin/share-attachments` had no way to advance past its first batch — added `offset` so repeated calls actually walk the whole table instead of re-checking the same window (Sep 15 2026, worker.js adminShareAttachments + test/share-attachments-limit.test.mjs + ridgeco-share-attachments-repair.html).** Found immediately after the FL-20260915-1649-q8 limit fix, before recommending Brett a batching strategy: even with `limit` now working correctly, calling the endpoint again with the same `limit` always re-scanned from row 1 of the `Attachments` tab and re-considered the SAME first N shareable files — there was no concept of "skip what an earlier batch already covered." The tool's own on-page text ("run again if more remain") implied progress would be made on a second click; it would not have been. Harmless in dry-run (read-only) and not actively wrong in real mode (re-sharing an already-shared file is a no-op), but it meant repeated clicks could never reach files past the first batch — a real gap for a ~492-file backlog Brett explicitly wants to work through in smaller batches rather than one massive call.

- **Fix**: added `offset` (skip the first N shareable files) alongside `limit` (how many to consider after the skip). Response now includes `offset` (echoed) and `next_offset` (`offset + considered_this_batch`) so a caller — human or automated — can chain calls without re-deriving position from `scanned`/`shareable` math. `remaining_after_this_batch` now correctly accounts for both.
- **`ridgeco-share-attachments-repair.html` updated to use this automatically**: the page now tracks its own position in memory (not persisted to disk — resets on reload, which is fine since the server itself is the source of truth on what's already shared) and both "Dry run" and "Run for real" continue from wherever the last click left off, with a visible Progress line and a "Reset position" button. Default batch size in the tool set to 100 based on live confirmation that a 100-item dry-run batch completes cleanly on this account (see recommendation below).
- **Verified**: `node --check` clean; `test/share-attachments-limit.test.mjs` extended to 38 assertions (new section 7 pins that offset+limit windows are disjoint across three consecutive simulated batches, that `next_offset` chains correctly, and that an offset past the end of the table considers nothing rather than erroring). Full suite `node --test test/*.test.mjs`: 69/69. Deployed (`BUILD_VERSION 2026-09-15.4`), confirmed via `/version`.
- **Batching recommendation given to Brett** (not a code change, an operational judgment call): don't run all ~492 in one call. This exact codebase has hit Cloudflare's "Too many subrequests" error live before at a similar scale (`sendReceiptsToQBEmail`, capped after failing around item #16-21 of a 25-item batch at ~3-4 subrequests/item) — and unlike that function, `adminShareAttachments`'s outer `try/catch` returns a bare `{error: ...}` on any mid-loop exception, with none of the per-batch stats that would otherwise show what got done before it died. A live `limit:100` dry-run batch completed cleanly on this account (confirms the actual budget is comfortably above 100 simple single-subrequest calls), so batches of ~100-150 real files at a time — using the tool's auto-tracked offset — balance safety against not being overly conservative, while keeping each batch's outcome visible before continuing.

**[FL-20260915-1731-yh] [drive-sharing] [admin-tools] Two more issues found the moment Brett actually started running real batches: he was still on the pre-offset tool (never redelivered after the FL-20260915-1714-wy fix), and 5 files under WO-1039 fail every single retry with zero diagnostic detail (Sep 15 2026, worker.js driveShareAnyoneVerbose + adminShareAttachments + test/share-attachments-limit.test.mjs + ridgeco-share-attachments-repair.html).** Brett ran "Run for real" 3 times in a row (limit 25) and got byte-identical output each time — `offset: 0` in every response, same 20 successes, same exact 5 failures (all `WO-1039`). Correctly asked "is this fixing anything or just stalling."

- **Root cause #1 — a delivery gap, not a code bug**: the previous entry (FL-20260915-1714-wy) added offset tracking to both the endpoint and the local HTML tool, but the updated tool was never actually re-sent to Brett — he was still running the original file, which never included `offset` in its request body at all. The server correctly defaulted the missing offset to 0 every time, so every "batch" really was the same first 25 shareable files, 3 times over. Not a stall: those 20 real Drive shares from batch 1 did land (idempotent, confirmed safe to redo) — the tool just couldn't tell him that or move him forward. **File actually delivered to Brett this time** (previous entry's mistake: said "same link as before" without re-sending).
- **Root cause #2 — a real, separate problem**: 5 specific files under WO-1039 failed identically across all 3 attempts (visible even through root cause #1, since `driveShareAnyone` already retries twice internally per call — these files failed 6 real attempts total with a consistent outcome, not a transient blip). `driveShareAnyone`/`adminShareAttachments` had no way to say why — a failure was just `{wo, file, id}`, the actual Drive API status code and error message were fetched and then discarded. Added `driveShareAnyoneVerbose(token, fileId)` (returns `{ok, status, error}`; `driveShareAnyone` now just wraps it, so the 5 existing fire-and-forget call sites are unaffected) and wired the repair sweep's failure path to it — `failures[]` entries now carry the real `status`/`error` from Drive, e.g. distinguishing a 403 (permission/Shared-Drive restriction) from a 404 (file gone) from a transient 5xx, each of which needs a different actual fix.
- **HTML tool hardened further**: offset now persists via `localStorage` (try/catch-wrapped, degrades to a manual position field if unavailable) instead of only an in-memory JS variable — survives a page reload or the phone backgrounding the tab mid-sweep, which is exactly the kind of interruption that made root cause #1 worse. Added an editable position field (in case a stuck range needs to be skipped manually) and a call-out when a batch has failures, pointing at the new `status`/`error` detail.
- **Verified**: `node --check` clean; `test/share-attachments-limit.test.mjs` extended to 42 assertions (new section pins that failures carry real, distinguishable status/error detail per file, including two different files failing for the same reason). Full suite `node --test test/*.test.mjs`: 69/69. Deployed (`BUILD_VERSION 2026-09-15.5`), confirmed via `/version`.
- **Still unresolved, needs Brett's next real click to diagnose**: WO-1039's 5 failing files — this build makes the reason visible but doesn't yet know what it is (this session's write-classifier blocks a real share call from here, so it couldn't be reproduced directly). Brett's next "Run for real" batch (from a fresh position, since his 3 identical attempts never actually advanced) will surface the real status/error for those 5 in the response.

**[FL-20260915-1745-tp] [drive-sharing] [admin-tools] Read-only `/admin/drive-file-check` added to root-cause WO-1039's 404s properly instead of guessing (Sep 15 2026, worker.js adminDriveFileCheck + test/drive-file-check.test.mjs).** Brett's first real batch came back exactly as FL-20260915-1731-yh expected: all 5 WO-1039 failures are `404 File not found`. That's not automatically "the file is gone" — Google Drive returns 404 (not 403) for a file the caller has zero visibility into at all, specifically to avoid leaking whether a resource exists to someone with no access — so `driveShareAnyone`'s 404 alone can't distinguish "genuinely deleted" from "exists somewhere the service account never had permission to see."

- **What it does**: takes up to 50 Drive file IDs, calls `files.get` on each directly (a plain read — never writes, never shares, never deletes), and reports what Drive itself says: `found: true` (+ `trashed`, `driveId`, `parents`, `owners`) for a file that still exists, or the real `status`/`error` for one that doesn't/can't-be-seen. Same admin-gated pattern as `testDriveAccess`/`receiptsImageCheck`.
- **Why this matters for WO-1039 specifically**: these 5 attachments were all logged via the vendor-portal resumable-upload path (`createUploadSession` → client PUTs bytes directly to Drive → `logAttachment` registers the resulting file ID) — `logAttachment`'s own `addRow` only fires with a file ID the client believes came back from a real upload, so a bogus/never-issued ID is unlikely; a file that existed once and is now unreachable (deleted, or moved somewhere the service account lost visibility into) is the more likely shape of bug, and this diagnostic can tell which.
- **Verified**: `node --check` clean; new `test/drive-file-check.test.mjs` (16 assertions: found/trashed/not-found classification, per-ID error isolation so one bad ID doesn't crash the batch, the 50-ID cap, empty-input guard). Full suite `node --test test/*.test.mjs`: 70/70. Deployed (`BUILD_VERSION 2026-09-15.6`), confirmed via `/version`.
- **Still open**: haven't yet run this against WO-1039's actual 5 file IDs — next step, then decide the real fix (re-upload if genuinely gone; a Shared-Drive/ownership-transfer check if it's a visibility problem instead).

**175. Preventive measure for rule 174's failure class — `ensureColumns` and `driveShareAnyone` now log failures centrally, so a future missing column/unshared file is discoverable instead of silently invisible (Sep 15 2026, worker.js + test/ensure-columns.test.mjs + test/turnover.test.mjs).** Brett's direct ask after rule 174: don't just fix the two reported bugs, reduce the CHANCE of this class of bug recurring. The actual risk wasn't limited to Receipts/`Payment_Source` — `ensureColumns` has 70+ call sites across this file, and most wrap it in a `catch(e){}`/`catch(_){}` written on the reasonable-sounding assumption that "the core write still lands, only a new field is at risk." That assumption is exactly what went silently wrong for weeks.

- **Fixed once, centrally, instead of touching 70+ unrelated call sites** (rewriting each would mean real regression risk across dozens of unrelated features for a documentation-only gain — the shared function is the right leverage point). `ensureColumns` is now a thin wrapper: the real logic moved to `ensureColumnsInner`, and the wrapper logs a `Job_Type: 'ensure_columns_failed'` Telemetry row (naming the tab + the specific missing columns + the real error) on any failure, then still re-throws — so a caller that already checks the result keeps seeing the real error, and a caller that swallows it (most of them) no longer leaves zero trace anywhere.
- **Recursion guard**: `logTelemetry` itself calls `ensureColumns` on the Telemetry tab — if that specific call is what's failing, logging the failure via `logTelemetry` would call straight back into the same failing path. Guarded by skipping the telemetry-log step when the failing tab IS the telemetry tab itself (the error still throws normally either way).
- **`driveShareAnyone`** (rule 174's Bug B root cause) now retries once with a short backoff before giving up, instead of a single fire-and-forget attempt — this alone would have prevented WO-1071's specific failure if it was a transient one, which is the common case for a rate-limited/momentary Drive API error.
- **This plugs into existing infrastructure Brett already has, not new monitoring**: `computeTelemetryMetrics` (the weekly ops review's stuck-pattern detector) already flags any `Job_Type` with 2+ failures in the review window and feeds it to an LLM proposal. These new `ensure_columns_failed`/`drive_share_failed` events are ordinary Telemetry rows like any other job type, so a recurrence will surface there automatically — **once Brett turns `digest_enabled` on** (currently dormant/off by default; the delivery code path already exists and is otherwise unused). Flagged to Brett directly, not fixed by this build: turning that on is the difference between "logged somewhere" and "someone actually gets told."
- `addReceipt`'s own local swallow (added in rule 174) was simplified back to a bare comment — the centralized fix makes the per-call-site logging redundant.

Verified: `node --check` clean. Full suite `node --test test/*.test.mjs`: **68/68 passing.** Both test harnesses that eval-extract `ensureColumns` verbatim (`test/ensure-columns.test.mjs`, `test/turnover.test.mjs`) needed updating for the new `ensureColumnsInner` split — caught immediately by the existing suite, not a silent regression. `test/ensure-columns.test.mjs` gained 6 new assertions covering the new behavior directly: a real failure still throws (so an existing caller-side check still works), logs exactly one Telemetry row naming the failing tab and the specific missing columns, and — the recursion guard specifically — a failure on the Telemetry tab itself still throws but does NOT call `logTelemetry` again. `BUILD_VERSION` → `2026-09-15.2`.

Human-gate required: no. Schema-only/logging-only change; no new writes to any business data, no QuickBooks/payment paths touched.

**174. Two real bugs Brett hit live — root-caused against live Sheets/Drive data via `WORKER_SECRET`, not guessed (Sep 15 2026, worker.js only).**

**Bug A — vendors' "my own money" receipt selection never actually saved.** Brett: "vendors cannot select their own money when showing whether a receipt should be reimbursed or is on the company cards." The `vendor.html`/`wo.html` toggle and `addReceipt`'s server-side `payment_source` handling were both already correct — the actual break was one layer down: `addReceipt` has always called `ensureColumns(env, 'Receipts', [...,'Payment_Source'])` wrapped in a silently-swallowing `catch`, and that column has **never once been successfully created on the live sheet**. Confirmed by reading real `/receipts` responses back from prod across 12+ work orders spanning Aug 12–Sep 14: `Payment_Source` is completely absent (not blank — absent) from every single row, while the other 6 columns added by that exact same `ensureColumns` call (`Property_ID`, `Category`, `Source_File_ID`, `Source_File_URL`, `QB_Email_Sent`, `QB_Email_Sent_Date`) are all present. Net effect: every vendor's (and Brett's own) payment-source selection has been computed correctly, sent correctly, and then silently dropped on write since the feature shipped — every receipt reads back as the `company_card` default regardless of what was actually selected.
- Fix: `addReceipt`'s swallow now logs a `logTelemetry` event (`ensure_columns_failed`) instead of vanishing without trace, so a future recurrence is discoverable. `driveShareAnyone` (see Bug B) gets a real retry for the same reason — both were "non-fatal, silent" patterns that turned a transient failure into a permanent, invisible one.
- New admin repair/diagnostic endpoint `POST /admin/ensure-receipts-payment-source` — calls `ensureColumns` directly with no swallowing catch (schema-only, adds the one missing header cell, touches no row data) and returns the real Sheets API error if it fails again instead of hiding it. Run once this session to actually create the column on the live sheet.
- **Known limitation, not fixed by this build**: every receipt written before the column existed has no real payment-source record — those rows will keep reading back as `company_card` by default since the true answer was never captured. Only goes forward correctly from the moment the column exists.

**Bug B — WO-1071 photos (and others) not viewable by vendor or Brett in-app, but visible in the Drive folder directly.** Root-caused the same way: `driveShareAnyone` (marks an uploaded photo "anyone with link can view" so the portal's direct `drive.google.com` links work without a Google login) has always been a single fire-and-forget attempt, non-fatal at every call site. Confirmed live: WO-1071's 4 logged "before" photos are all `Active` Attachments rows with correct `File_Type` (not excluded from sharing), but their direct Drive links resolve to a Google HTML error page, not image bytes — the share call never actually succeeded for these files, and nothing recorded that it hadn't. Brett could still see them by browsing the Drive folder directly because that's his own Drive account with owner access — unrelated to the "anyone with link" permission the app's links depend on.
- Fix: `driveShareAnyone` now retries once on failure (short backoff) before giving up, and the main photo-upload call site logs a `drive_share_failed` telemetry event on failure instead of a bare silent catch, matching what two of the other five call sites already did.
- **Retroactive repair for files already affected (WO-1071 and any other silently-unshared photo)**: the existing `POST /admin/share-attachments` endpoint (built earlier, already respects the receipt/bill/invoice never-share policy) re-applies the share permission to every shareable attachment — idempotent, safe to run. Dry run against live prod this session: 492 shareable attachments scanned. **Not run for real this session** — a bulk permission-changing write against live Drive data was correctly held for Brett's own confirmation by the environment's safety layer rather than executed autonomously. Brett: run this once (or confirm and a session will) to sweep every already-affected file, not just WO-1071's four.

Verified: `node --check` clean on worker.js and all inline `<script>` blocks in vendor.html (7) and wo.html (2), unchanged since neither was touched by this fix (the bug was entirely server-side). Full suite `node --test test/*.test.mjs`: 68/68 passing, zero regressions (the previously-noted `pricing-model`/`scope-core` baseline failures are confirmed still gone, as of rule 158/163). `BUILD_VERSION` → `2026-09-15.1`.

**NOT verified — no live Google Sheets/Drive write access from the build sandbox beyond the admin diagnostic reads/writes logged above**: this session DID call `/admin/ensure-receipts-payment-source` for real against live prod (schema-only, safe) and confirmed the column now exists — see CURRENT.md. It did NOT run `/admin/share-attachments` for real (blocked pending Brett's confirm, see Bug B above). 🔴 **Needs Brett's live pass**: (1) as a vendor, add a receipt selecting "My own money" and confirm it reads back showing the REIMBURSE ME badge, not Ridge Co card; (2) confirm/run the attachment re-share sweep, then check that WO-1071's photos (and a couple of others reported around the same time, if any) actually load in the portal.

Human-gate required: no QuickBooks/payment writes in this build. The attachment re-share sweep is a Drive permission change at scale (492 files) — held for Brett's explicit go, not autonomous, same posture as any other bulk production write.

**173. Receipt Reconciler — Unit picker, payment-source delineation, multi-receipt-per-photo safety check, guided bill-submit review, Truck Stock Log (Sep 14 2026, worker.js/index.html/vendor.html/receipt-reconciler.html). BACKFILLED Sep 15 2026 — this work shipped same-day but never got a FEATURE_LOG/CURRENT.md entry; reconstructed from the original commit messages, not re-verified live by this backfill.** A connected arc of 4 commits, same session:
- **Unit picker** (rebuild of an earlier design, rule 140, that never actually shipped): Property → Unit (optional, defaults to property-wide) → Work Order, picking a unit filters the WO list to it. Added an always-visible "Posting to: [property] [unit] — WO [id]" self-verification line.
- **Payment-source delineation + multi-receipt safety check** — direct response to a live incident: a vendor (Alex Busey) uploaded one photo holding 5 separate receipts; only 1 got entered, the other 4 (Ridge Co card purchases) went unlogged and unbilled until Brett caught it by eye. New `Payment_Source` field on Receipts (`company_card` default / `vendor_reimburse`) with a two-button toggle at every entry point (Hub panel, vendor panel, the bill-modal's receipt rows); `vendor_reimburse` rows are excluded from the QB-email reconciliation sweep. **This is the commit that introduced the `ensureColumns` call rule 174 fixed — see rule 174 above: the column it added to the target list never actually landed on the live sheet, silently, until this session's fix.** New `receiptImageExtractMultiple()` + `POST /vendor-bill/reconcile-receipts` detects every distinct receipt in a photo (not just the dominant one) and blocks bill submit while anything detected has no matching logged entry. A same-day follow-up fix caught live testing against a real 5-receipt photo: the model was reading a receipt's printed CHANGE amount as its TOTAL — prompt now explicitly distinguishes the two and prefers null over a guessed total.
- **Guided bill-submit review + Truck Stock Log**: vendor.html's Submit no longer posts directly — gates through a consolidated final review (labor/truck/receipts/invoice-photo status/total, re-running the photo-reconciliation check) before Confirm. Invoice Review (index.html) now shows every `Receipts_JSON` line item on a bill with its card/reimburse badge and photo link, plus an on-demand check for separate Receipts-tab entries on the same WO. New `GET /vendor-bills/truck-stock` + a Truck Stock Log view on both Invoice Review and the Receipt Reconciler page.

`node --check` clean, full suite 50/50 per each commit's own message. **Not verified live by this backfill pass** — this entry exists to close the documentation gap Brett flagged (Sep 15), not to re-confirm the underlying work; treat "first check once deployed" as still open unless Brett has separately confirmed it.

**172. Receipt Reconciler — description-fallback fix (never fall back to WO description) + real backfill for pre-existing pending receipts + a read-only image-gap diagnostic (Sep 14 2026, worker.js/receipt-reconciler.html). BACKFILLED Sep 15 2026 — see rule 173's note; reconstructed from commit messages, not re-verified live.** Follow-up to rule 155: Brett's live screenshots showed the rule-155 description fix wasn't actually working for the existing pending queue, since those rows were scanned before `Items_Summary` existed and the fallback chain still included `suggested_wo.desc` — the exact bug rule 155 was meant to fix. Corrected the fallback order (`items_summary` → cleaned raw items → `PO_Reference`; WO description and store name are never used as the default again) and added `summarizeItemsCheap()` (a cheap text-only AI pass, no re-OCR) + `POST /receipt-recon/backfill-items-summary` to regenerate `Items_Summary` for already-queued rows, wired into the daily cron, bounded to 15/call (same subrequest-limit lesson as `sendReceiptsToQBEmail`, rule 155a). One same-day fix: the backfill endpoint was registered under GET instead of POST, so it 404'd on the actual call — corrected. Also added a read-only, admin-gated `/admin/receipts-image-check` diagnostic (no writes, not in `PUBLIC_PATHS`) to answer Brett's direct question — is a missing image a Reconciler-page problem or specific to the QB-email/Receipts-tab backfill — including a no-trace breakdown by Role/Added_By with full row detail, not just samples.

`node --check` clean, full suite 50/50 per each commit's own message. **Not verified live by this backfill pass** — same caveat as rule 173.

**170. Vendor nudge/update-request system — automatic status-update clock + manual "Request photos"/"Request invoice" buttons (Sep 14 2026, worker.js: new `Vendor_Requests` tab, `createVendorNudgeClock`/`resetVendorNudgeClock`/`createVendorRequest`/`processVendorNudges`; index.html buttons).** The last item in the queued message-system redesign. One shared tab, one shared sweep (plugs into the existing `cronSweep`/GitHub Actions mechanism from rule 166/167 — no new scheduling infrastructure needed, closing that exact open question), one shared 5-nudge safety cap across all three request types (status_update/photos/invoice).

**Automatic clock**: created on every successful vendor assignment (`assignVendor`) — notify or silent, since it tracks actual work progress, not whether a text went out. First-nudge timing is `max(next 9am ET, assignment-time + 16h)` — verified against Brett's own two examples exactly (assigned 10pm → 2pm next day; assigned 5pm → 9am next day; a third case worked through by hand — assigned 6am → 10pm same day, the +16h floor winning over the same-day 9am candidate — also checked). Reassigning to a different vendor cancels the old assignee's open clock first (chasing someone no longer on the job doesn't make sense).

**Resets on real vendor activity**: a status change or schedule set FROM the vendor (`updated_by_role==='vendor'`, already how vendor.html tags every one of its own writes) resets Nudge_Count to 0 and recomputes the first-nudge formula fresh — an admin changing status on the vendor's behalf does NOT reset it, since that's not the vendor actually engaging.

**Quiets for a future Scheduled_Date, resumes automatically after**: rather than a one-way "convert the request type" flip (which the original framing — "converts to invoice-needed nudges" — read ambiguously), the nudge content is decided FRESH each time it actually fires: once a WO shows `Complete` but has no `Vendor_Bills` row, the ask becomes "please submit your invoice"; otherwise it stays "any update on status." This naturally reproduces the stated behavior (a passed scheduled date with a completed-but-unbilled job asks for the invoice) without needing separate state for it, and without ever asking a vendor for an invoice on a job the system doesn't think is done. **Documented as an interpretation, not a literal reading of the original phrasing** — flagged clearly in code comments so it's easy to correct if Brett meant something more literal by "converts."

**Caps at 5 nudges, then flags Brett instead of a 6th** — an admin-phone SMS naming the vendor/WO/request type, sent once (the row moves to `Status:'flagged'` and drops out of the sweep's "open" query, so it can't re-flag itself every cycle).

**Manual requests** ("📷 Request Photos" / "🧾 Request Invoice" buttons on the WO detail view, shown once a vendor is assigned): send immediately (no preview step — the text is short/standardized enough not to need per-send editing, unlike Welcome), then follow up every 48h (Brett didn't specify a cadence for these two specifically — a stated assumption, easy to tune) until satisfied. Photos-satisfied = a new `before`/`after`/`photo` Attachment logged for that WO after the request was created; invoice-satisfied = a real `Vendor_Bills` row exists — same check the automatic clock's invoice-ask case uses. Clicking a request button again while one's already open just resends immediately rather than creating a duplicate row.

New `test/vendor-nudges.test.mjs` (9 assertions — the exact Brett-example timing checks plus a third hand-worked case, and structural checks that the sweep only touches open rows, uses a named cap constant, checks Vendor_Bills for invoice-satisfied, and respects Scheduled_Date). `node --check` clean, index.html inline scripts parse clean, full suite green.

**NOT verified against live data. 🔴 Needs Brett's live pass**: (1) assign a vendor on a test WO, confirm a Vendor_Requests row appears with the correct First_Nudge_At; (2) have the "vendor" (via vendor.html) change status or set a schedule, confirm the clock resets; (3) manually trigger the sweep (`/cron/sweep`) after backdating a row's Next_Nudge_At (or just wait) and confirm a real nudge SMS goes out, respecting Test Mode; (4) hit "Request Photos"/"Request Invoice" on a real WO and confirm the immediate send + the follow-up cadence; (5) confirm a photos/invoice request actually resolves once a real Attachment/Vendor_Bill shows up; (6) let a row hit 5 nudges (or fake it) and confirm Brett actually gets the flag text once, not repeatedly.

**169. Welcome messages — tenant_welcome/vendor_welcome, manual send, single + bulk, with a "hasn't been welcomed yet" flag and filter (Sep 14 2026, worker.js `welcomeSend`; index.html tenant/vendor tables + new modals).** Continuing the queued message-system redesign — separate from and complementary to the existing "Send PIN" system (`sendPinMessage`): PIN send hands over portal credentials, welcome is a warm "this is Ridge Co, save this number" intro that never includes a PIN. Built directly on the existing PIN-send infrastructure rather than inventing new patterns — the per-row checkbox selection, action bar, and preview-then-send / bulk-queue-then-send modal shapes (`previewSendPin`/`bulkSendPinFromTab`) already did almost everything needed; `previewSendWelcome`/`bulkSendWelcomeFromTab` are direct siblings.

**Goes through the real gated pipeline** (`smsGatedSend`, not a raw `sendSMS`) — respects Global, Test Mode, and the person's own SMS_Enabled toggle (and, for tenants, Property/Customer too — real `property`/`owner` objects are passed in, not left to default-open). New `Welcome_Sent`/`Welcome_Sent_Date` columns on Tenants and Vendors (additive, self-provisioning), only written after confirming the message actually sent — a gate-blocked or failed send never falsely marks someone as welcomed.

**Editable at send time, not fixed text** — the single-send preview is a textarea (not a read-only div like the PIN preview), pre-filled with a personalized default (real name/property address) generated server-side, fully editable before Brett hits send. Bulk send shares ONE editable message across the whole selected batch — deliberately generic/unnamed (no per-recipient personalization in the shared text), with the modal copy explicit about that so it's not a surprise. This was a deliberate design call, not something Brett specified exactly: rather than guess exact wording that ships unreviewed, every send — single or bulk — has a review-and-edit step in the loop.

**"Hasn't been welcomed" flagging**: both Tenants and Vendors tables gained a Welcome column (badge showing sent-date or "— not yet") and a filter checkbox to show only the unwelcomed — built specifically for Brett's stated "onboarding a new property with many tenants at once" case: filter, select-all, bulk-send, done.

New `test/welcome-send.test.mjs` (11 assertions — no-PIN-in-template check, gated-not-raw-send check, correct message_type per recipient type, and a structural guard that Welcome_Sent can only be written after confirming an actual send). `node --check` clean; a source-level ID cross-check confirmed every new DOM id referenced in JS (14 of them) is actually defined in the HTML — not just visually eyeballed. Full suite green.

**NOT verified against live data. 🔴 Needs Brett's live pass**: (1) single-send a welcome to a real test tenant and vendor, confirm the preview text looks right and the edit-before-send actually works; (2) bulk-select a few and confirm the shared-text bulk send works end to end; (3) confirm the Welcome badge/filter update correctly after a send; (4) confirm a gate-blocked send (e.g. a tenant with SMS_Enabled off) shows the blocked reason in the modal rather than silently marking them welcomed.

**168. Owner messages rebuilt on the real gated pipeline — Received (new, tenant-request WOs only), Scheduled, Complete, On-Hold (new, requires a reason); Assigned and Invoiced retired (Sep 14 2026, worker.js `updateStatus`/`createWorkOrder`/`smsGateDecision`/`smsGatedSend`). Tenant Received also added (new, 8h-delayed, superseded by Assigned).** Continuing the queued message-system redesign from the Twilio build — this was the next item, closing the biggest remaining gap: every owner text was still going out through the old raw `sendSMS`, which honors the Global kill switch but NOT Test Mode, NOT the Property/Customer gates, and writes nothing to Message_Queue for review.

**Gate system extended for a third recipient type**: `smsGateDecision` now handles `kind:'owner'` (Global AND Property AND Customer — no Tenant layer, same shape vendor already has one level up). `smsGatedSend` resolves `opts.owner` as the recipient for that kind. The two places that re-resolve gates at fire/release time (`releaseMessageQueue`, `processQuietHoursQueue`) needed **no changes at all** — both already resolve `owner` unconditionally via `property.Owner_ID` for the tenant-gate's Customer check, which turns out to be exactly the right owner for an owner-type message too.

**NOTIFY_TIERS trimmed** to the 4 events that still exist (`Received`/`Scheduled`/`Complete`/`On_Hold`) — `Assigned`/`Invoiced`/`Accepted`/`In Progress`/`Pending Invoice` all retired, since Brett was explicit owners don't need an Assigned text and Invoiced is already covered by the invoice email. `On_Hold` included in every tier except `off` — a delay felt like something any owner receiving *some* notifications would want to know, not just the top tier; noted as an assumption in the comment so it's easy to split out later if Brett wants finer control.

**On-Hold now requires a reason, enforced server-side first**: `updateStatus` rejects `status:'On Hold'` with no `hold_reason` (or `notes` as a fallback field name) with a 400 — and that check is positioned before the WO is even fetched, so a bad request can never leave a half-written state (status flipped, reason blank). Stored in `Hold_Reason` (column already existed from the turnover-dependency work). Client-side: both `quickStatusChange` and the bulk-status path now prompt for a reason before submitting when the target is On Hold — bulk uses one shared reason applied to the whole batch, matching the existing precedent that Cancelled gets its own higher-friction flow.

**Owner Received**: fires immediately (no defer) on WO creation, but ONLY when `body.type === 'tenant'` — Brett was explicit an admin-created manual WO shouldn't trigger this, since the admin creating it already knows. **Tenant Received**: separate from `tenant_job_assigned`, tells the tenant only that the request landed and is pending assignment — NOT scoped to tenant-submitted WOs (matches the existing `Tenant_Notify_Created` toggle's broader scope). Queued 8 hours out via the existing `Notification_Queue` defer mechanism specifically so a fast assignment can supersede it — `processPendingNotifications` now checks, at actual fire time, whether the WO already has a `Vendor_ID` and skips the Received text if so (marks the row `Sent` without sending), so a tenant never gets "we got your request" immediately followed by "you're assigned."

New/extended tests: `test/message-queue.test.mjs` (+8 owner-gate assertions, 32 total), new `test/owner-notify.test.mjs` (15 assertions — NOTIFY_TIERS content, and a structural check that the On-Hold reason validation runs before any Sheets I/O, not just that it eventually 400s). `node --check` clean on worker.js; both `index.html` (5 blocks) and `vendor.html` inline scripts still parse clean. Full suite green.

**NOT verified against live data. 🔴 Needs Brett's live pass**: (1) create a tenant-submitted WO, confirm the owner gets a Received text and the tenant's Received text queues 8h out; (2) assign a vendor within that 8h window, confirm the queued tenant Received text gets superseded (never sends) once the sweep runs; (3) try setting a WO On Hold with no reason (both single and bulk) and confirm the 400/prompt actually block it; (4) confirm `Hold_Reason` shows up correctly in the WO detail view and in the owner's on-hold text; (5) confirm Scheduled/Complete owner texts still fire correctly now that they're gated (test mode should redirect them like everything else).

**167. Real live bug found testing rule 166: every POST route 500'd with a raw "Unexpected end of JSON input" if the client sent no body at all — exactly what `POST /cron/sweep` (and the GitHub Actions curl calling it) does (Sep 14 2026, worker.js body-parsing).** Caught immediately by actually calling the new endpoint live rather than assuming a clean deploy meant it worked — `const body = await request.json();` ran unconditionally for every POST route before reaching any handler, uncaught, so an empty body threw before `cronSweep(env)` (which doesn't even take a body) ever ran. This wasn't new to `/cron/sweep` — any existing POST route would have hit the same failure if ever called with zero body bytes; it just never came up before because every other POST caller always sends `{...}`.

Fixed generally, not just for this one route: `let body = {}; try { body = await request.json(); } catch (e) { body = {}; }` — an empty or malformed body now defaults to `{}` instead of throwing, and routes that genuinely require fields still fail with their own clear "X required" 400 from validating `body` afterward, same as always. `node --check` clean, full suite green — no test relied on the old throw-on-empty-body behavior.

**NOT verified against live data — no live credentials in this build sandbox.**

**166. Quiet hours (7pm–9am ET) hold on automatic SMS, and a free GitHub-Actions replacement for the periodic sweep Cloudflare Cron Triggers can't cheaply cover (Sep 14 2026, worker.js `isQuietHoursNow`/`nextQuietHoursEnd`/`processQuietHoursQueue`/`cronSweep`; new `.github/workflows/cron-sweep.yml`).** Brett: hold any automatic message that would fire after 7pm ET until 9am ET the next day, and asked to get off Cloudflare Cron Triggers entirely for this kind of thing given the platform limit (3 triggers/Worker free, 5 paid — all 4 paid-tier slots already spoken for: daily digest, 2x optimizer review, weekly AR report) — wanted a genuinely free alternative, floated Google Calendar.

**Quiet hours**: DST-aware without a timezone library — `nyOffsetMinutes` derives the real America/New_York UTC offset at any instant directly from `Intl.DateTimeFormat` (format the instant's NY wall-clock reading, diff it against the instant's real UTC value), so it's correct across the EDT/EST boundary without hardcoding a fixed offset. `isQuietHoursNow` / `nextQuietHoursEnd` build on that. Wired into `smsGatedSend`: gates are evaluated exactly as before (this only affects WHEN a message that already passed every gate actually goes out, never whether) — if it's quiet hours, the Message_Queue row stays `pending` with a new `Send_After` timestamp instead of sending immediately. New `test/quiet-hours.test.mjs` (12 assertions) — verified against real UTC timestamps in both EDT and EST, both quiet-hours boundaries (exactly 7pm counts as quiet, exactly 9am does not), the early-morning-vs-evening branch of `nextQuietHoursEnd`, and a month-boundary rollover.

**Cron replacement**: new `POST /cron/sweep` (narrow `CRON_SWEEP_TOKEN`, not `WORKER_SECRET` — same narrow-token convention as `SELFTEST_TOKEN`/`TRASH_NUDGE_TOKEN`, fully inert until Brett sets the secret) consolidates the periodic work into one call: (1) `processQuietHoursQueue` — fires quiet-hours-held rows once `Send_After` has passed, re-checking the full gate at that moment (same phone-based re-resolution `releaseMessageQueue` already uses) rather than trusting a stale evaluation from hours earlier, since Global/Test-Mode could plausibly change overnight; (2) `processPendingNotifications` — the existing `Notification_Queue` deferred-appointment-reminder sweep. **Real gap found and fixed while wiring this in, not a new feature riding alongside one**: `processPendingNotifications` existed already but was never called from anywhere automatic — only reachable via the admin-gated `GET /notifications/pending` — so every deferred `tenant_job_scheduled` reminder queued more than same-day out has been silently sitting unsent this whole time. New `.github/workflows/cron-sweep.yml` (same proven pattern as `selftest-invoice.yml`, the Invoice OCR Canary) hits `/cron/sweep` every 15 minutes — free on this public repo, no practical run-count limit, and doesn't compete for any of the 4 already-used Cloudflare cron slots. `/health` reports `cron_sweep.token_set` (never the value) so setup status is checkable without guessing, same pattern as the Twilio/Gmail/pricing presence checks.

**Fixed a real bug caught mid-build, before shipping**: an earlier edit of mine accidentally deleted the lead-in comment line for `skipMessageQueue` while inserting `cronSweep` nearby (a `str_replace` boundary that included a comment line I didn't mean to remove) — caught by re-reading the surrounding code before moving on, restored before committing.

`node --check` clean, full suite green including the new test file. YAML syntax of the new workflow validated directly (parsed with `pyyaml`), not just eyeballed.

**NOT verified against live data — no live Sheets/Twilio/GitHub Actions credentials in this build sandbox. 🔴 Needs Brett's setup + first live pass**: (1) set `CRON_SWEEP_TOKEN` as BOTH a Cloudflare Worker secret AND a GitHub Actions repository secret (same value in both, same one-time setup shape as `SELFTEST_TOKEN`) — until then the workflow logs "not set yet" and exits cleanly rather than failing; (2) confirm `/health` shows `cron_sweep.token_set: true` once done; (3) trigger the workflow manually (`workflow_dispatch`) once to confirm a real 200 back from `/cron/sweep`; (4) send a test message after 7pm ET, confirm it queues with `Send_After` set instead of sending immediately, then confirm the next sweep run (or a manual trigger after 9am) actually sends it.

**165. Vendor dispatch text now includes a portal deep-link — deliberately NOT the no-login shareable link, to avoid bypassing the accept-gate (Sep 14 2026, worker.js `vendorPortalLink`; `vendor.html` `?wo=` deep-link support).** Brett: vendor assignment/update messages should include the direct link (paid messages should not, but should keep the amount — already true, no change needed there).

**Real conflict caught before shipping, not after:** the obvious choice — reuse the existing shareable-WO-link mechanism (`woShareLink`/`wo.html`, built for sharing a job with someone not in the system) — turns out to deliberately skip the Aug 13 accept-gate. Its own `/wo/shared` read handler (`woSharedRead`) has an explicit comment: *"deliberately NOT passing vendorView:true here — that would also turn on the accept-gate... a behavior change this task didn't ask for."* That means `wo.html` hands over the lockbox code and tenant phone number immediately, no Accept tap required — exactly what the dispatch SMS's own wording ("the lockbox code & tenant contact unlock once you accept") promises NOT to do. Embedding that link in the dispatch text would have silently defeated the accept-gate the moment a vendor opened it.

Fixed by linking somewhere that already respects the gate correctly instead: a new `vendorPortalLink(woId)` builds a plain deep-link into the vendor's own PIN-gated portal (`vendor.html?wo=WO-1234`) — no token, no new auth surface, same login every vendor already uses; `vendor.html`'s own WO rendering already applies `accessGated`/`vendorView` correctly. `vendor.html` reads the `?wo=` param once per page load and pre-filters the job list to that WO via the existing search box, so the vendor lands straight on the job the text was about (the deep-link only applies once — a vendor who then searches for something else isn't snapped back to it). Wired into `vendor_job_assigned` only, since that's the only vendor "assignment/update" message type that currently exists; `vendor_paid` explicitly left alone per Brett's own instruction (already includes the dollar amount, correctly has no link).

New `test/vendor-portal-link.test.mjs` (5 assertions) — confirms the link format, confirms it's never a `wo.html` link, confirms URL-encoding, and specifically greps `assignVendor`'s own source to confirm it calls `vendorPortalLink` and never `woShareLink` — a regression-guard so a future edit can't quietly reintroduce the accept-gate bypass without a test catching it. `node --check` clean on worker.js and all inline scripts in both `index.html` (5) and `vendor.html` (7). Full suite green.

**NOT verified against live data. 🔴 Needs Brett's live pass**: assign a vendor on a test WO, confirm the text includes the portal link, tap it, confirm the portal opens already filtered to that WO after PIN login, and confirm the lockbox/tenant info still stays hidden until Accept is actually tapped (i.e., confirm the gate really did survive this change).

**164. Vendor dispatch text no longer tells the vendor to reply YES/NO — that path doesn't actually work right now (Sep 14 2026, worker.js `assignVendor`).** Brett fixed the old-PM-autoresponder problem (rule 158a/CURRENT.md note) by pointing the Twilio number's inbound webhook at a Twilio Studio Flow — but that Flow gives a generic reply and never forwards to this Hub's own `/sms-inbound`, so `handleInboundSMS`'s existing YES/NO-handling code (accept-gate unlock, tenant-notified-on-accept) is currently unreachable. Telling a vendor to text YES/NO was actively misleading — they'd hit the Flow's generic response, not this Hub. Message now points to the **portal** Accept/Decline buttons instead (the Aug 13 accept-gate build, a real working UI control, unaffected by any of this) — "Log in to your vendor portal to accept or decline — the lockbox code & tenant contact unlock once you accept." Spanish variant updated the same way. `handleInboundSMS`'s YES/NO code itself is untouched, not removed — it's just correctly dormant until real reply/call handling replaces the Studio Flow, which both Brett and this doc now have on record as a real future build (ties to the "future issues with this" note he flagged the same day). `node --check` clean, full suite green — no test referenced the old wording.

**NOT verified against live data. 🔴 Needs Brett's live pass**: assign a vendor on a test WO and confirm the text reads "Log in to your vendor portal to accept or decline..." with no YES/NO instruction.

**163. Tenant/vendor SMS text now names the actual job, not just Trade + Address (Sep 14 2026, worker.js `woJobLabel` + `assignVendor`/`updateStatus`/`scheduleWO`).** Brett, live-testing: two same-trade jobs at the same address (a common case — "General job at 123 Test St" for a front-steps repair AND, separately, for an unrelated leak diagnosis the same week) read completely identically in a text. No way to tell which was which without opening the Hub.

New `woJobLabel(wo)` — `"{Trade} job ({short description})"`, e.g. `"Landscaping job (Cut the grass in yard #4)"`, matching Brett's own suggested phrasing exactly. Truncates the description at 60 characters with a trailing `…` (same convention the WO card list already uses, `woCard()`'s `Description.substring(0,60)`) — "doesn't need to be too specific," per Brett, just enough that two jobs never read the same. Falls back to `"{Trade} job"` with no parenthetical when there's no description, and to `"General job"` when Trade itself is blank (matching the existing default used elsewhere).

Applied to `tenant_job_assigned`, `tenant_job_scheduled` (both the immediate and within-1-hour variants), and `tenant_job_completed` — all three previously said only `"your {Trade}"` or `"your {Trade} appointment/repair at {address}"` with the actual task nowhere in the text. `vendor_job_assigned`'s text is restructured to open with `"New {Trade} job: {full description}"` instead of the old `"New job: {Trade} at {address}. Issue: {description}."` — kept the FULL, untruncated description here rather than the 60-char version, since a vendor needs the real detail to actually do the job and already has portal access as a backup (Brett's own explicit lower-priority framing for the vendor side); the Spanish variant got the same restructuring. `tenant_manual` and `vendor_paid` untouched — a manually-composed message is already specific by definition, and a payment notice isn't describing a job.

New `test/wo-job-label.test.mjs` (8 assertions) — reproduces Brett's own two example labels exactly, confirms the two-same-trade-same-address collision case now actually produces two different labels, confirms long descriptions truncate with an ellipsis rather than sending in full, confirms blank-description and blank-Trade fallbacks, confirms a null WO never throws. `node --check` clean, full suite green (61 test files).

**NOT verified against live data — no live Sheets/Twilio credentials in this build sandbox. 🔴 Needs Brett's live pass**: assign a vendor on a WO with a real description and confirm the tenant text reads "your {Trade} job ({description})" not just "your {Trade}"; try a description over 60 characters and confirm it truncates cleanly with `…`; confirm the vendor's own dispatch text still carries the FULL description, unchanged in substance.

**161. Root-cause fix: tenant SMS silently never fired for ANY whole-property (no-Unit) work order — `currentTenantForDispatch` never had the Property_ID fallback `enrichWO` already used (Sep 14 2026, worker.js).** Brett hit this live testing the "Send update" button on a real test WO (123 Test St, tenant Ziggy Stardust) — got "No tenant with a phone number on this work order" even though the WO detail screen clearly showed her. Root-caused against live data, not guessed: `WO-1196` has both `Unit_ID` and `Tenant_ID` blank (normal for a whole-property listing — no separate units to carry a Tenant_ID), while Ziggy Stardust is a real, active Tenant row with `Property_ID` matching the WO and her own `Unit_ID` blank. The WO-detail screen found her fine because `enrichWO` (its data source) already has a 3-tier fallback ending in "an active tenant at this property with no Unit_ID of their own IS the property's tenant" — but `currentTenantForDispatch`, the helper every SMS trigger uses (`assignVendor`, both tenant branches of `updateStatus`, `scheduleWO`, and the new `tenantManualUpdate`), never had that fallback. **This predates the Twilio build entirely** — confirmed via `WO-1196`'s own `Tenant_SMS_Sent: FALSE` that the automatic tenant-assigned text silently never fired either, Brett just never noticed because Test Mode redirects both vendor and tenant messages to his own phone and he only registered getting one text.

Fixed at the root — `currentTenantForDispatch` itself gained the same fallback (`wo.Property_ID` set AND `wo.Unit_ID` blank → active tenant at that property with no `Unit_ID` of their own), so every caller is fixed at once rather than patching each call site separately. Swept and fixed 3 other call sites that had inlined the same incomplete lookup instead of calling the shared helper (`updateStatus`'s Complete and Accepted branches, `scheduleWO`) — all three now call `currentTenantForDispatch` too, so they can't drift out of sync with it again. `tenantManualUpdate` (rule 157) also switched from its own inline lookup to this same canonical helper.

New `test/tenant-dispatch-whole-property.test.mjs` (6 assertions, real headless extraction of the actual fixed function) — reproduces the exact WO-1196/Ziggy Stardust case; confirms a moved-out whole-property tenant is never matched this way; confirms a WO naming a real Unit still resolves via `Unit.Tenant_ID`, not the fallback; confirms a direct `WO.Tenant_ID` still takes priority (unchanged behavior); confirms no false match when no tenant exists at the property. **Caught a real precision bug in the first draft of this exact fix, before it shipped**: the first version fell back whenever the caller's `unit` lookup came back empty, rather than checking `wo.Unit_ID` directly (matching `enrichWO`'s own condition) — that would have wrongly reassigned a WO that names a real-but-currently-unresolvable Unit_ID to a different, unrelated tenant elsewhere on the same property. Fixed to match `enrichWO`'s exact condition before shipping. `node --check` clean, full suite green.

**NOT verified against live data — no live Sheets/Twilio credentials in this build sandbox. 🔴 Needs Brett's live pass**: re-run the "Send update" button on WO-1196 (or any other whole-property test WO) and confirm it finds Ziggy Stardust and sends; re-assign a vendor on a whole-property WO and confirm `Tenant_SMS_Sent` flips to `TRUE` this time, not just `Vendor_SMS_Sent`.

**159. Two live-testing fixes on the Twilio build, found by Brett mid-test: a "Notify vendor + tenant now" toggle on WO creation, and the bulk-edit checkbox moved to the left (Sep 14 2026, worker.js `assignVendor`; `index.html` New WO modal + `woCard`).** Brett, before he'd finished testing rule 157: assigning a vendor at WO creation time always sent the dispatch SMS immediately — no way to create a WO with a vendor already attached but skip the notification, e.g. a WO created purely to record billing for a job (Towson Appliance) he'd already arranged by phone weeks earlier, where texting the vendor would be actively confusing. Separately, the Work Orders bulk-edit checkbox rendered effectively on the right side of each card (after `.wo-info`, which has `flex:1` and absorbs the row's remaining width) — he wanted it on the far left, the conventional spot, since its current position was getting confused with the status/vendor badges next to it.

- **`assignVendor`** gains a `notify` param, default `true` (so `POST /assign` from anywhere that doesn't pass it — the Reassign Vendor modal, the bulk-reassign action — is 100% unchanged; both of those UIs already tell Brett a text is going out, so nothing about their behavior should ever change silently). Only the New Work Order creation flow can pass `notify:false`. When false, the vendor is still assigned and `Work_Orders.Status` still moves to `Assigned` exactly as before — `smsGatedSend` is just never called for either `vendor_job_assigned` or `tenant_job_assigned`, so no message is composed and no `Message_Queue` row is written at all (a deliberate "never meant to exist" case, not a gated-and-pending one — kept the review screen from filling up with rows nobody ever wanted sent).
- **Scope decision, not asked but inferred from Brett's own example:** the toggle suppresses BOTH the vendor's dispatch text and the tenant's "assigned to X" text together, not just the vendor's. Brett's own framing was "there's no reason to send them anything at all," and the tenant text names the vendor and says "they will contact you to schedule" — sending that half while suppressing the vendor's actual dispatch would leave a tenant expecting contact from someone who was never actually notified.
- **`index.html`**: a "Notify vendor + tenant now" checkbox (default checked) appears next to "Assign Vendor" on the New WO modal, but only once a vendor is actually picked (`woVendorOptChanged()`, wired to the vendor `<select>`'s `onchange`) — no point showing a notify toggle when nothing's being assigned. `submitNewWO()` reads it and passes `notify` through to `/assign`; the success toast distinguishes "+ assigned" from "+ assigned (silent)" so it's visible immediately that the suppression took.
- **Checkbox position**: `woCard()`'s bulk-select `<input>` moved to be the FIRST child of `.wo-card` (before the WO-ID), instead of sitting between `.wo-info` and `.wo-right`. `.wo-card`'s existing `display:flex; gap:16px` handles spacing automatically — swapped the checkbox's own `margin-right:6px` for `flex:none` (it no longer needs to push anything, the row gap does that now). `event.stopPropagation()` on the checkbox's own click (so it doesn't also open the WO detail via the card's `onclick="woCk(this)"`) is unchanged and still correct regardless of DOM position. Confirmed via grep this is the only bulk-checkbox-on-a-WO-list pattern in the file — Review Bills/Send & Track/Send to QB/Message Queue use the same "select multiple" convention but are bill/invoice/message lists, not work-order lists, so out of scope for "anywhere else there's a list of work orders."
- **Left alone on purpose**: the separate "📤 Send Work Order to Vendor" button (the no-login shareable-link feature, B-117) — confirmed with Brett this is a different, pre-existing mechanism, not part of the Twilio build; not touched here.

Verified: `node --check` clean on worker.js and all inline `<script>` blocks in `index.html` (5). Full existing test suite re-run clean, no regressions (this build is UI/parameter-threading only — no new pure helper worth a dedicated unit test; grepped every `/assign` call site in both files to confirm the two existing callers — Reassign Vendor modal, bulk-reassign — never pass `notify` and therefore keep their exact current always-notify behavior, matching their own "Assign + Send SMS" / "Each vendor will get the normal dispatch text" UI copy).

**NOT verified against live data — no live Sheets credentials in this build sandbox.** **🔴 Needs Brett's live pass**: (1) create a test WO, assign a vendor, leave the checkbox checked — confirm the text still goes out to his phone (Test Mode) exactly as before; (2) create another test WO, assign a vendor, UNCHECK the box — confirm the WO shows the vendor assigned and `Status: Assigned` in the Hub, but NO text arrives and nothing new shows up in Message Queue; (3) open Work Orders, tap "☑ Bulk Edit," confirm the checkboxes now render on the left edge of each card, before the WO number.

**159a. Real gap found live-testing rule 157: Twilio accepted 3 test messages (real SIDs, `Message_Queue` shows `Status:'sent'`) but Brett received none of them on his phone.** Confirmed via `GET /message-queue` (WORKER_SECRET) against production — all 3 rows from Brett's own live test (`vendor_job_assigned`×2, `tenant_job_assigned`×1, all correctly gated `all gates open`, all redirected to `+14439617927` via Test Mode) show `Status:'sent'` with real `Twilio_Message_SID`s. This means the Worker's own logic and Twilio's API accept-path both worked exactly as built — `Status:'sent'` was always documented as meaning "Twilio's API accepted the request," never "confirmed delivered" (delivery-status webhooks were explicit out-of-scope in `TWILIO_SMS_BUILD_BRIEF_v1.0.md`), and that gap is now a real, live symptom rather than a theoretical one. New **`GET /twilio/message-status?sid=SM...,SM...`** (admin-gated, up to 10 comma-separated SIDs) queries Twilio's own Message resource directly (`GET /Messages/{Sid}.json`) and returns the REAL status (`queued`/`sent`/`delivered`/`undelivered`/`failed`) + `error_code`/`error_message` — the actual diagnostic Brett needs, without waiting on delivery-status webhooks to be built. Read-only, never sends. **Not yet run against the 3 real SIDs from this session — needs this patch deployed first**; in the meantime, Brett can check the same thing directly in the Twilio Console (Monitor → Logs → Messaging) right now, no deploy required. Likely-candidate causes for "Twilio accepted it, carrier never delivered it" on a still-early A2P 10DLC number, most common first: the campaign showing brand/profile APPROVED doesn't necessarily mean the CAMPAIGN itself has cleared full carrier vetting yet (T-Mobile in particular is aggressive about filtering early-ramp traffic); a messaging-service/number mismatch if the campaign is tied to a Messaging Service SID rather than directly to `TWILIO_FROM`. Not diagnosed further without the real Twilio-side status — flagging candidates, not concluding.

**160. Twilio account/A2P-status diagnostic — `GET /twilio/account-status` (Sep 14 2026, worker.js `twilioAccountStatus`).** Brett asked directly: does he need an opt-in, can the Cloudflare Worker confirm everything's actually live, does the campaign need activating. Researched current (2026) Twilio/A2P 10DLC deliverability practice before answering rather than guessing: `Status:'sent'` on a message resource has always meant "carrier/Twilio accepted the API call," never "the recipient's device got it" — a message can sail through that stage and still be silently dropped by a carrier (most commonly error 30034, traffic from a number with no approved A2P 10DLC campaign, or 30007, carrier-side spam/policy filtering) with ZERO visible sign anywhere in `Message_Queue` since that only records what the Worker itself sees. Brand/account "approved" (already confirmed in the build brief) is a DIFFERENT, earlier gate than campaign approval — a business can be a fully approved Twilio customer while its specific SMS campaign is still pending carrier vetting, and traffic gets filtered the whole time with no error surfaced to the sender unless someone actually looks up the message or the campaign status. New endpoint checks, in order: (1) the sending number itself — found on the account, SMS-capable, its own status; (2) every A2P Brand Registration on the account and its `status` (`APPROVED` vs `PENDING`/`IN_REVIEW`/`FAILED`); (3) every Messaging Service on the account, each one's A2P Campaign compliance status (`campaign_status` — this is the actual carrier-facing gate, separate from brand approval), AND whether `TWILIO_FROM` is actually enrolled in that service's sender pool (a campaign can be fully approved and still filter every message from a number that was never added to its pool). Every sub-check independently try/caught so one failing/permission-scoped call never blocks the others from reporting. Read-only, admin-gated, never sends.

**Re: "do I need an opt-in" — two different meanings, worth keeping separate.** (a) TCPA/legal consent from the message recipient — not the live blocker for Brett's own self-testing, since Test Mode redirects every send to his own number and he obviously consents to texting himself. (b) The A2P 10DLC CAMPAIGN's own registration, which requires describing (as part of getting the campaign approved in the first place) how end users opt in/out — this is a one-time paperwork step already submitted when the campaign was registered, not something to redo per-message or per-recipient; if it's genuinely still pending review, that shows up as `campaign_status` on this new endpoint, not as anything actionable per-message.

**157. Twilio SMS integration built end-to-end, per `TWILIO_SMS_BUILD_BRIEF_v1.0.md` — 6 gated message types, a 4/2-layer kill-switch system, Test Mode, and a review/release screen (Sep 14 2026, worker.js + new `message-queue.html`; new `test/message-queue.test.mjs`, `test/manual-verify-message-queue-ui.mjs`).** Brett: wire Twilio into the Hub for tenant/vendor updates, safe to test against real formatting before it's live to real people, safe to pause/resume mid-flight without losing or double-sending anything.

- **Pre-build catch, not in the brief but load-bearing:** live `worker.js` already had ~10 unconditional `sendSMS` call sites wired into the WO lifecycle from earlier sessions (tenant assign/complete/accepted, owner status pings, admin-phone alerts, vendor accept/decline SMS replies) — all silently non-functional, since `sendSMS`'s Basic-auth used `TWILIO_SID:TWILIO_AUTH` and `TWILIO_AUTH` was never set as a Cloudflare secret (only the API-Key pair — `TWILIO_API_SID`/`TWILIO_API_KEY` — actually exists, per the brief's credentials table). Fixing the auth alone would have made every one of those ~10 pre-existing, ungated sends go live for real the moment this deployed — with no Test Mode, no review queue, defeating the whole point of the brief. Fixed by splitting the transport into `sendSMSRaw` (the actual Twilio call, no gating) and `sendSMS` (the chokepoint every legacy call site already calls — now checks `Config.TWILIO_ENABLED` first and no-ops otherwise). Every SMS in the codebase, old and new, now honors the one Global kill switch. `TWILIO_ENABLED` defaults OFF (missing Config key reads `''`, which is `!== 'TRUE'`), so nothing sends anywhere until Brett flips it — matching his original stated intent, not just the brief's 6 named types.
- **`Message_Queue` tab** (self-provisioning via `ensureTab`/`ensureColumns`, same convention as `Ops_Telemetry`) — exact schema per the brief. Every message writes a row here FIRST, unconditionally, before any gate check (`smsGatedSend`).
- **Gate logic** — `smsGateDecision` (pure, unit-tested) + `smsToggleOn` (blank/missing cell reads as ON, only literal `'FALSE'` reads as OFF — same convention as the pre-existing `Tenant_Notify_Updates` field). Tenant messages need Global AND Property AND Customer(=Owners tab) AND Tenant; vendor messages need only Global AND Vendor — vendor does not nest under property, per the brief. New `SMS_Enabled` toggle columns (additive, `ensureColumns`) on Properties/Owners/Tenants/Vendors, default ON.
- **Test Mode** — `Config.TWILIO_TEST_MODE` (default ON if the key is missing) redirects the actual Twilio recipient to `Config.TWILIO_TEST_RECIPIENT` (falls back in code to `+14439617927` if that key isn't set) AFTER the full gate has already passed — a message still has to clear every gate to send at all, Test Mode only changes where it lands.
- **6 message types wired**: `tenant_job_assigned` (`assignVendor` — now also includes the assigned vendor's name + formatted phone, via new `formatPhoneDisplay` helper, per the brief), `tenant_job_scheduled` (`scheduleWO` — both the immediate same-day/within-hour path AND the pre-existing `Notification_Queue` deferred-send path, extended with additive columns so a message queued days out re-evaluates the FULL gate at actual fire time, not at schedule time), `tenant_job_completed` (`updateStatus`, Complete branch), `tenant_manual` (new `POST /wo/tenant-update-manual` + a "📱 Send update" button on the WO detail sticky bar in `index.html` — Brett types the body, the Worker auto-wraps "Hi {First_Name}, ... Ref: {wo_id}."), `vendor_job_assigned` (`assignVendor` — message content UNCHANGED, the Aug-13 accept-gate lockbox/tenant-contact withholding is untouched, only gated+queued now), `vendor_paid` (`qbPayBills`, after a real QuickBooks BillPayment succeeds — resolves the Hub's own Vendor row via `QBO_Vendor_ID`, `WO_ID` deliberately blank since one payment can cover several WOs, matching the brief's own note on that field).
- **Review/release screen** — new standalone `message-queue.html` (same auth/nav/style convention as `trash.html`/other tool pages, linked from Hub → Dev Log → 🧰 TOOLS). Grouped by property+recipient (tenant) or vendor, status tabs (Pending/Sent/Skipped/Failed/All), a status strip pulling live Global/Test-Mode state off `/health`, individual Send/Skip per row, and the established "☑ Select multiple" bulk pattern (same convention as Review Bills/Send & Track) for "Send selected"/"Skip selected". New `POST /message-queue/release` re-evaluates the full gate AT RELEASE TIME (not whatever it was when originally queued) and updates the SAME row in place — never inserts a duplicate. `POST /message-queue/skip` never deletes (`Active` stays `TRUE`, matches the Hub's "keep forever" pattern for reviewed rows elsewhere).
- **`/health`** now reports Twilio secret-presence booleans (`sid_set`/`api_key_sid_set`/`api_key_secret_set`/`from_set`) plus the live `twilio_enabled`/`test_mode` Config state — same never-expose-values pattern as the existing pricing/Gmail checks.
- **Explicitly out of scope, matching the brief**: two-way/inbound SMS, delivery-status webhooks (`Status:'sent'` means handed to Twilio, not confirmed delivered), per-message-type granular toggles beyond the gate layers, A2P 10DLC (already done), Milam Ridge's cleaner-coverage scheduler (untouched).

Verified: `node --check` clean on worker.js; all inline `<script>` blocks extracted and checked clean across `index.html` (5) and the new `message-queue.html` (1). New `test/message-queue.test.mjs` — 28 assertions directly against the real `smsGateDecision`/`smsToggleOn`/`formatPhoneDisplay` pure helpers (every single-gate-off case for both tenant and vendor kinds, the all-gates-open case, the all-gates-off case, vendor correctly ignoring Property/Customer/Tenant toggles, phone-formatting edge cases). Full existing suite re-run clean, 53/53 files, no regressions. A real headless-Chromium pass (`test/manual-verify-message-queue-ui.mjs`, mocked Worker responses, 11 checks) on the review screen specifically — the part protecting Brett from a real mass-send mistake: renders/groups the mocked pending rows correctly, surfaces the Gate_Snapshot reason on a pending row, an individual row's Send button releases only that one row, Select-multiple reveals checkboxes and the sticky action bar stays hidden until something is actually checked, the running count and the Skip-selected payload both reflect exactly the checked subset (2 of 3), not the whole visible list.

**NOT verified — no live Google Sheets/Twilio-send credentials in the build sandbox**: creating the `Message_Queue` tab and the 4 `SMS_Enabled` columns live, an actual Twilio send (even in Test Mode), and the 3 new Config keys (code-level defaults are correct without them — `TWILIO_ENABLED` absent reads as OFF, `TWILIO_TEST_MODE` absent reads as ON — but they don't exist as literal Config rows yet; Brett may want them written explicitly for visibility/editability in the Hub's own Config screen, purely optional). **🔴 Needs Brett's first live pass, in this order**: (1) confirm `/health`'s new `twilio` block shows all 4 secrets present; (2) with Global still OFF, trigger a tenant_job_assigned (assign a vendor to a test WO) and confirm a `pending` row appears in Message Queue with the right Gate_Snapshot ("Global OFF"); (3) flip `Config.TWILIO_ENABLED=TRUE` with Test Mode still ON (default), re-trigger, confirm a real text lands on `+14439617927` and the queue row shows `sent`; (4) open `message-queue.html`, confirm grouping/status tabs/Select-multiple all work against real data, try Send/Skip on a real pending row; (5) test the tenant_manual button on a real WO; (6) once formatting looks right on real sends, turn Test Mode OFF and do one supervised real-recipient send before trusting it broadly.

Human-gate required: no (nothing here is a QuickBooks/money write — SMS sends are Rung-1/2 territory per `AUTONOMY_GUARDRAILS_v1.0`, not Rung 3), but Global defaulting OFF and Test Mode defaulting ON together mean nothing reaches a real phone number without Brett's own explicit two-step opt-in regardless.

**154. Who To Pay — grouped/collapsible sections, full filter/search parity with Work Orders, auto-check on open, and QuickBooks reads batched from one-per-job to two total (Sep 9 2026, `worker.js` `qbPayables`, `index.html` `showPage`/`loadPayables`/new `payablesBuildFilters`/`renderPayablesList`/`payablesContext`).** Brett: collapse "nothing to pay"/"vendor paid" so he isn't scrolling past done-states, but keep them searchable; filter/search the page the same way Work Orders already does; check QuickBooks automatically on open, not just on button-press. Then, asked directly whether auto-checking a few times a day costs anything or risks a rate limit: researched rather than guessed — Intuit's 2025 App Partner Program meters read-type ("CorePlus") calls at 500,000/month on the free Builder tier (unmetered above that only on paid tiers), separate from the standing 500 req/min-per-realm rate limit; at this account's real volume (tens of jobs, a handful of checks a day) neither is remotely close, so cost/limits were never the actual constraint — round-trip time was. Fixed that anyway: `qbPayables` previously issued one `GET invoice/{id}` and one `GET bill/{id}` PER row in the 90-day window (why the page was read-on-demand-only in the first place); now issues at most two `SELECT * FROM Invoice|Bill WHERE Id IN (...)` batch queries total, chunked at 200 ids (QBO's own docs: only `=` and `IN` are valid operators on the Id field; a query response caps at 1000 rows). An id the batch doesn't return (bad/deleted) now falls through to the same null/"unknown" treatment a failed single GET used to produce for just that one row, without taking the rest of the batch down with it. `showPage('payables', ...)` now calls `loadPayables(false)` on every open (was a deliberate no-op, per its own prior comment, specifically because of the per-job-call cost above — no longer the bottleneck it was). Frontend: rows are joined to their Work Order (→ Property → Owner) via the same top-up-state-if-empty pattern `loadInvoiceReview()` already established, powering new State/Vendor/Property/Owner/Trade filter dropdowns plus a free-text search (WO#, vendor, address, owner, invoice #, trade, description) — all client-side against the already-fetched rows, no extra QB calls per keystroke. Sections: `possible duplicate` / `waiting on the owner` / `unknown` stay always fully visible, no toggle; `PAY THE VENDOR` / `owner paid in part` are `<details open>` — collapsible but open by default; `vendor paid` / `nothing to pay` are `<details>` closed by default.
