Any active search/filter force-opens every group (a match can never end up invisible behind a closed toggle), and Clear restores the original collapsed/open state. The top "N vendors waiting on $X" banner and the pay-bills checkbox bar are still built off the full unfiltered row set, so the global picture doesn't disappear just because the list below is narrowed. **Verified**: `node --check` clean on worker.js and all 5 `index.html` inline script blocks; a headless-Chromium run (`test/manual-verify-payables-filters.mjs`, 23 assertions — every filter renders, every group's default open/closed state, search narrows the count AND force-opens a normally-collapsed group, Clear restores both the count and the collapsed state, vendor filter narrows correctly, property/owner dropdowns resolve real addresses/names) caught two real bugs in the test harness itself before it could pass clean: it never simulated the login gate (`#app` stays `display:none` until `GET /config` succeeds, which this offline mock never triggered — every `page.fill()` correctly refused to act on a 0×0 element) and its assertions expected literal ALL-CAPS header text where the implementation (correctly, matching this codebase's existing per-row state-badge convention) uses CSS `text-transform:uppercase` over mixed-case source text — `element.textContent` is unaffected by CSS, so a case-insensitive comparison is the right fix, not re-casing the labels. Also updated `test/who-to-pay-duplicate-guard.test.mjs`'s `qbApi` mocks (2 spots), which asserted on the now-retired `invoice/{id}`/`bill/{id}` REST-path shape — re-verified 21/21 against the batched query shape. Full suite **50/50** (also incidentally confirms the "2 pre-existing failures" baseline several older FEATURE_LOG entries still reference was fully fixed at rule 152, Sep 8 — nothing new here, just noting the stale references). **Not yet run against live QuickBooks or a live Sheet** — no `WORKER_SECRET`/QB credentials in this session; correctness is verified against the real extracted code paths + a real headless browser, not guessed, but Brett should confirm on first open. **First check once deployed:** open Who To Pay and confirm it starts loading immediately (no more "Hit Check QuickBooks" placeholder); confirm "Vendor paid" and "Nothing to pay" start collapsed with a correct count in the header; type a vendor name or WO# into the new search box and confirm it narrows the list AND pops open a collapsed group if the match is inside one; try the State/Vendor/Property/Owner/Trade dropdowns; hit Clear and confirm it goes back to the original view.

**153. Who To Pay — double-pay guard: detects a vendor payment made outside QuickBooks' Pay Bills flow and never linked to the Bill, before ever telling Brett to pay it again (Sep 8 2026, `worker.js` `qbFindLikelyUnlinkedPayment`/`qbPayables`, `index.html` `card()`).** Brett, live, with a real example: "WO-1025 · Alex Busey ... this one shows up as payable to the vendor on my who to pay page but the qb entry says paid by venmo... i can't afford to double-pay vendors." Root-caused, not guessed, against QuickBooks' own data model: `qbPayables` correctly reads a Bill's LIVE `Balance` from the QuickBooks API on every call (confirmed no caching) — so if it says a bill is still open, QuickBooks' `Bill` object genuinely still shows an open balance. The real gap is upstream of that read: a `Bill`'s `Balance` only changes when a `BillPayment` transaction is created and linked to it via QuickBooks' own Pay Bills flow. A payment made outside that flow — Venmo, Zelle, a check — and then hand-entered into QuickBooks as a plain Expense/Check (`Purchase` entity) against the vendor has **no link back to the Bill it was really for**, so the Bill sails on showing open even though the vendor was actually paid. This exact failure mode was named once before and never systematically guarded against (Andreas Cleaning audit, Sep 4 2026: "it was a QuickBooks Expense/Check entered directly against the vendor rather than a Bill payment (won't show in a Bill query)"). **Fix — a supplementary, read-only cross-check, never a correction:** new `qbFindLikelyUnlinkedPayment(env, token, vendorQboId, amountOwed)` queries QuickBooks `Purchase` transactions for the vendor (`select Id, TxnDate, TotalAmt, PaymentType, DocNumber, PrivateNote from Purchase where EntityRef = '<id>'`) and looks for one within $1 of what's still owed — amount is the only reliable signal, since a `Purchase` has no bill-reference field by definition (that's the whole problem), so this deliberately doesn't also filter by date. `qbPayables` now runs this check ONLY for rows that would otherwise resolve to `'PAY THE VENDOR'` (the one state where a double-pay is actually possible — confirmed via a dedicated test that an already-`'vendor paid'` row never even triggers the extra QB API call) — a match overrides the row's `state` to a new `'possible duplicate'` and attaches the matched transaction's date/amount/payment-type/memo. **Every failure mode fails open, never closed**: no vendor QBO id, $0 owed, or a QuickBooks API error all fall through to null with no warning — this can only ever ADD information on top of the existing state, never hide, suppress, or auto-correct anything (matches `QUALITY_BAR_v1.0`'s Money Change "reconcile-flags-never-auto-corrects" invariant exactly). `index.html`: `'possible duplicate'` sorts above even `'PAY THE VENDOR'` (position -1), renders in danger-red with an explicit "⚠ POSSIBLE DUPLICATE — CHECK QB" badge and a detail line naming the exact matched QuickBooks transaction, is excluded from the bulk-pay dollar total/count, and — critically — **is never checkable in the bulk "mark paid in QB" selector** (`canPay` still requires the literal string `'PAY THE VENDOR'`), so a flagged bill cannot get swept into a batch action; Brett has to go verify in QuickBooks and clear it there first. New `test/who-to-pay-duplicate-guard.test.mjs` (21 assertions, two parts): the helper in isolation (finds a matching Venmo purchase, tolerates a few cents of slack, ignores unrelated amounts, fails open on no-vendor-id/zero-owed/API-error, never crashes) and a full `qbPayables` state-machine integration reproducing Brett's exact WO-1025/Alex Busey numbers verbatim (owner billed $495 paid in full, vendor bill $297.50 still open, a matching $297.50 Venmo Purchase in QuickBooks) — confirms the row flips to `'possible duplicate'` with the right transaction attached, is excluded from `owed_now`/`owed_total`, and — as a regression guard — that an ordinary case with no matching Purchase still correctly says `'PAY THE VENDOR'` (no false positives), and an already-fully-paid bill never even triggers the extra lookup. `node --check` clean on worker.js and all 5 `index.html` script blocks. Full suite 50/50. **NOT verified against live QuickBooks — this session had no `WORKER_SECRET`/QB credentials, so the fix is built and offline-tested against synthetic data reproducing Brett's exact numbers, but has not yet actually run `GET /qb/payables` against the real WO-1025/Alex Busey row.** Brett should check directly in QuickBooks in the meantime (see CURRENT.md) rather than wait for deploy to confirm the account is safe.

**152. Both pre-existing test-suite failures (`pricing-model`, `scope-core`) actually fixed, not just documented (Sep 8 2026, test-only — zero production code touched).** Every session since at least Aug 12 had noted "same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures" as an accepted baseline without anyone actually diagnosing them. Brett asked directly what they meant and how to fix them — traced both to real, root-caused, single-cause bugs, both in the TEST HARNESSES themselves, not in worker.js/index.html: (1) `pricing-model.test.mjs` extracts `calcTieredEstimate`/`invPricing` out of `index.html` via regex and evals them in a synthetic sandbox — but those two functions read pricing config off a bare module-level global (`var PRICING_CFG = null`, populated at real app boot via `GET /pricing-config`), never as a function parameter, and the extraction only ever pulled the function bodies, never that top-level declaration — so every synthetic run threw `ReferenceError: PRICING_CFG is not defined` the instant either function ran. (2) `scope-core.test.mjs` extracts worker.js's own `calcTieredEstimate(rawCost, pc)`, which returns `null` outright when `pc` is missing/unconfigured (`if (!pc || !Array.isArray(pc.tiers) || !pc.tiers.length) return null;`) — the test called it with only one argument at every call site, so `pc` was always `undefined` and every call returned `null`, then crashed the next line reading `.finalPrice` off it. Both fixed by supplying the same real, already-proven tiered-config shape `test/scope-variants.test.mjs` already uses successfully against this exact function (`{tiers:[[1000,.35,50],[2000,.30,0],[null,.25,0]], adminFee:85, adminFeeThreshold:3000, cardFeeMult:1.05, roundTo:5}`) — declared as a module-level global in both of `pricing-model.test.mjs`'s synthetic-module preambles, and passed explicitly as the second argument at every `calcTieredEstimate()` call site in `scope-core.test.mjs`. **Full suite is now 49/49 — the first time this baseline has ever been fully green.** Nothing about the underlying pricing math or config-loading changed; this was purely a stale-test-harness gap that pre-dated the config-driven pricing refactor and was never updated to match it.

**151. Who To Pay — direct "open bill in QB" deep link on every vendor-bill card (Sep 8 2026, `index.html` `card()`/`qbBillLink()`).** Brett, with a real example (WO-1048 · Eddie Smith · vendor inv WO-1048 · cust inv #1619 · PAY THE VENDOR): "this is ready to pay but i have to look up the bill in QB... I want to go from a bill link on the who to pay page directly to that bill so i can schedule payment when i'm on my desktop/laptop." Found an exact, already-proven precedent already shipping on Send & Track: `qbInvoiceLink(id)` builds `https://app.qbo.intuit.com/app/invoice?txnId=<id>` for customer invoices. Added the mirror-image `qbBillLink(id)` (same base URL, `/app/bill` instead of `/app/invoice`) and wired an "open bill in QB" link into `loadPayables()`'s `card(r)` renderer, right next to the "Vendor owed $X" line, using the real QuickBooks Bill ID already present on every row (`ir.QB_Bill_ID`, confirmed live against the QuickBooks Bill API in `qbPayables` itself — not a guess). Shows whenever a row has a real `bill_id`, not gated to `PAY THE VENDOR` specifically — Brett can look up any bill this way, e.g. a partially-paid one too. Opens in a new tab (`target="_blank" rel="noopener"`), same convention as the invoice link. `node --check` clean on all 5 `index.html` script blocks. New `test/who-to-pay-qb-link.test.mjs` (10 assertions) extracts the real `card(r)` renderer and reproduces Brett's exact WO-1048/Eddie Smith example verbatim — confirms the link URL/bill id, that it still shows the amount/terms/due-date/owner-billed text unchanged, that it degrades cleanly to the existing "no vendor bill" text when there's no bill, that it shows on non-PAY-THE-VENDOR states too, and that the bill id is URL-encoded rather than concatenated raw. Full suite 49/49. **First check once deployed:** open Who To Pay, confirm any card with a bill shows "open bill in QB," tap it, confirm it lands on the right bill in QuickBooks in a new tab.

**150. Who To Pay's "completed" bucket can no longer hide an unbilled job — a new read-time-only 'ready-to-bill' status, linked to the Work Order (Sep 8 2026, `worker.js` `scopeList()`).** Follow-up correction to rule 149 below, same day: Brett flagged that "completed" needs to track the Work Order (how billing actually gets triggered), not just the Scope's own booking history — "so we don't end up with unbilled jobs in the 'complete' pile of proposals." Root problem named precisely: if "completed" were driven by the WO reaching Complete, a job could sit in the collapsed, easy-to-forget bottom bucket while genuinely still unbilled, since physical completion and final invoicing are two different events that can lag each other by days or weeks. Fixed with a THIRD, deliberately never-persisted status: `scopeList()` now checks, for every scope whose own Status is `'invoiced'` (deposit booked) and has a `WO_ID`, whether the linked Work Order's status is still in `WO_STILL_ACTIVE_STATUSES` (`New/Assigned/Accepted/In Progress/On Hold`) — if the WO has moved PAST that (Complete/Pending Invoice/anything later) but the scope's own final balance hasn't been booked yet, the row's displayed `status` is overridden to `'ready-to-bill'` for that response only; nothing is ever written back to the Scopes sheet, so this can never go stale — it's recomputed fresh on every `/scopes` read. Only fetches `Work_Orders` at all when at least one row could actually need it (cheap by default, tested explicitly). `'completed'` (the collapsed, safe-to-ignore bucket) is now reserved strictly for `Status === 'fully-invoiced'` — i.e. genuinely, already fully billed — so nothing can reach it without money having actually moved. `scope-creator.html`: new amber `.pill.readytobill` style + `'ready to bill'` label; `loadScopes()` now renders a 4th, always-visible, never-collapsed warning banner ("⚠ N job(s) marked done on the work order but not yet billed — generate the final invoice from Signed Proposals") above even the active list — structurally impossible to bury alongside the collapsed bundles. New `test/scope-ready-to-bill.test.mjs` (8 assertions, extracts the real `scopeList()` + `WO_STILL_ACTIVE_STATUSES`): WO still active → stays plain in-progress; WO done, final not booked → ready-to-bill; already fully-invoiced → stays completed regardless of WO status (doesn't even need the WO lookup to be correct); missing/unknown `WO_ID` or a `WO_ID` with no matching Work_Orders row → fails safe to plain in-progress, never throws; confirms `Work_Orders` is never fetched at all when nothing could need it. Headless Playwright run confirms the banner and card render structurally outside any `<details>` (not just visually hidden). `node --check` clean, full suite 46/48 at the time (same 2 pre-existing failures — see rule 152, fixed same day).

**149. Proposal photos, editable scope title, and in-progress/completed status grouping — three separate asks in one session (Sep 8 2026, `worker.js` + `scope-creator.html` + `scope-proposal.html`).** Brett, after a real live gap (Riverside Ave / Tyler Frank — photos uploaded to the WO from his phone, estimate generated separately on desktop, no photos on the sent link): "proposal needs to share photos from estimate as well... also need to be able to edit title after creation and at any time. Also move accepted and funded proposals into an -in-progress status... once completed they should be collapsed into completed... in-progress can also be collapsed as long as I can change the status from the work order to generate the final bill." **(1) Photos:** new `scopeProposalPhotos(env, s)` (worker.js) reads the scope's Attachments by `WO_ID` (or `SCOPE-<id>` before a WO exists — the same staging key `scopeToWO()` already re-keys at conversion, so this is correct at any point in the lifecycle with zero extra wiring), returns only customer-safe, viewable types (`before`/`photo`/`other` — never `after`/`report`/`receipt`/`bill`/`invoice`), included as a new `photos` array on `scopeProposalView`'s response. These files are already made anyone-with-link shareable at upload time (`logAttachment` → `driveShareAnyone`, rule from FEATURE_LOG's original vendor-media sharing work), so no new sharing/auth work was needed — just reading and returning them. `scope-proposal.html` gained a photo strip + tap-to-open lightbox (same `drive.google.com/thumbnail?id=` convention already proven in `index.html`/`vendor.html`'s own WO galleries), wired into both the interactive and legacy flat-text render paths. **(2) Title:** new "✎" button next to the scope title in the editor, callable any time — not just at scope-creation — via the same `/scope/update` path `saveItems()` already uses (backend already supported `body.title`, this was purely a missing UI control). **(3) Status:** found that `Scopes.Status` already flips to `'invoiced'`/`'fully-invoiced'` at exactly the right moments (`scopeProposalBook`/`scopeProposalBookFinal` success — i.e. Brett's own "accepted and funded" → "completed" language already matched the existing money-booking hooks) — but `scope-creator.html`'s `statusPill()` had no mapping for either and silently rendered both as a generic grey "draft" pill (a real pre-existing display bug). Fixed the pill labels/colors and reworked `loadScopes()` to bucket the list into active (unchanged, always visible), a collapsed "In progress" `<details>`, and a collapsed "Completed" `<details>` at the bottom — collapsing is purely a list-view convenience, doesn't touch the WO's own status or the final-bill trigger on Signed Proposals. **Superseded same day by rule 150** — Brett caught that "completed" needs to be linked to the Work Order, not just money-booking history, to avoid unbilled jobs hiding in the collapsed pile; see rule 150 for the actual final status logic. New `test/scope-proposal-photos.test.mjs` (9 assertions: right WO, right types, mime detection incl. no-mime-still-counts-as-image, fails open on a Sheets error, no vendor-cost/private-field leakage). Headless Playwright runs verified all three pieces end-to-end: photo thumbnails render and the lightbox opens/navigates/closes correctly; title edit fires the right `/scope/update` call and updates the DOM immediately; list grouping buckets correctly with bundles starting collapsed. `node --check` clean on worker.js and both HTML files' inline scripts. Full suite 45/47 at the time (same 2 pre-existing failures — see rule 152).

**148. Fixed: adding an "alternate option" on a scope item and tapping "Generate proposal" silently dropped the new option — the customer never got a selector (Sep 7 2026, `scope-creator.html` `genProposal()`).** Brett, screenshotting a real job (3101 Gibbons — deck replacement, "Treated lumber decking" $11,880 vs "Use composite decking instead of treated yellow pine" $15,480 vendor cost): "adding optional changes does not change the proposal nor give me selector items on the proposal so that tenant can choose option." Root-caused from live code, not guessed: `/scope/proposal` (`scopeProposal()`, worker.js) always builds `Proposal_Text`/`Proposal_Items_JSON` from the scope's already-SAVED `Line_Items` on the Sheet — it has no idea what's still sitting unsaved in the browser. `genProposal()` in `scope-creator.html` called `/scope/proposal` directly with only `scope_id`, never first persisting whatever was on screen via `/scope/update` — unlike `applyCommand()` and `splitSelected()`, which both already do exactly that before acting. So the sequence Brett was actually doing — add an alternate option in step 2 (`addVariant()`), then go straight to step 7's "📄 Generate proposal" without a separate tap on "💾 Save edits" — silently regenerated the proposal from the OLD, already-saved item data. The new option never reached `Proposal_Items_JSON`, so `scope-proposal.html`'s already-built variant-picker (rule 123 — radio-button choice per item when `variants.length > 1`, live-updating total, selection recorded at signing) correctly rendered nothing new, because the server genuinely never received the new option. **Not a missing feature** — the customer-facing selector UI and its whole pricing/signing path were already fully built and working (rule 123); this was a one-step workflow gap in the admin tool. **Fix:** `genProposal()` now calls `collectItems()` and `POST /scope/update` (same call `saveItems()`/`applyCommand()` already make) immediately before `POST /scope/proposal`, so whatever's on screen — including an alternate option just added and never explicitly saved — is guaranteed to be what the proposal is generated from. A failed save now stops before generating (clear error shown) instead of silently generating from stale data. Zero worker.js changes; reuses the existing, already-shipped `/scope/update` write path exactly as `saveItems()` does — no new Sheet field, no new endpoint, no new risk surface. **Verified with a real headless-browser reproduction** (Playwright): scripted the exact scenario (open a scope with one priced item, click "+ Add alternate option," fill both vendor-cost/label fields, click "Generate proposal" — no separate Save tap) against BOTH a fresh unmodified clone and the fixed file. Pre-fix: only `/scope/proposal` fires, `/scope/update` never called — the new option is silently lost while the UI still reports "✅ proposal ready," exactly reproducing Brett's report. Post-fix: `/scope/update` fires first with both variants (correct labels/costs) in its payload, then `/scope/proposal`. `node --check`-equivalent (extracted inline `<script>`) syntax-clean; full suite re-run 44/46, same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures (`pricing-model`, `scope-core`), nothing newly broken (this change touches only `scope-creator.html`, worker.js untouched). **First check once deployed:** open the 3101 Gibbons scope (or any scope with a priced item), confirm the deck item still shows only its original single price if unchanged, then add a 2nd alternate option to a fresh item, tap "Generate proposal" directly (no separate Save), and confirm the shareable proposal link now shows both options as a real selector with live-updating total/deposit as you pick between them.

**139. Real checkbox multi-select for sending bills/invoices to QuickBooks — Send & Track (AR) and Send to QB (AP), both screens (Aug 24, 2026; `index.html` `renderSendTrack`/`stRow`/`loadSendTrack` + new `stToggleBulkMode`/`stSelectAllVisible`/`stUpdateBulkBar`/`stBulkSend`; `renderQBCard`/`loadQBQueue`/`qbBatchSend` + new `qbToggleBulkMode`/`qbSelectAllVisible`/`qbUpdateBulkBar`/`qbBulkSendSelected`/`qbSendRows`).** Brett asked "where's my select-multiple-bills-to-send-to-QB fix" believing he'd built it over the weekend (Aug 22-23) — traced every commit from that weekend (Receipt Reconciler UI, B-127 routing, B-142/validate wiring, trash service, session-efficiency protocol) and confirmed nothing touched bill selection at all; this was a memory mix-up with the Review Bills "Select multiple"/"Approve selected" toggle (rule 98, Aug 17), which only bulk-*approves* bills into the QB queue and explicitly never sends to QuickBooks. Two real gaps existed and both are now closed: (1) **Send & Track** (customer invoices/AR) had ONLY a one-at-a-time "Send →" per row — no bulk path ever existed there. (2) **Send to QB** (vendor bills/AP) had an all-or-nothing "⚡ Send all N" button (B-015, July 20) but no way to pick a subset. Both fixes are frontend-only, zero worker.js changes, because both reused already-shipped, already-tested backend capability that the UI never exposed: `POST /ar/remind` already accepts an `invoice_ids` array and loops server-side (rule 81) — `stBulkSend` calls it with `preview:true` across every checked invoice for one combined confirm() (names + total, mirrors `sendInvoiceNow`'s existing preview-first discipline), then the real send restricted to whatever the preview's own re-check says `willSend` (a row that flips to already-paid/no-email between preview and confirm is naturally excluded, never smuggled through). `qbBulkSendSelected`/`qbToggleBulkMode` mirror the same "☑ Select multiple" sticky-bar UX as rule 98's Review Bills pattern; the original `qbBatchSend` loop body was extracted into a shared `qbSendRows(rows, btn, statusEl, heldBack)` so "Send all" and "Send selected" are the exact same sequential `/qb/send-invoice` call, same 400ms pacing, same warning surfacing — not two diverging code paths. **Independent review subagent caught one real gap before ship**: `qbBulkSendSelected` relied only on the checkbox's `disabled` attribute to keep `needs_individual_send` rows (no QuickBooks status on record — could double-invoice a job already billed by hand) out of a batch send, unlike `qbBatchSend`'s explicit array `.filter()`. Fixed with the same code-level filter, not just a UI-level one, before push. `node --check` clean on all 5 `index.html` script blocks. **Known, pre-existing, out of scope for this fix**: `qbSendRows` still combines ALL open sibling bills on a WO into one invoice regardless of which checkbox(es) were ticked (same as single Preview & Send always has — surfaced via the existing `combines_with` banner, not silent); "Send all" still passes no button reference into `qbSendRows` so a double-tap isn't blocked mid-run (pre-existing, unchanged by this fix). **First check once deployed:** on Send & Track, toggle "Select multiple," check 2-3 not-sent invoices, tap "Send selected," confirm the combined dialog lists the right customers/total and only the confirmed ones actually send; on Send to QB, toggle "Select multiple," confirm a `needs_individual_send` row shows a disabled checkbox with the explanation instead of a live one, check a couple of batchable rows, tap "Send selected," confirm only those specific bills post to QuickBooks.

**119. Scope-proposal `note`-field leak fixed (live incident) + hand-edited Proposal_Text guard + one-tap link revoke/relink (Aug 24, 2026; `worker.js` `scopeProposalView`/`scopeUpdate`, `scope-creator.html`, `scope-proposal.html`; NEW `test/scope-proposal-note-leak.test.mjs` + `test/scope-update-proposal-text-guard.test.mjs`).** Brett found a vendor's raw quote text ("Vendor quote $650 for this full punch list (lump sum, not per item).") rendered directly on a live customer-facing proposal link (931 St Paul Apt 2F, Scope #2), right next to the final price. Root cause: `scopeProposalView` already stripped `vendor_cost` from every price variant at the public boundary (rule 123's #1 non-negotiable) but never stripped the item's separate free-text `note` field — a generic field with no internal/customer split, introduced back in rule 90 with only "no prices in the generated JSON" as its stated constraint. Brett had typed the vendor's quote into that box as a reminder to himself; it went straight to the customer. **Fix, closed at two layers:** (1) `scopeProposalView`'s customer item-mapping no longer includes `note` at all (matches the existing vendor_cost-strip pattern exactly); (2) `scope-proposal.html`'s `itemHtml()` no longer renders `it.note` even if it were ever present again (defense-in-depth); (3) the item editor's "Note" field in `scope-creator.html` is now labeled "private; never shown to the customer" to match the vendor-cost field's existing label, addressing the root behavioral cause. **Same-day follow-up** (Brett: "fix the hand edit path as well"): the separate hand-edit path — `POST /scope/update` letting an admin/Cowork session save a hand-typed `Proposal_Text` directly, bypassing the AI rewrite in `scopeProposal()` — had the identical unfiltered-free-text risk, just via a different write path into the same customer-facing surface (the legacy flat-text fallback `renderLegacyText()` uses when a scope has no `Proposal_Items_JSON` yet). Added `findVendorPricingLeak(text)`: a keyword/phrase net for vendor-pricing language (vendor/contractor/sub cost|quote|quoted|price|charged, "we were quoted", markup, money-sense "margin" — deliberately NOT bare "margin," which collided with ordinary door/cabinet trade language — "our/my cut/profit," "we're making/clearing $..."). `scopeUpdate` now runs it against `body.proposal_text` and rejects the save (400, no write) with a specific "here's what you typed that can't ship" message rather than silently stripping or saving it anyway. **Documented limitation, not a bug**: this is a keyword net, not a semantic filter — two dollar amounts with no trigger word (e.g. "our cost is $400, customer price is $650") still slip through; it's a backstop on an admin-only endpoint, not a guarantee, and is not sold as leak-proof. (4) **New "🔁 Kill old link & get a new one" button** in `scope-creator.html`, next to the existing Copy Link — calls the already-existing-but-previously-UI-less `/scope/proposal/link-revoke` (bumps `Link_Rev`, invalidating every token minted against the old value) then `/scope/proposal/link` (mints fresh), reusing Brett's already-stored admin token via the existing `post()`/`token()` helpers — no new credential handling. Confirmed via adversarial trace that revoking+re-minting a link for an ALREADY-SIGNED scope still correctly shows the locked/signed view (Scope_Signatures lookup is keyed on Scope_ID, independent of Link_Rev/token) rather than reopening it as editable. **Validated via `ridgeco-validate`, twice** (fresh subagent each time, no memory of the build): both PASS-WITH-NOTES, zero criticals — traced every other public scope/proposal path (scopeProposalSign, the legacy text path, PUBLIC_PATHS) for the same leak class and found none; confirmed no false-positive on the standard "Total Estimated Cost / Required 50% Deposit" boilerplate; found and fixed two real gaps from the first pass (possessive/plural "vendor's quote"/"vendors quote" and "sub charged us $..." were missed; bare "margin" false-positived on ordinary trade language) before shipping. Full suite re-run clean both times — same 2 pre-existing/documented-unrelated failures (`pricing-model`, `scope-core`). **First check once deployed:** for the affected scope (#2, 931 St Paul Apt 2F), tap "Kill old link & get a new one," open the new link in a private/incognito tab, confirm the vendor-quote line is gone before Brett sends it to the customer — the old link is dead either way once that button is tapped.

**118. Quality Bar / Definition-of-Done rubric shipped — B-144, the last named Phase-1 gate before B-127/B-211 deployment (Aug 23, 2026; new `context/QUALITY_BAR_v1.0.md`, doc-only, no worker.js/index.html touched).** `BUILD_ORDER_v1.0`'s locked rule blocked any hand-edited `worker.js` from shipping until Phase-1 substrate (preview lane + validator + quality bar) existed — B-140/B-141/B-142 were already done, B-144 was the remaining piece. Three change-class Definition-of-Done tables, each a binary pass/fail checklist rather than a style opinion: **Worker Endpoint** (8 checks — auth-tagged, header-name column resolution per rule 6, `ensureColumns` before write per rule 37, standard `json({ok:false,...})` error shape, telemetry-logged, no N+1 Sheet/QB reads, idempotent-or-explicitly-not, `node --check` clean per rule 18); **Hub Screen** (6 checks — Status SSOT, one button system, real touch targets per UI_QA_CHECKLIST rules 132/134 incl. `claimSubmit` reuse, EN/ES parity on customer-facing surfaces, loading/empty/error states not just the happy path, no h-scroll at 390px); **Money Change** (7 checks — penny-correct with the `>0.005` tolerance convention, no double-post, reconcile-flags-never-auto-corrects invariant, correct-entity resolution before any QB write, the in-house-exclusion safety invariant, preview-first for customer-facing money moves, money-math factored into its own pure tested function). Every criterion traces to a real documented bug or an existing enforced pattern (grepped from worker.js and this file directly) — deliberately kept scorable, not aspirational. This is what `ridgeco-validate` and the future B-146 reviewer agent score against; B-149 (house-style consistency checks) is the automatable/greppable subset of the same rubric. Updated `BACKLOG.md` (both B-144 rows) and `CURRENT.md` (new file-registry row + status note) in the same commit. Committed locally, then **pushed to `main` (`ad02fb4b`) using a fresh classic PAT Brett supplied** (`BRETT_GH_PAT` was empty in this session's env — same gap that stalled B-127's push) — confirmed live via a fresh anonymous clone of `Ridge-Co/RidgeCo`, not just a local `git log`. No live-Sheet or live-Worker verification needed; this is a pure documentation deliverable with zero blast radius. **Real-world consequence:** the Phase-1 gate blocking B-127 (`routeAI`) and B-211 (`judge()`) deployment now has its last named prerequisite satisfied — the next real step for either is a live write path / call site, not more prerequisite-building, per the locked BUILD_ORDER. B-145 (golden-path tests) is still the remaining Phase-1→Phase-3 item, separate from this gate.

**117. Subject line and `AR_Report_Log` entry are both tagged as a test (`[TEST] ...` subject, `Trigger: manual-test`, `Result: sent (test)`) so the audit log never confuses a test send with a real one. `ar-report-admin.html` gained a "Test email" field above the customer list, threaded into `sendOne()`'s preview and send calls, with its own confirm-dialog wording so Brett can't mistake a test send for a real one mid-click. **Still blocked on the same prerequisite as rule 111**: nothing can send — test or real — until Brett finishes the Gmail OAuth setup (`GMAIL_CLIENT_ID/SECRET/REFRESH_TOKEN`); this only adds where the email goes once sending works. Verified: `node --check` clean, `ar-report-core` 14/14, full suite re-run clean (same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures at rule 100). **113. Inspection Scheduler — blackout date ranges + times, and quota-safe bulk property/unit import (Aug 18, 2026, Worker `2026-08-18.8` + `inspect.html`; B-226 follow-up to rule 110).** Brett, after confirming Phase 1 stuck: "add ability to bulk add dates in a batch and date ranges as well as time ranges (in the blackout dates bulk add). also need to be able to select from existing properties/tenants/units. i don't want to onboard hundreds of units manually. i will import them generally plus add one-off units as needed." Two changes: (1) **Blackout dates now support ranges + times in the same save.** `INSP_BLACKOUT_HEADERS` gained a `Date_End` column; `inspBlackoutAdd`'s `dates[]` array now accepts either a plain `'YYYY-MM-DD'` string (single day, unchanged) or an `{from,to}` object stored as ONE row (not exploded per-day) with a 731-day max-span guard and start/end validation. A shared `Start_Time`/`End_Time` now applies to the whole batch being saved, so "block Dec 24–26 all day, but only 1pm–5pm on the 31st" is two saves instead of Brett hand-adding a row per day with no time control (the old UI had no time fields on one-off dates at all). `inspect.html`'s blackout builder got a From/To range picker, a paste-a-batch textarea (comma/space/newline-separated dates, validated + deduped against the pending chip list before Save), and Start/End time inputs — chips render mixed single-dates and ranges, `fmtBlackout()` shows a range as "2026-12-24 → 2026-12-26". (2) **Bulk import for properties/units** — new `inspBulkImport(env, body)` + `POST /insp/bulk-import`, built specifically to avoid the quota trap from rule 99: reads `Insp_Properties`/`Insp_Units` ONCE each via a raw `sheetsRequest` (not `fetchTab` per row), groups the pasted rows by normalized Address into properties (matching existing ones, creating new ones only when the address is new), computes every new ID in-memory, dedupes units by a `Property+Label+(Phone|Name)` signature so re-pasting an overlapping batch is a no-op, and writes with at most 2 batched `:append` calls total — hundreds of rows costs a handful of Sheets API calls, not hundreds of them (max 2000 rows/call, enforced). `inspect.html` got a new "Bulk import properties/units" card under Properties & Units: paste a tab- or comma-separated block (Address/Zip/Unit_Label/Tenant_Name/Tenant_Phone/Visit_Duration_Min headers, naive-CSV quote handling), a client-side preview (rows found, property count, rows skipped for missing address) before any write happens, then Confirm chunks the import into batches of 150 rows/call with a progress indicator and reports back created/matched/duplicate-skipped counts. Also fixed the underlying scale problem Brett flagged ("select from existing… hundreds of units"): `loadProperties()` now makes ONE unfiltered `/insp/units` call and groups client-side instead of firing one `/insp/units?property_id=X` call per property (was N redundant full-tab reads); each property's unit list is now collapsed by default with a tap-to-expand count line instead of always rendering every unit inline; and a search box (address/zip substring) sits above the properties list so finding one property among hundreds doesn't mean scrolling. Verified: `node --check` clean on worker.js and the extracted `inspect.html` inline script; HTML tag-balance check (div/script/textarea/select) clean; router entry for `/insp/bulk-import` confirmed present (was written but not wired in the prior session before compaction — caught and fixed here). **Not yet run against live Sheets by Brett** — first check: open Inspection Scheduler, pick the new customer, paste a small test batch (2-3 addresses, one multifamily with 2 rows) into Bulk import, confirm the preview counts look right, confirm, then refresh and check the properties/units actually persisted with the search box finding them; separately add a one-off blackout with a date range + a time window and confirm it displays correctly and actually blocks that window.

**112. Fixed a real connection-starvation bug behind "Hub loads very slowly, no work orders" — api() timeouts now actually cancel the request (Aug 18, 2026; index.html `api()`/`loadAll()`/`_loadAllFallback()`).** Rule 106 added 20s timeouts to the data load, but Brett reported it was STILL slow — "very slowly, no work orders posted" — even after that shipped, while scope-creator.html (hitting the same Worker) worked fine. Root cause: rule 106's timeout used `apiWithTimeout()`, which only races a `Promise` against a timer — when the timer wins, the JS stops WAITING on the original `fetch()`, but never actually cancels it. On a bad connection, a "timed out" `/hub-bootstrap` request could still be quietly occupying one of the browser's ~6 concurrent connection slots to the Worker's origin when `_loadAllFallback()` immediately tried to fire 8 more requests at the same origin — the abandoned zombie request could starve the very fallback that was supposed to save the load, making a bad connection look like it hangs even LONGER than the 20s timeout implied, which matches "very slowly" better than a clean 20-40s failure would. Fixed: `api()` now takes an optional `timeoutMs` 4th argument and uses a real `AbortController` — when the timer fires, `controller.abort()` actually cancels the in-flight fetch, freeing its connection immediately for whatever tries next. `loadAll()` and `_loadAllFallback()` updated to pass `timeoutMs` directly instead of wrapping calls in `apiWithTimeout()`. Also added a safe, values-never-exposed pricing-config diagnostic to the public `/health` endpoint (`secret_set`/`sheet_set`/`parses_ok`) so a config problem (rule 109/111, Brett's real markup numbers) can be verified from a plain curl without needing the admin token — checked immediately after shipping and confirmed Brett's `PRICING_CONFIG` has NOT been saved anywhere yet (both `secret_set` and `sheet_set` are `false`), so that's still an open action item on his end, not a bug. Verified: `node --check` clean on worker.js and every inline `<script>` block in all 4 HTML files, post-merge with two concurrent sessions' unrelated features (Open Item Report / AR Report) that landed mid-fix. **Not yet confirmed live by Brett** — first check: open the Hub and confirm work orders load promptly; if it's ever still slow, it should now fail cleanly within ~20-40s with a clear retry message rather than hanging indefinitely.

**111. Weekly Open Item Report — automated + on-demand, sub-customer rollup, token-gated customer link (Aug 18, 2026, Worker `worker.js` new AR-report block + `wrangler.toml` new Monday cron + NEW `ar-report.html` + NEW `ar-report-admin.html` + `index.html` one Tools link; SHIPPED, DORMANT — no live send yet).** Brett wants open-item/AR-aging reports automated to clients weekly (skip anyone with nothing open), a manual by-customer trigger that rolls sub-customers up to the parent (his Goldszmidt example — several properties, one owner), and ideally a direct link to each invoice. Built: (1) **`buildArReportGroups`** (pure, tested — `test/ar-report-core.test.mjs`, 14 assertions) walks each open invoice's QuickBooks `ParentRef` chain to its top-level customer, combining every sub-customer's balance into one group per Owner — a $50 invoice on one property + $30 on another under the same owner correctly rolls up to one $80 group, not two separate $50/$30 groups each below threshold. **Eligibility = $75+ open OR oldest invoice open >10 days** (Brett's refinement after the initial ask), so a lingering small balance doesn't go unreported forever. (2) **Customer link reuses the Shareable Work Order's (B-117) exact token pattern** — `{scope:'ar-report', owner, rev}` HMAC off `WORKER_SECRET`, revoked by bumping a new `Owners.AR_Report_Rev` column — no new token-storage sheet tab needed. (3) **Pay-link security**: before acting, re-derives the invoice's own root customer from live QuickBooks and confirms it matches the token's owner, so one customer's link can never touch another's invoice by guessing an id. Prefers QuickBooks' own `InvoiceLink` field when present (unverified live yet — inconsistent across QBO accounts); falls back to triggering QuickBooks' own invoice resend (the exact mechanism `/ar/remind` already uses) so the customer always gets a real, working pay path either way. (4) **Real Gmail API send** (`gmailSendEmail`/`gmailAccessToken`, closes out B-210's internal-only scope for this use) — OAuth refresh-token flow, sends from `ridgecomaintenance@gmail.com`. Deliberately fails LOUD when unconfigured (not a silent stub like the digest email adapter) since this is a real customer-facing send. **DORMANT until Brett completes the one-time Gmail OAuth setup** (Worker secrets `GMAIL_CLIENT_ID/SECRET/REFRESH_TOKEN/SENDER`) — full brief in `context/AR_REPORT_BUILD_BRIEF_v1.0.md`. (5) New endpoints: `GET /ar/report/preview`, `POST /ar/report/send` (preview-first, same pattern as `/ar/remind`), `POST /ar/report/opt-in`, `POST /ar/report/revoke`, PUBLIC `GET /ar-report/view` + `POST /ar-report/pay-link`. (6) New weekly Cloudflare cron `0 13 * * 1` (Mon 9am EDT, an hour after the daily digest), gated by Config `ar_report_enabled` (default off — same dormant-by-design pattern as `digest_enabled`); reads new self-provisioning `AR_Report_OptIn` tab so nobody is auto-emailed until Brett checks them in. Every send attempt (success or failure) logs to new `AR_Report_Log`. (7) **`ar-report-admin.html`** (new Hub tool page, linked from 🧰 TOOLS → 💵 MONEY & QUICKBOOKS as 📋 Open Item Report): lists every customer with an open balance, eligibility + reason, a weekly-auto-send checkbox, and "Send now" (preview → confirm, mirrors the vendor-reconcile/receipt-reconciler tool-page pattern). **`ar-report.html`** (new customer-facing page, no login, styled to match `customer.html`): lists open invoices with amount/due date/linked WO description per invoice, Pay Now per invoice. Verified: `node --check` clean on worker.js + both new pages' inline scripts; full test suite 28/30 (same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures noted at rule 100 — `pricing-model`, `scope-core`). Landed via `git rebase` on top of a very active concurrent session (rules 104-110, turnover trigger through Inspection Scheduler) — re-verified `node --check` clean on worker.js post-merge, grepped for route/function-name collisions (none) since both sessions were adding new endpoints in the same file at the same time. **Nothing live yet — three things needed before any customer sees an email:** Brett's Gmail OAuth setup, a live check of the QuickBooks `InvoiceLink` field, and opting in the first customer (Goldszmidt) via `ar-report-admin.html`.

**110. Inspection Scheduler Phase 1 shipped — new venture line, data model + admin onboarding (Aug 18, 2026, Worker `2026-08-18.4` + new `inspect.html`; B-226).** New standalone page `inspect.html` (mh_auth-gated, same login as the rest of the Hub — reuses the shared access code, no new credential) for a brand-new PM customer's annual rental-property inspections (single-family + multifamily, batched per-tenant) plus a shared engine for the existing undocumented AMSCRE gig income (#527 in the Gemini archive). Full design in `context/INSPECTION_SCHEDULER_BUILD_BRIEF_v1.0.md` — researched Cal.com and Easy!Appointments as a free "Calendly backbone" and rejected both (neither runs on Cloudflare Workers; both need a second server + database outside the one-Worker/one-Sheet stack). Built natively instead, mirroring the Trash Service (rule 50-53) architecture exactly: 5 new self-provisioning tabs (`Insp_Customers`, `Insp_Properties`, `Insp_Units`, `Insp_Availability_Rules`, `Insp_Blackouts` — `ensureInspTabs`, one `:batchUpdate` for all 5, same pattern as `ensureTrashTabs`) and 14 new `/insp/*` endpoints, all behind the existing WORKER_SECRET gate (no PUBLIC_PATHS change this phase — outreach/booking-link auth comes in Phase 2). Phase 1 scope is deliberately just the data model + Brett's own onboarding screens, no outreach/SMS yet: **Customers** tab (name, rental-vs-AMSCRE line, contact); **Properties & Units** tab (pick a customer, add properties with zip + type + per-unit visit duration, add units per property with tenant name/phone, sortable by zip for route-grouping); **Availability** tab — default weekly hours (day + start/end) plus a **Blackouts** builder supporting three shapes in one model: one-off dates (multi-date chips added in a single save, so Brett can block a batch of days at once), recurring weekly (e.g. "nothing after 3pm on Fridays" = Fri + Start 15:00, End blank), and recurring annual (month+day, e.g. Christmas). Launcher added to Hub → Dev Log → 🧰 TOOLS. This build ran alongside a very active concurrent session shipping rules 107-109 (BUILD_VERSION process fix, scope-creator crash fix, pricing-engine extension) — merged cleanly via `git rebase` three separate times as the other session kept landing commits mid-merge; `BUILD_VERSION` bumped once more to `2026-08-18.4` to cover all of it, re-verified `node --check` clean on `worker.js` post-merge plus `inspect.html`'s inline script each time; grepped for duplicate/colliding routes (none) and confirmed every new endpoint resolves to a real function. **Not yet run against live Sheets by Brett** — first check: open Inspection Scheduler from Dev Log → Tools, log in with the Hub access code, add a test customer, add a multifamily property with 2 units, add a weekly availability rule and a one-off blackout date, refresh and confirm everything persisted (this also proves the 5 new tabs self-provisioned correctly on the live RidgeCo Main sheet). **Next (Phase 2):** the actual window/slot engine, `Start Outreach`, and the public `inspect-book.html` self-serve booking page.

**109. Pricing engine extended for Brett's actual markup model — per-tier minimum + conditional admin fee (Aug 18, 2026; worker.js `calcTieredEstimate`/`getPricingConfig`, index.html mirror).** Rule 108 fixed the crash, but the real pricing had never been set (dormant since rule 91, Aug 13) and the existing config schema couldn't express two things Brett's real model needs: (1) a per-tier DOLLAR FLOOR on markup — 35% on jobs up to $1,000, but never less than $50 — the old formula was pure percentage with no floor concept; (2) the $85 admin fee only applying to jobs $3,000 and up, not every job — the old formula added `adminFee` unconditionally. Extended the tier format from `[maxCost, pct]` to `[maxCost, pct, minMarkupAmt?]` (3rd element optional, defaults to 0 — fully backward compatible with any 2-element tier) and added a top-level `adminFeeThreshold` (defaults to 0 = "always", same as old behavior). New formula: `markup = MAX(cost × pct, minMarkupAmt)`, `admin = cost >= adminFeeThreshold ? adminFee : 0`, everything else (card fee multiplier applied last so processing is baked into the total and never itemized, round-up-to-`roundTo`) unchanged. index.html's `calcTieredEstimate` mirror updated to match EXACTLY — these two must never drift. Brett's actual config, verified against 6 spot-check dollar amounts by hand-running both formulas: `{"tiers":[[1000,0.35,50],[2000,0.30,0],[null,0.25,0]],"adminFee":85,"adminFeeThreshold":3000,"cardFeeMult":1.05,"roundTo":5}` — e.g. a $100 job prices at $160 (the $50 floor kicks in, not $35), a $3,500 job prices at $4,685 (25% + $85 admin + 5% processing, rounded up). Itemized/on-site hourly fields intentionally left unset for now (Brett only gave tiered-markup numbers) — those suggestion paths stay dormant (\$0) until he provides those too, same as before, no regression. **🔴 Still needs Brett to actually set the config** — nothing above changes anything live until the JSON above is saved as either the Cloudflare Worker secret `PRICING_CONFIG` or the Config-sheet row `pricing_config`; recommended the Cloudflare secret since it never touches the shared Sheet. Verified: `node --check` clean on worker.js + every inline `<script>` block in all 4 HTML files; manually re-ran the formula in Node for cost=100/200/1500/2500/3500/5000 and confirmed each matches Brett's stated rule by hand.

**108. Fixed scope-creator "Generate proposal" — always crashed, `calcTieredEstimate()` called with a missing arg (Aug 18, 2026; worker.js `scopeProposal`).** Brett hit "⚠ Cannot read properties of null (reading 'finalPrice')" every time he tapped "Generate proposal from scope + estimate" on scope-creator.html. Root cause: `scopeProposal()` called `calcTieredEstimate(estAmt)` with only ONE argument — the function's own signature is `calcTieredEstimate(rawCost, pc)` and it deliberately `return`s `null` whenever `pc` (the pricing config) is missing (line right above: "Pure, config-driven — no constants baked in. Returns null if pricing isn't configured."). Since `pc` was never passed at all, it was `undefined` on literally every call, so `pricing` was always `null`, and the very next line's `pricing.finalPrice` threw — this endpoint could never have worked, not a regression from anything shipped today. `generateEstimateText()` a few hundred lines down has the correct pattern (`await getPricingConfig(env)`, guard for missing config, then pass it in) — `scopeProposal()` now mirrors that exactly: fetches the pricing config first, returns a clear 400 ("Pricing not configured...") if it's genuinely unset, otherwise passes it to `calcTieredEstimate` so `pricing` is a real object. Verified: `node --check` clean on worker.js. **Not yet confirmed live by Brett** — first check: on scope-creator.html, approve a scope, save a vendor estimate, tap "Generate proposal from scope + estimate," confirm a real proposal with a dollar total and deposit appears instead of the null error.

**107. ⚠️ PROCESS BUG FOUND — BUILD_VERSION never bumped across rules 101/102/103/105/106, so no client could self-detect any of today's frontend fixes (Aug 18, 2026; worker.js `BUILD_VERSION`).** Brett confirmed rule 106's fix was live (per my own curl checks against GitHub Pages), then reported it was "exactly the same" — Hub loads, menu shows, but data never loads, same as before the fix supposedly shipped. Root cause: this file has a documented convention — "BUILD_VERSION: bumped on every deploy that changes the Worker OR any portal" — that every one of today's five frontend-only deploys (101, 102, 103, 105, 106) violated; `BUILD_VERSION` sat at `2026-08-17.6` all day. The client-side auto-refresh mechanism (B-093/rule 101/103) exists specifically to catch already-open or HTTP-cached tabs up to a new deploy, but it works ONLY by comparing this string — if it never changes, an already-loaded or GitHub-Pages-cached (max-age 600s) client has literally no signal that anything shipped, no matter how many commits landed. This means Brett's device may well have been running JS from several fixes ago the entire time I was verifying "it's live" against my own fresh curl fetch (different cache/edge than his phone). Fix: bumped `BUILD_VERSION` to `2026-08-18.1`, confirmed live via `/version`. Any client currently running ANY of today's poll-script versions (all of them have it, since rule 101) will now detect the mismatch on its next 60s interval tick or foreground/focus event and self-heal via `reloadFresh()` (or the tap-to-refresh banner if something looks unsaved) — no action needed from Brett beyond giving it up to ~60s, or backgrounding/foregrounding the Chrome tab to force the check immediately. **Going forward: every deploy that touches index.html/vendor.html/tenant.html/owner.html/worker.js MUST bump `BUILD_VERSION`, no exceptions** — this is now the top thing to check before calling any frontend fix "shipped." Not yet confirmed live by Brett — first check: within a minute or two, the Hub should either auto-reload on its own or show "Update ready — tap to refresh"; after that, work orders should load normally.

**106. Hub data load hardened — 20s timeouts, no more infinite spinner (Aug 18, 2026, GitHub Pages only; index.html `loadAll()`/`_loadAllFallback()`).** After rule 105 fixed login, Brett got in fine but then reported the Work Orders list sitting on its "Loading..." spinner for a full minute with nothing ever appearing, even after his wifi issue was resolved. Root cause: `loadAll()`'s `/hub-bootstrap` call (and each of the 8 per-tab calls in its fallback path) was a bare `fetch()` with no timeout at all — on a degraded mobile connection a stalled TCP connection can sit open far longer than anyone will wait, and since the promise never rejects, the spinner had no reason to ever go away. Same failure shape as rule 105's login bug: not an error, just silence. Fix: wrapped the `/hub-bootstrap` call in the existing `apiWithTimeout()` helper (20s) — a stall now surfaces on schedule and automatically falls through to the 8-separate-request fallback, which is now ALSO individually timed per call (20s each) so one stuck tab can't hang the rest. If literally everything still fails (e.g. the connection is genuinely down), the spinners are replaced with a plain-language "Couldn't load data — check your connection" message plus a tappable Retry link, instead of an unexplained infinite spinner. Verified: `node --check` clean on every inline `<script>` block in all 4 files + `worker.js`. **Not yet confirmed live by Brett** — first check: open the Hub on a normal connection and confirm work orders still load promptly; if a load ever does stall again, confirm a clear message + Retry link appears within ~20s instead of a silent spinner.

**105. Hub login hardened — visible feedback + fixed stale-cache race (Aug 18, 2026, GitHub Pages only; index.html `doLogin()`).** Rule 103 fixed the regression where login went dead from the head-redirect, but Brett then ran a clean controlled test (tap Enter once, wait 5s, no refreshing) and reported "nothing" — confirming a SECOND, pre-existing bug independent of rule 101/103. Root cause was two real bugs in `doLogin()` itself: (1) zero visual feedback between tapping Enter and any outcome — if the request was slow, or any unexpected exception fired before reaching `.catch()`, the screen sat exactly as it was, indistinguishable from the tap never registering at all, which is exactly what was reported both times. (2) `api()`'s GET de-dup cache (`_getCache`, 3s TTL) is keyed by path only (`/config`), not by which `AUTH_TOKEN` was active — the auto-login-on-load path (using any previously-stored token) also hits `/config` on every fresh page load, so a manual login typed within 3 seconds of that auto-attempt could silently be handed back the STORED token's cached result instead of making its own fresh request with what was just typed. Fix: `doLogin()` now shows "Checking…" the instant Enter is tapped or the button is pressed, clears `_getCache` before its own request so it always fires fresh, wraps the whole function in try/catch, and adds an 8-second timeout — so every outcome (success, "Incorrect access code" with any extra server message, a timeout warning, or a specific JS error) now shows real text in `#login-error` instead of a silent nothing. Landed in the same commit as a concurrent session's unrelated Turnover trigger + WO bulk actions work (rule 104) — merged cleanly via `git merge`, re-verified with `node --check` on every inline `<script>` block across all 4 files plus `worker.js` post-merge, confirmed `doLogin()` came through the merge unchanged. **Not yet confirmed live by Brett** — first check: open the Hub fresh, type the access code, tap Enter once, and report EXACTLY what text appears (should never again be silent/nothing).

**104. Turnover trigger (B-100) + expanded WO bulk actions bar (Aug 18, 2026; worker.js `createTurnoverWOs`/`startTurnoverManual`/`scheduleMoveOutWithTurnover`/`releaseTurnoverCleaningIfReady`/`releaseTurnoverByDate`, new `POST /turnover/start` + `POST /tenant/schedule-move-out`, hook in `updateStatus` + daily `scheduled()` cron; index.html Work Orders bulk bar + Unit Detail "Start Turnover" + tenant "Schedule Move-Out"; new `test/turnover.test.mjs`, 30 assertions).** Brett asked for three things in one session. (1) **Turnover trigger** — a standard turnover is 3 connected jobs: Repairs, Cleaning, Paint. Per Brett: Repairs + Paint should run in PARALLEL (open immediately, vendors can be lined up ahead of time — his stated pain point is knee-jerk, last-minute turnovers that are "almost impossible to get everything scheduled"), but Cleaning must wait until both finish, OR until the day before the target move-in date, whichever comes first. Built as `createTurnoverWOs` — reuses `createWorkOrder()` for the actual WO creation (single source of truth, PAT-001) rather than re-deriving ID assignment/tenant-linking/telemetry, then stamps 4 new `Work_Orders` columns (`Turnover_Group_ID`, `Turnover_Role`, `Turnover_Release_Date`, plus `Hold_Reason` which didn't exist on this tab yet — the B-127 notes model that would have added it never merged, so it's created here via `ensureColumns`). Cleaning starts life `Status:'On Hold'` with a human-readable `Hold_Reason`. Two ways to trigger it: **"Start Turnover"** button (new, on Unit Detail — manual, works on any unit) and **"Schedule Move-Out"** (new, on each tenant — books a FUTURE move-out date and starts the turnover immediately for lead time, but deliberately does **NOT** call the existing `/tenant/move-out` — the tenant stays `Active`, keeps their PIN and portal access right up to the real day; only the destructive red "Move Out" button does that). Idempotent — a unit with an active (non-done) turnover group rejects a second `/turnover/start` (409) rather than stacking jobs. Release happens two ways: `updateStatus` now checks, whenever a WO carrying a `Turnover_Group_ID` goes `Complete`, whether its non-Cleaning siblings are ALL done (`releaseTurnoverCleaningIfReady`) and flips Cleaning from On Hold to New if so; separately the daily 11:00 UTC cron now also sweeps for any Cleaning WO whose `Turnover_Release_Date` has arrived (`releaseTurnoverByDate`) — the "whichever comes first" date fallback, so a late vendor on Repairs/Paint can't indefinitely block a cleaner who has to be in before a new tenant's move-in. WO cards/detail show a `🔄 TO-####` badge; the detail's Status row surfaces the Hold_Reason inline instead of a bare "On Hold" with no explanation. (2) **WO bulk actions bar** — the existing "☑ Bulk Edit" toggle only ever did bulk status change; Brett wanted reassign, tenant-visible toggle, tenant-notify toggle, and priority added, explicitly NOT everything ("shouldn't be able to do everything there"). Added four more rows to the same bar: Reassign (one vendor applied to the whole selected batch via the normal `/assign` dispatch-text flow), Priority (urgent/normal/low via `/wo/admin-update`), and two Tenant toggles (Show/Hide via `Tenant_Visible`, notify On/Off via `Tenant_Notify_Updates`, both through the existing `/wo/set-tenant-visibility`) — every action is a thin `Promise.all` loop over the SAME already-tested single-item endpoints the one-at-a-time UI uses, no new bulk-write endpoints, so there's no second copy of any business logic to drift. Per-item errors in a batch are surfaced (`"Updated 4/5 — 1 failed: ..."`) instead of one failure silently swallowing the whole batch's result. Brett specifically flagged bulk-Cancel as needing "double protection" — it's the one destructive bulk action — so selecting Cancelled in the status row no longer runs the plain `confirm()` the other statuses use; it opens a dedicated modal requiring BOTH a checkbox ("I understand this cancels every selected work order") AND typing the literal word CANCEL before the confirm button even enables. (3) **Review Bills multi-select** — Brett asked for this same session but it turned out to already be live (rule 98, Aug 17); no work needed, just confirmed and pointed him at the existing "☑ Select multiple" toggle. Verified: `node --check` clean on worker.js and every inline `<script>` block in index.html (5, after upstream rules 101-103 landed mid-session — rebased cleanly, re-verified post-rebase), new `test/turnover.test.mjs` (30 assertions: 3-WO creation + shared group id + correct trades, Cleaning's On Hold start + Hold_Reason + day-before release date math, idempotency reject-then-allow-once-done, release requires BOTH siblings not just one, date-fallback sweep releases even with open siblings but leaves a future date alone, `scheduleMoveOutWithTurnover` books the date without touching Active/PIN), full suite 27/29 (same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures — `pricing-model`, `scope-core`). **Not yet used live** — first check: on a unit, tap "🔄 Start Turnover" with a target move-in date a few days out, confirm 3 WOs appear (Repairs + Paint open, Cleaning On Hold with a reason and the right release date); mark Repairs and Paint Complete and confirm Cleaning flips to New automatically; separately try "📅 Schedule Move-Out" on an active tenant and confirm they're NOT deactivated (PIN/portal still work) while the turnover WOs appear; on Work Orders, tap "☑ Bulk Edit," select a few WOs, and try Reassign / Priority / tenant Show-Hide / tenant notify On-Off, then try bulk Cancel and confirm the checkbox+typed-word gate actually blocks the button until both are satisfied.
**103. ⚠️ REGRESSION FIXED — rule 101's synchronous force-refresh broke the Hub login (Aug 18, 2026, GitHub Pages only; index.html + vendor.html `<head>`, all 4 files' tail version-poll).** Brett: typed his access code on the Hub, tapped Enter, nothing happened at all — no error, no reaction — even after a pull-to-refresh and a browser refresh. This was a regression from rule 101 (same day, earlier). Root cause: rule 101 put a **synchronous** redirect at the very top of `<head>` in index.html and vendor.html — on literally every open, before a single byte of the rest of the page rendered, it appended a cache-bust `_hr` param and forced a full network re-fetch of the ENTIRE file. index.html is ~9,900 lines; the login markup sits near the top, but `doLogin()`/`api()` are defined much further down inside one large inline `<script>`. Forcing a full re-fetch on **every** open (instead of letting the browser use its normal cache the vast majority of the time) is exactly the kind of load a flaky mobile connection can stall or cut short mid-download — the browser renders whatever HTML arrived (the login box, near the top) and the script further down that defines `doLogin()` simply never finishes loading or executing. Net effect: a visually normal, fully-rendered login screen whose button does nothing — zero error, because the code that would even show an error hadn't loaded either. This exactly matches Brett's report, and explains why a manual refresh didn't fix it: a refresh doesn't help if the SAME oversized synchronous fetch stalls again on the same weak connection. Only index.html and vendor.html carried this risk — rule 101 only ever added the head-level version to those two; tenant.html/owner.html only got the softer tail-only poll and were never at risk. **Fix:** (1) removed the synchronous head-of-page redirect entirely from both files — `<head>` now only keeps the no-cache meta tags + a `pageshow`/bfcache guard (safe, since bfcache only holds a page that already finished loading successfully once). (2) Moved the actual staleness check to the END of `<body>`, i.e. strictly AFTER the page has already loaded and run — so a forced reload can never be the thing standing between Brett and a working page. The first `check()` call now also compares the live `/version` against a value persisted in `localStorage` (`rc_build_ver`) from the last successful visit; if they differ, a real new deploy happened since Brett was last on it, and only THEN (after this load already proved the connection works) does it do a cache-busted reload (idle) or show the tap-to-refresh banner (mid-entry, existing `dirty()` check). Applied the same safer tail pattern to all 4 files for consistency. **Net effect: Brett still never gets stuck on a stale deploy (rule 101's original goal), but getting there no longer has to win a race against a flaky mobile connection just to let today's page load at all.** Verified: `node --check` clean on every inline `<script>` block in all 4 files (index 5, vendor 7, tenant 3, owner 4). **Not yet confirmed live by Brett** — first check: open the Hub fresh, type the access code, tap Enter, confirm it logs in normally; do this a few times including right after switching apps/foregrounding, to rule out a repeat.


**102. Voice-to-text on every text block (Hub + all 3 portals) + vendor Description field justified/larger (Aug 18, 2026, GitHub Pages only — no Worker change; index.html + vendor.html + tenant.html + owner.html).** Brett: wants voice-to-text on any description/text block "now and going forward" in the Hub and every portal, plus the vendor Description text is right-aligned and needs justified + one size larger. (1) **Voice-to-text** — a self-attaching 🎤 button (Web Speech API `SpeechRecognition`) added identically to all 4 files: scans every `<textarea>` on load AND watches for any added later via `MutationObserver` (modals, dynamically-built forms — e.g. vendor.html's per-item estimate/receipt/notes textareas that only exist after a render call), so anything rendered by JS after page load gets the mic with zero extra wiring — this is what makes it "going forward" rather than a one-time pass over today's fields. Feature-detected (`window.SpeechRecognition || window.webkitSpeechRecognition`); on browsers without support (iOS Safari has none) the whole block no-ops and textareas work exactly as before — never blocks typing. Tap mic → speak → tap again to stop; final transcript appends to whatever was already typed (`base + ' ' + transcript`, whitespace-guarded) and dispatches a real `input` event so existing `oninput` handlers (e.g. the estimate-item live-resize handler at vendor.html:668) still fire. Each textarea gets wrapped in its own small positioning `<span class="rc-voice-wrap">` at attach time — verified first that no file has a direct-child `textarea` CSS selector (only tag selectors `textarea{...}`/`input,select,textarea{...}`), so the extra wrapper doesn't break any existing styling. (2) **vendor.html Description field** — the WO "Issue" value was sharing `.detail-val`, the same `text-align:right;font-size:12px` class used for short label:value pairs (address, phone, scheduled date) where right-alignment reads fine; for a paragraph of job description it read as a ragged right-flush block. Left `.detail-val` itself untouched (still correct for the short fields) and added an inline override on just that one span: `text-align:justify;font-size:13px`. Verified: `node --check` clean on every inline `<script>` block in all 4 files (index 5, vendor 7, tenant 3, owner 4 — one more each than last count, from the new script block). Purely additive except the one Description-field style change. **Not yet used live** — first check: open any portal, tap into a textarea (WO notes, vendor bill notes, estimate item description, tenant/owner contact form, anything), confirm a 🎤 appears, tap it, speak, confirm the words land in the field; open a WO in the vendor portal with a multi-line Description and confirm it now reads justified and slightly larger instead of ragged-right.


**101. Force hard refresh on open, both index.html and vendor.html (Aug 18, 2026, GitHub Pages only — no Worker change, no version bump; index.html + vendor.html `<head>` + end-of-body scripts).** Brett: "still don't have access to the site" + wants both the Hub and the vendor site to force a hard refresh every time they're opened — flagged as recurring and urgent. Root cause: GitHub Pages sends no custom cache-control headers, so a browser can serve a fully-cached copy of `index.html`/`vendor.html` indefinitely with **zero network check**, meaning "not having access" was very plausibly a stale cached page running old/broken JS, not an actual auth failure. vendor.html already had a soft live-poll (`/version` every 60s, auto-reload if idle / banner if mid-entry — B-093) but nothing forced a fresh fetch on the **initial** open, and **index.html had no such mechanism at all**. Fixed both, purely additive: (1) **`<head>` no-cache meta tags** (`Cache-Control`/`Pragma`/`Expires`) as a first line of defense. (2) **Synchronous cache-bust redirect** — the very first script in `<head>`, before anything else parses: on load it checks for a `_hr` (timestamp) query param; if missing, or older than 3s (a stale bookmarked link), it appends a fresh one and `location.replace()`s — a different URL was never cached, so the browser is forced to fetch the current deploy from the network. Every existing query param (`index.html?page=`/`?wo=`, etc.) is preserved via `URLSearchParams`. Fires exactly once per real navigation (the reload's own `_hr` is <3s old, so it doesn't loop). (3) **`pageshow` bfcache guard** — mobile app-switch-back can restore the page from memory with no network activity at all; `e.persisted` forces a real reload. (4) **index.html gained vendor.html's B-093 live-poll** (it never had one) — `/version` polled every 60s, auto-reload if idle, tap-to-refresh banner if a form looks mid-entry (checked via `document.activeElement` + `[data-unsaved="1"]`, same dirty-check vendor.html uses). (5) **Both files now also check immediately on `visibilitychange`/`focus`**, not just the 60s interval, so a backgrounded tab/app picks up a new deploy the moment Brett foregrounds it again, not up to a minute later. **Nothing in the money/auth/data path touched** — this is entirely load-time/cache behavior in the two frontend files; `worker.js` is unchanged, so no `BUILD_VERSION` bump and no Worker redeploy. Verified: `node --check` clean on every inline `<script>` block in both files (4 in index.html, 6 in vendor.html) — extracted and checked individually, all pass. **Not yet confirmed live** — first check: open the Hub and vendor portal fresh (note the URL picks up a `?_hr=...` param — expected, harmless) and confirm they load; ship a trivial next deploy and confirm an already-open tab either auto-reloads or shows the "Update ready" banner within a few seconds of switching back to it, not up to a minute later.

**100. Per-code access visibility ("Brett Only") + broadened lockbox/code type map — 828 S Charles St's electronic door code now shows on its WO (Aug 17, Worker `2026-08-17.7`; worker.js `getWOLockboxes`/`enrichWO`/`updateKeyWithHistory`; index.html WO-detail access-code widget + `setKeyVisibility`; new `Keys.Visibility` column).** Brett: "Electronic door code for 828 S Charles St not appearing on work order in hub at all... need to be able to share access no matter what type... for this one in particular it should only be viewable for me but it should be viewable on the work order assigned to me." Two separate bugs, both real. (1) `getWOLockboxes`'s `TYPE_MAP` only recognized a handful of legacy `Key_Type` strings (`Lockbox`, `Front Door`, etc.); a code saved under the current vocabulary (`Building-FrontDoorCode`, `Unit-DoorCode`, ...) fell through unlabeled, AND the admin Hub's WO-detail widget separately filtered by the single literal string `'Lockbox'` — so anything else, electronic codes included, was invisible in the Hub twice over, not just mislabeled. `TYPE_MAP` now covers both vocabularies; the widget renders every active key/code keyed by type (`KEY_TYPE_GROUP`/`keyTypeLabel`, shared with `renderKeysView` so there's one source of truth, not two), not just ones literally typed `Lockbox`. (2) There was no way to mark an individual code — independent of its type — as never shown to a vendor; visibility only existed as an all-or-nothing per-WO `Vendor_Needs_Access` gate. Added `Keys.Visibility` (blank = Auto, or `Brett Only`) with a per-code dropdown right in the WO-detail "ACCESS CODES (live)" widget (`setKeyVisibility` → `POST /key/update`), and `enrichWO` now filters `Brett Only` codes out of every vendor/tenant/owner/shared-link view UNCONDITIONALLY (not gated behind `opts.vendorView` — admin never calls `enrichWO` at all, so this can only ever affect non-admin views) — **with one exception**: if the WO's assigned vendor is Brett's own in-house record (`Vendors.In_House === 'TRUE'`), nothing is hidden, since that "vendor" is Brett. This is a two-state control (Auto / Brett Only), not three — a "Vendor" middle option was sketched then dropped because it wouldn't behave any differently from Auto given how the filter actually composes with the existing all-or-nothing access gate. **Brett's own to-do:** the code fix makes 828 S Charles St's electronic code VISIBLE in the Hub now, but marking that *specific* code "Brett Only" is a one-tap action on the new dropdown — not something this session could set programmatically without Sheets write access to that exact row. Verified: `node --check` clean, `test/access-code-visibility.test.mjs` (12 assertions: broadened TYPE_MAP incl. no-regression on legacy `Lockbox` + unknown-type fallback, per-code visibility passthrough, `enrichWO` source-guard checks for the filter + in-house exception), full suite 26/28 (same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures — `pricing-model`, `scope-core`). **Not yet used live** — first check: open 828 S Charles St's WO in the Hub and confirm the electronic door code now shows in "ACCESS CODES (live)"; set it to "Brett Only" via the dropdown and confirm it disappears from that WO's vendor-portal view (unless the assigned vendor is an in-house record) while staying visible in the Hub.

**99. Sheets API quota fix — "Quota exceeded... Read requests per minute per user" during normal same-session use (Aug 17, Worker `2026-08-17.7`; worker.js `sheetsRequest`/`fetchTabs`/`getAccessToken`/new `GET /hub-bootstrap`/new in-isolate tab cache; index.html `loadAll`).** Brett hit the Sheets 429 error mid-session, correctly pushing back that he "wasn't moving that fast" — the real cause wasn't Brett's pace, it's that every Hub screen/action fires several serial reads and the whole app (admin, vendor, tenant, owner, crons) authenticates as ONE shared Google service account, so all of it shares a single 60-reads/minute bucket. Five changes, additive, no behavior change when nothing is under load: (1) `sheetsRequest` now retries a 429 (any HTTP method — a rate-limited call never applied, so it's always safe to resend) and a 5xx (**GET only**, never POST, since a 500 on a write may have landed and retrying could double-append a bill/row) with backoff, instead of throwing straight to the screen. (2) `fetchTabs` batches many tabs into ONE `values:batchGet` instead of one `values.get` per tab — a screen that used to cost 5 reads now costs 1. (3) `getAccessToken` caches the hour-long OAuth token instead of minting a fresh one on every single request. (4) New `GET /hub-bootstrap` returns the Hub's 8 core tabs (Work_Orders, Properties, Units, Tenants, Vendors, Owners, Keys, Config) in one call; `index.html`'s `loadAll()` uses it first, with the original 8-separate-calls path kept as an automatic fallback if the bootstrap call itself fails for any reason. (5) A short-lived (6s) in-isolate `Map` cache absorbs duplicate reads of the same tab within one burst (e.g. two widgets on the same screen both wanting Work_Orders) — best-effort, not cross-isolate-guaranteed (no KV/Durable Object provisioned), invalidated immediately on any write to that tab. Also: the front-end `api()` helper got its own independent 3-second GET de-dup cache (cleared on any POST), so a screen re-rendering twice in quick succession doesn't double-fire the same request before the Worker-side cache even helps. Verified: `node --check` clean, `test/read-layer.test.mjs` (retry/backoff/method-safety, batchGet shape/order, token caching) and `test/invoice-review-bulk.test.mjs`'s write-call-count assertions still pass against the new cache layer, full suite 26/28 (same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures). **Not yet used live** — first check: work through several WOs back-to-back the way Brett was when this hit, and confirm no 429 surfaces; if the Hub ever does still hit one, it should now visibly retry and recover rather than erroring on screen.

**98. Review Bills bulk-approve — select several bills, approve them together, no per-item refresh (Aug 17, Worker `2026-08-17.7`; worker.js `approveInvoiceReviewBulk`, new `POST /invoice-review/approve-bulk`; index.html Review Bills page, `invBuildApprovalPayload`/`irBulkApprove`/`irToggleBulkMode`/`irHideCard`).** Brett: "need to select multiple items for review bills, not one at a time then refresh after each one." Two things were actually happening: there was no multi-select at all, AND an approved card didn't visibly leave the queue until a manual refresh (the bill really was gone server-side — `?status=submitted` already excludes it — the screen just didn't know yet). Fixed both. `invBillThisJob`'s validation/pricing math was extracted into `invBuildApprovalPayload(k, opts)` — same function the single-card approve button now calls — so the new bulk path uses **the identical money math**, not a second hand-written copy that could quietly drift from it. Review Bills gained a "☑ Select multiple" toggle, a sticky selection bar (count + running $ total), and "Approve selected," which builds a payload per checked card, silently skips any that are below-cost (those need their own explicit lose-money confirmation — firing N `confirm()` dialogs back to back in a batch was judged a worse experience than asking Brett to approve those few individually) or already-approved, shows one confirm dialog with the batch total, then sends the whole batch in **one** `POST /invoice-review/approve-bulk` call. The new Worker endpoint does one read of Vendor_Bills + one read of Invoice_Review, loops in memory, and issues exactly one `batchUpdate` + one `append` regardless of batch size (capped at 50) — not a read/write pair per bill. Every approved card fades out of the list immediately (`irHideCard`), which is the direct fix for "doesn't leave the list until I refresh," for both the single-card and bulk paths. **Deliberately out of scope:** bulk-approve does NOT also push to QuickBooks in bulk — it only moves a bill from "submitted" to "approved, awaiting QuickBooks," same as today's single approve; the existing preview-first, one-at-a-time-confirmed Send-to-QB screen (money actually leaving the building) is untouched.
