## STANDALONE-PRICING DISCLAIMER — locked policy, baked into every proposal (Aug 21, 2026, Worker `2026-08-21.7`, commit pending)

**128. New standing disclaimer on partial-scope/standalone pricing, added to both proposal-generation templates.** Brett: "if the overall quote is declined in favor of selecting only smaller items, the estimates are only a best efforts estimate and may change on the final price because the larger estimate is built in a way to absorb the unknowns of the smaller [items] as well as the larger tasks together... I want to make sure it's clear that price might not even be accurate when we're done with that smaller job because the larger overall estimate was not accepted[, which] would have absorbed all the unknowns." Two-part ask, confirmed in conversation: (1) cherry-picked items should already cost more than their share of a combined job (Problem 1, resolved earlier this session with the tapering-trip-charge standalone model), and (2) even that higher standalone number is only a best-efforts estimate, not guaranteed, because the combined price is what actually absorbs the small unknowns of mixing larger and smaller tasks in one visit — a lone item doesn't get that cushion. Wrote the concise disclaimer, confirmed the exact wording with Brett before shipping it everywhere: *"If only part of this scope is approved or completed instead of the full project, pricing for those individual items is a best-efforts estimate, not a fixed quote: the combined price is built to absorb the small unknowns of doing larger and smaller tasks together in one visit, and a standalone item doesn't get that same cushion — its actual final cost may run higher than estimated once that work is underway on its own."* Added as a new terms bullet in THREE places: (a) Gladden's live `Proposal_Text` directly (`/scope/update`, verified the $4,950 total and existing text were otherwise untouched), (b) `scopeProposal()`'s doc template (worker.js) — every future Scope-based proposal gets this automatically, regardless of whether a standalone comparison is actually shown (harmless boilerplate when it isn't), (c) `generateEstimateText()`'s doc template (worker.js) — the older, separate WO-based estimate-text generator (`POST /generate-estimate-text`, still live from index.html) gets the same clause via a new `partialScopeClauseText` constant, appended before the existing (unrelated) `integrityClauseText`. **Deliberately kept distinct from the older Estimate Integrity Clause** (15% adjustment + $150 travel fee, fires only when items are dropped from an already-multi-versioned WO estimate) — that's a punitive re-pricing clause for a narrower scenario; this new one is a universal best-efforts caveat that applies to any proposal, Scope- or WO-based, version history or not. Documented as **LOCKED policy** in the private `brett332/data` repo's `billing-model.md` (Problem 1 + Problem 2 framing, the exact disclaimer text, and how it relates to the older Integrity Clause) so it's the authoritative reference for any future proposal work. `node --check` clean on worker.js; full suite re-run: same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures (`pricing-model`, `scope-core`), nothing newly broken; `scope-variants`/`scope-book` still 13/13 and 11/11 (this change doesn't touch either's pure helpers, only the doc-string templates).

## BULK IMPORTER — St/Saint/Street address folding (Aug 21, 2026, Worker `2026-08-21.8`)

**129. Fixed: the shared Bulk Importer (rule 122) couldn't recognize 931 Saint Paul St as an existing property — "St" vs "Saint" address-abbreviation mismatch, not a matching-logic bug.** (Renumbered from 127 to 129 on rebase — landed on top of this same day's rules 127-128 above, Gladden un-gated/editable proposal text + the standalone-pricing disclaimer; one `BUILD_VERSION` conflict, `.6` vs `.7`, resolved to `.8`, no route/function collisions.) Brett's first live check of rule 122 (this same day): pasted a CSV for 931 St. Paul St. + 1305 N Calvert St., but the importer proposed 13 brand-new units instead of recognizing 6 that were already on file (1F/1R/2F/2R/BF/BR, added Aug 11 per `context/sheet-ops/completed/ops_20260811_131304.json`). Traced it, not guessed: `hubBulkImport`'s address matching is logically correct (property by normalized address, unit by property+normalized label) — but its normalizer only stripped punctuation/whitespace, and Property 70's Address on file is "**931 Saint Paul St**" (spelled out) while the CSV said "**931 St. Paul St.**" (abbreviated) — "st paul" vs "saint paul" never matched, so the importer treated an existing property as brand new and every unit under it as new too. Brett confirmed via the Hub ("hub says 931 Saint Paul St") before anything shipped, and flagged this specific ambiguity will recur — Saint Paul is a common Baltimore street/neighborhood name, and any "St ___" address is exposed to the same abbreviation collision (Street vs Saint). **Fixed at the root, not just the symptom:** worker.js already had a more capable, tested address normalizer for this exact problem — `qbNormAddress`/`QB_ADDR_WORDS` (built for QuickBooks customer matching, already folds Street/Ave/Rd/Blvd/N-S-E-W abbreviations) — it was one dictionary entry short of covering "Saint." Added `saint: 'st'` to `QB_ADDR_WORDS` (one line) instead of writing a second normalizer, so the fix applies everywhere that dictionary is used, not just the bulk importer. `hubBulkImport` and `inspBulkImport` each had their own separate, weaker, hand-rolled `normAddr` (punctuation/case only, no abbreviation folding) — replaced both with `const normAddr = qbNormAddress;`, single source of truth per PAT-001, so this class of bug can't recur in either importer and any future QB_ADDR_WORDS improvement benefits both automatically. **The "help" Brett asked for** (visibility, not just silent correctness): added `properties_matched_via_address_normalization` to the preview response — when a property match only worked because of the St/Saint/Street folding (detected via a separate `normAddrStrict`, punctuation-only, never used for the actual match decision — only to notice when folding was the deciding factor), `bulk-importer.html` now shows a green "✓ matched an existing property whose address was typed differently" banner with a pasted-vs-matched address table, so Brett gets a one-glance confirmation instead of a silent fuzzy match. New `test/bulk-import-address.test.mjs` (11 assertions, run against the real extracted worker.js source, same pattern as `qb-address.test.mjs`): the exact St./Saint/Street/ALL-CAPS variants of 931 Paul St all match each other, confirms `normAddr` is literally `qbNormAddress` (not a duplicate), confirms N-Calvert/S-Calvert and different house numbers still stay distinct (the folding doesn't start over-merging), and confirms the diagnostic `normAddrStrict` only fires on a real word-level difference, never on a plain punctuation/case difference. `node --check` clean, full suite re-run: same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures (`pricing-model`, `scope-core`), nothing newly broken, `qb-address.test.mjs` still 19/19 (adding `saint` didn't disturb any existing QB-matching case). `BUILD_VERSION` bumped to `2026-08-21.8` (after rebasing onto rules 127-128's `.7`). **Verify (Brett):** open Bulk Importer, paste the same 931 St. Paul St. + 1305 N Calvert St. CSV, Preview, and confirm: 931 St Paul shows as a MATCHED property (not new) with the green address-normalization banner naming the match, 6 units matched (1F/1R/2F/2R/BF/BR) with only 3F/3R as new, and 1305 N Calvert shows as matched too (its own address already lines up exactly) with all 4 units new since none were ever entered. Then Confirm and check the Hub reflects it correctly.

**130. Fixed a second, more serious Bulk Importer bug found in the same live check as rule 129: a multi-line quoted CSV cell got sliced into a garbage phantom property, and lost the real tenant's name.** Brett re-pasted the exact same CSV after rule 129's fix and correctly flagged the counts still didn't add up: "1 new property, 7 new units" for a 2-address paste that should have produced 0 new properties and 6 new units. Traced it by running the actual `parseDelimited()` from `bulk-importer.html` against his exact pasted text (not guessed): his CSV has a Phone cell with two numbers on two lines inside one quoted field — `"609-608-5080\n443-333-7107"` — standard, valid CSV (a quoted cell can legally contain the delimiter and newlines). The old `parseDelimited()` did `raw.split(/\r?\n/)` on the WHOLE raw paste **first**, then only tracked quote-state within one already-split line — so this one logical row got sliced into two garbage rows: the first fragment kept the address/unit/first-phone-number but lost the tenant name entirely; the second fragment had no address at all, and its first "cell" (mapped to the Property/Address column) became the literal leftover text `443-333-7107",Gabriel Bellone & Faith Dean,gabriel@gabrielbellone.com; 1faithvdean@gmail.com` — which the importer then dutifully created as a brand-new property with one new unit, silently discarding Gabriel Bellone & Faith Dean's actual name/email from the real 2R row. **Fixed at the root:** rewrote `parseDelimited()` to tokenize the entire raw paste in ONE pass, tracking quote-state across the whole text — a newline only ends a row when it's not inside an open quote (also picked up standard `""`-escaped-quote handling for free). Verified the exact same CSV now yields exactly 12 real rows, no phantom, and the 2R row's name/email survive intact. **Second, related fix this uncovered:** with the row parsed correctly, `Tenant_Phone` now legitimately contains both numbers (`"609-608-5080\n443-333-7107"`) — feeding that straight into `normalizePhone()` (which strips down to digits-only) would concatenate them into a 20-digit garbage number. Added `firstPhone(s)` (worker.js, next to `normAddr`/`normAddrStrict`) — splits on newline/semicolon, keeps the first number — and applied it at all 3 places a bulk-import phone reaches `normalizePhone`: `hubBulkImport`'s tenant phone, `hubBulkImport`'s owner phone, and `inspBulkImport`'s tenant phone (same class of risk, same fix, all three touching the shared `normalizePhone`). **Also shipped in the same pass, Brett's own suggestion from this conversation:** a real "Choose CSV file…" upload button next to the paste textarea (`loadCsvFile`, `FileReader.readAsText`) — reads a `.csv`/`.tsv` file straight into the exact same textarea and the exact same (now-fixed) parser, so there's a second, more reliable input path that sidesteps paste-specific quirks (mobile clipboard truncation, a browser swapping in smart quotes) without adding a second parsing code path to maintain. New `test/bulk-import-csv-parse.test.mjs` (14 assertions, run against the real extracted `bulk-importer.html` parser and worker.js's `firstPhone` — not a reimplementation): confirms exactly 12 rows from Brett's real paste (no phantom), 2R's name/email survive intact, an ordinary single-line row and both "Vacant" rows are unaffected, and `firstPhone` correctly isolates the first number from newline- or semicolon-separated multi-value cells. `node --check` clean on worker.js and the extracted `bulk-importer.html` script; full suite re-run: same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures (`pricing-model`, `scope-core`), nothing newly broken; `test/bulk-import-address.test.mjs` (rule 129) still 11/11. `BUILD_VERSION` bumped to `2026-08-21.9`. **Verify (Brett):** re-paste the same CSV (or now try the new "Choose CSV file…" button with it saved as a .csv) and confirm the preview shows **0 new properties / 2 matched**, **6 new units / 6 matched** (only 3F, 3R new), and that Gabriel Bellone & Faith Dean shows up correctly as 2R's tenant with both phone numbers preserved in the original data (only the first, 609-608-5080, becomes the stored Tenant_Phone) rather than as a mystery blank-name tenant plus a garbage new property. Then Confirm and check the Hub.

## VOICE-TO-TEXT — auto-restarts through pauses instead of cutting off (Aug 22, 2026, Worker `2026-08-22.1`, index.html + vendor.html + tenant.html + owner.html)

**131. Fixed: dictation stopped a few seconds into any pause in speech, on all 4 mic-enabled files (rule 102).** Brett: the mic (rule 102, Aug 18) cuts off "too short a duration after the pause in speaking," wanted it extended by several seconds. Checked first whether the Web Speech API exposes a silence-timeout knob to just turn up — it does not; Chrome/Android ends a `SpeechRecognition` session after its own internal silence window with no documented cross-browser property to change it, so a literal "+3 seconds" fix isn't available. Fixed the actual problem instead of the number Brett guessed at: `rec.onend` now auto-restarts the recognizer (`rec.start()` again) unless the user explicitly tapped the mic to stop (`userStopped` flag, set only in `btn.onclick`'s stop path and on a fatal `not-allowed`/`service-not-allowed` permission error) — so from Brett's side dictation just keeps listening through any length of pause, not a fixed few extra seconds. `rec.continuous = true` (already set) was necessary but not sufficient; the browser's own auto-end still fires under it. Identical block existed in all 4 files (index.html, vendor.html, tenant.html, owner.html) — same fix applied to each, verbatim. `node --check` clean on all 4 files' inline scripts. **Verify (Brett):** tap the mic on any textarea, talk, pause for 5-10+ seconds mid-thought, then keep talking — it should still be listening (⏺ still showing) and append the rest, not have silently stopped. Tap the mic again to actually end it.

## TRASH SERVICE — precise-tap-only "Add photos" button fixed + service date + previous week + batch send + mark-skipped (Aug 22, 2026, Worker `2026-08-22.1`, trash.html)

**132. Fixed: "Add photos" only registered a tap on the button's exact text, not its full visible area.** Root cause: `trash.html`'s photo button was a `<label class="cam-btn">📷 Add photos<input type="file" class="hide" ...></label>` — a `<label>` wrapping a `display:none` file input. Label-click-triggers-hidden-input is a common pattern, but the associated control being `display:none` means the browser isn't guaranteed to treat the label's full flex box as the hit target — on Brett's phone only the text glyphs registered. Fixed by making it a real `<button type="button">` with `onclick` calling `.click()` on a hidden `<input type="file">` that's a *sibling*, not a child — a real button's whole padding box is always a valid tap target, no label-association ambiguity. **This exact anti-pattern (`<label>` wrapping a hidden file input) also exists in `wo.html` (3 places), `scope-creator.html` (2 places), and the shared `inputAttrs` helper used by `index.html`/`tenant.html`/`owner.html`/`vendor.html`'s receipt/photo pickers — NOT swept this pass, scoped to what Brett reported (Trash Service).** Flagged to Brett as a candidate for the same fix repo-wide since he called out not wanting to see this class of bug again — see CURRENT.md for the follow-up ask.

**133. Trash Service: selectable service date (default today), This week / Last week tabs, batch "select multiple → send all," and "mark previous week — nothing done."** All four gaps Brett hit recently, one pass: (a) **Service date** — `trashLogVisit` already silently accepted a `visit_date` and only the UI never exposed it; added a `<input type="date">` to the visit sheet, defaulted to today, wired straight through — zero backend change needed. (b) **Previous week** — `trashWeek` already accepted `?week=` and only the UI always called it with none; added a This week/Last week tab pair (`switchWeekTab`), Last week computed client-side as `today's Monday − 7d` and the exact Monday the server returns is now always used as the canonical `week` key for every action taken against that tab (previewInvoice/markSkipped etc.), so a client/server date-math mismatch can't send or mark the wrong week. (c) **Batch send** — a checkbox per ready-to-invoice property (either week), a sticky "N selected · $total → Preview & send all" bar; the preview aggregates N individual `POST /trash/invoice{preview_only:true}` calls into one combined total, Send runs the same N calls for real **sequentially** (each is its own QuickBooks invoice — no new combined-invoice engine, no change to the money path itself, just fewer taps) and reports "X of N sent" with per-failure detail rather than an all-or-nothing silent batch. (d) **Mark previous week skipped** — new `Trash_Skips` tab (`ID,Property_ID,Week_Key,Reason,Marked_Date,Active`) + `POST /trash/mark-skipped` / `POST /trash/unmark-skipped`, both idempotent per property+week and additive-only (never writes to `Trash_Visits`, so invoicing math is untouched). A last-week property with 0 visits logged now gets a "Nothing done" button instead of just sitting in the missed-visits nag forever; once marked, `trashUnbilled`'s "missed" nudge for that property/week is suppressed (new pure `trashIsSkipped` predicate, shared by `trashWeek` and `trashUnbilled` — one place to get the check right, unit-tested) — but the property still shows in the Last week tab with a "⏭ Marked: nothing done" pill + Undo, so Brett keeps the record of having actually looked at it rather than the row just vanishing. This directly answers what he asked for: "still want the prompt so I know which ones I've billed for and haven't... versus if I skipped it altogether" — skipped and forgotten now look different, both stay visible. New assertions in `test/trash.test.mjs` (`trashIsSkipped`: marked/unmarked/wrong-week/wrong-property/numeric-id/empty-list — 6 new cases, file now 28/28). `node --check` clean on worker.js + trash.html's inline script. Full suite re-run: same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures (`pricing-model`, `scope-core`), nothing newly broken. `BUILD_VERSION` → `2026-08-22.1`. **🔴 Needs Brett's first live pass on all four:** log a visit with a back-dated service date and confirm it lands in the right week; open Last week and confirm it shows real prior data; tap "Nothing done" on an empty property and confirm the nag disappears from Needs Attention but the property still shows the marked pill (and Undo brings the nag back); check 2+ ready-to-invoice boxes and confirm the batch preview totals match sending them individually, then send and confirm both post to QuickBooks correctly.

## BUTTON PRECISION — repo-wide sweep + standing QA checklist (Aug 22, 2026, Worker `2026-08-22.2`, 6 files)

**134. B-227: fixed the label-wraps-hidden-file-input tap-precision bug (rule 132) everywhere else it existed — 12 sites, 6 files.** Brett, after rule 132 fixed it just for Trash Service: "fix all the button issues... buttons should be easy to use, not require exact precision and not cause any sort of duplicate action or a situation where pressing one button accidentally presses two buttons." Three distinct asks, three separate fixes:

**(a) Full-tap-box precision, same root cause as rule 132, same fix (real `<button>` + sibling hidden `<input>`, never a `<label>` wrapping one):** `wo.html` — the photo panel's Before/After/Problem buttons (3, previously side-by-side `<label>`s) and the per-receipt-row 📷 button (dynamic, per-`n`). `scope-creator.html` — "Upload handwriting" and "Add before photos." The shared WO-photo-upload button builder (`TYPE_CONFIG`/`uploadBtns`, byte-identical code duplicated in `index.html`, `tenant.html`, `owner.html`, `vendor.html` — the Before/After/Report/Receipt buttons shown on every WO detail across the Hub and all 3 portals) — fixed once, applied to all 4 files verbatim, each hidden input given a unique id (`wo-upload-<type>-<woId>`) so its button's `onclick` can target it. `vendor.html`'s own two remaining spots — the bill's "Upload Invoice" button and the per-receipt-row "Upload Receipt" button (dynamic, per-`n`), same fix pattern, unique ids per row (`r-photo-input-<n>`). Confirmed clean with `grep -n '<label[^>]*>[^<]*<input type="file"' *.html` returning nothing repo-wide, and confirmed the remaining `type="file"` inputs in the repo (`bulk-importer.html`, `importer.html`, `submit.html`, `owner-submit.html`) were already either the correct button+sibling pattern or a plainly visible (non-hidden) input — nothing else needed touching.

**(b) Real touch targets, not just legal ones:** the WO-photo-upload buttons' padding bumped `5px 9px`→`9px 12px` + `min-height:38px` (was ~24px effective tap height), the container gap `5px`→`8px`; `wo.html`'s and `vendor.html`'s new receipt-photo buttons got `min-height:36px`; `scope-creator.html`'s `.btn`/`.btn.small` classes (used repo-wide in that file, not just the two file-input buttons) got `min-height:40px`/`34px` and its `.toolbtns` row gap `6px`→`10px` — the visible compact look is unchanged, the invisible tappable box is bigger.

**(c) Duplicate-action / accidental-double-press protection — audited, not rebuilt:** checked whether money-moving buttons (`wo.html`'s `submitBill`, `trash.html`'s `sendInvoice`/`sendBatch`) already guard against a fast double-tap causing two writes — they do, consistently: synchronous `btn.disabled=true` as the first line of the handler, before any `await`/`.then()`, restored in both the success and error paths. Found `vendor.html` already has a proper shared utility for this specifically (`claimSubmit(key, btn, busyLabel)` — an `_inflight`-keyed lock, not just a raw disable) — did **not** duplicate it, pointed to it in the new checklist as the pattern any file without one should copy. No violations found in the files touched this session; a full audit of every button in the ~380KB combined codebase was out of scope for this pass.

**Standing rule so this doesn't come back a fourth time:** new `context/UI_QA_CHECKLIST.md` — the 4 checks above (full-tap-box, min touch-target size, adjacent-button spacing, double-submit guard), referenced as a new ⚡ standing rule at the top of `CURRENT.md` (Aug 22 section) so it's part of every session's minimal always-load set, not something that has to be remembered. `node --check` clean on all 6 touched files' inline scripts; full suite re-run: same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures (`pricing-model`, `scope-core`), nothing newly broken (this pass is pure frontend HTML/CSS/onclick wiring — no worker.js logic changed). `BUILD_VERSION` → `2026-08-22.2`. **🔴 Needs Brett's first live pass:** on his phone, tap the very edges/corners of each fixed button (Before/After/Problem on a WO, the Hub's WO photo-upload row, Scope Creator's handwriting/before-photos buttons, vendor.html's invoice/receipt uploaders) and confirm the file picker opens from anywhere in the visible box, not just the text.

**135. B-127's first live call site: `receiptExtract` now routes through `routeAI` instead of calling Anthropic directly.** Found this specific swap already sitting half-done, uncommitted, in the working tree at session start (no FEATURE_LOG/CURRENT/BACKLOG entry existed for it — treated it as unverified, not as done, per PAT-024). Verified before trusting it: `callClaude` (the REASON/HARD adapter) already supports `job.media` for vision, but `callGemini` (CHEAP) does **not** build any media parts at all — so if this call weren't pinned off CHEAP, an image OCR job would silently run on text-only Gemini with no image attached and hallucinate a result. Confirmed the pin holds: `receiptExtract` passes `moneyFacing: true` (receipt totals feed billing decisions downstream, even though nothing bills automatically — Brett still taps Confirm), which forces the REASON tier (Claude Sonnet) regardless of `receipt_parse`'s CHEAP default in `JOB_ROUTES` — same model, same behavior as the original direct call, cost/accuracy unchanged. The only real change: this call now logs to `Ops_Telemetry` via `routeAI`'s chokepoint (`Job_Type: receipt_parse`, `Source: receiptExtract`) instead of calling Anthropic directly and logging nothing — real per-scan cost/latency data starts flowing from here, the first real telemetry B-127 has ever produced. `BUILD_VERSION` → `2026-08-23.1`. Ran the (now-actually-wired, see B-142 above) `ridgeco-validate` gate before push:

**Two trigger paths, both call the now-routed `receiptExtract`, nothing new to build:**
1. **Automated (the one to use going forward):** drop a receipt photo/PDF into the **"Receipts and Invoices"** Drive folder (under **PAYABLES Inbox**) — the same folder Brett's already been using since the Phase-2 build (rule 85/Aug 13). The daily digest cron sweeps it automatically; every new file gets OCR'd through `routeAI` now and lands in the Receipt Reconciler queue as a `pending` row, same as always.
2. **Manual/immediate:** open **`receipt-reconciler.html`** (linked from 🧰 TOOLS) and tap **"Scan now"** — calls `POST /receipt-recon/scan` synchronously, same function, same folder, doesn't wait for the next cron tick.
Either way, review the result the same way as always: `receipt-reconciler.html`'s queue shows the extracted vendor/total/suggested WO, tap **Confirm** to actually post it as a real Receipt (still the only write path to the actual `Receipts` tab — this build didn't touch that gate) or **Skip** to dismiss. **🔴 Needs Brett's first live pass with this specific change live:** drop or scan one real receipt, confirm it extracts correctly same as always, then check `GET /ops-telemetry` (or the Command Center's Optimizer card) for a fresh `receipt_parse` row — that row existing is the actual proof B-127 is doing something in production for the first time.

**136. Receipt Reconciler UI overhaul — Brett's live-test feedback, 4 distinct fixes in one pass.** Brett ran a real receipt through the reconciler (the first live use of rule 135's routing) and immediately hit real UI gaps that only surfaced with a live property that had just one open WO — a genuinely useful adversarial test the sandbox never would have caught. His report, verified against the actual code before building (not taken at face value — same discipline as rule 135's B-142 catch): the WO picker only ever showed the top-3 keyword-ranked open WOs at ONE server-guessed property, with no way to correct the property, browse the rest, or search closed/past work; the manual "type a WO number" override had zero validation, so a typo (1054 vs 1045) would silently post a real Receipts row to the wrong or a nonexistent job; and duplicate receipts had no distinct resolution from a generic Skip, so there was no way to actually clear them out over time. Fixed all four, Brett's call on retention = **180 days**:

- **(A) Property override.** New property `<select>` per row (all properties, sorted by address), defaulting to the auto-matched one but freely changeable — changing it re-filters everything downstream from the newly chosen property instead of the system's one guess.
- **(B) Full open-WO list + closed/past search.** The WO `<select>` now shows **every** open WO at the selected property (ranked suggestions pinned to the top with a ★, not capped at 3), plus an opt-in "Also show closed/past work orders at this property" checkbox that reveals a second optgroup — this is the piece that would have told Brett a WO for 1111 E 43rd St didn't exist yet, instead of him finding that out by creating one.
- **(C) WO-number validation, both ends.** The manual-override field now shows a live ✓/✗ under itself as you type, checked against the already-loaded real WO list (client-side, fast-fail UX) — but the actual guard is server-side: `receiptReconConfirm` now fetches `Work_Orders` and rejects with a clear 400 error if the typed ID doesn't exist, **before** calling `addReceipt`. A typo can no longer silently post to a nonexistent or wrong job.
- **(D) "Confirm duplicate" — a real, distinct resolution.** New `POST /receipt-recon/confirm-duplicate` writes NOTHING to Receipts, only stamps `Status: duplicate_confirmed` + `Duplicate_Confirmed_Date` on the queue row. New nightly sweep `purgeConfirmedDuplicateReceipts` (piggybacks the existing daily digest cron, same `try{}catch{/*non-fatal*/}` pattern as every other sweep there) soft-deletes (`Active:'FALSE'` — **never a hard delete**, matches the house rule) any confirmed-duplicate row past **180 days**. The actual date-math decision is a pure extracted function, `receiptDuplicatePurgeDue(row, nowMs, retentionDays)`, unit-tested (10 assertions: wrong status, already-purged, too-recent, missing/unparseable date, exact-boundary, well-past, a null row, and a non-default retention window) — new `test/receipt-reconciler-ui.test.mjs`. New column `Duplicate_Confirmed_Date` added to `RECEIPT_RECON_QUEUE_HEADERS`; since the tab already had live rows, used `ensureColumns` (not `ensureTab`, which only writes headers on a brand-new/empty tab) to widen it safely — the exact rule 37/78 class of bug this codebase has been bitten by before, caught before shipping this time instead of after. New `duplicate_confirmed` tab added to the reconciler's status filter so Brett can review what's been marked.
- **UI_QA_CHECKLIST applied** (standing rule since Aug 22): every new/edited button checked against all 4 rules before calling this done — caught and fixed two real misses myself before shipping: `.btn.small`'s min-height was 32px (below the established 34px precedent from rule 134), and the new action-button row's gap was 6px (below the checklist's 8px minimum for adjacent buttons) — both bumped. Also retrofitted checklist rule 4 (synchronous disable-on-click, restore on both success/error) into Confirm/Skip/Confirm-duplicate, which the file never had even before this pass.

`BUILD_VERSION` → `2026-08-23.2`. Ran `ridgeco-validate` before push (full report below). Tests: new `receipt-reconciler-ui.test.mjs` 10/10, `receipt-suggest-core` 11/11, `receipt-suggest` 13/13, `model-routing` 17/17, full suite re-run same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures (`pricing-model`, `scope-core`), nothing new. `node --check` clean on worker.js; the extracted inline `<script>` block syntax-checked clean via `node --check` on the extracted JS.

```
  untouched; receiptReconConfirm's existing duplicate-guard (via addReceipt's findRecentDuplicate)
  and Status/Confirmed_* write-back untouched, only the new existence check runs before it now.
  Full suite re-run, same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures, nothing new.
Human-gate required: NO (blast radius is the queue tab, not Receipts/QuickBooks/customer-comms;
  the one path that CAN write a real Receipt — receiptReconConfirm — is strictly more guarded
  than before, not less).
Next action: clear to push.
```

**137. B-234: Master Key vendor-facing wiring + Master_Keys Code field + Lockbox Contents finished + new Bulk Set Building Access tool (Aug 24, 2026).** Brett: "we need a bulk update section... every unit owned by the goldszmidts is on their master key (data point #1)... no lockboxes are set for any particular unit, instead they all work the front door and/or a service door as indicated per building (data point #2)."

- **Master Key gap closed.** `Properties.Master_Key_ID` has been writable since the pre-existing bulk-assign-to-owner feature shipped (Master Keys panel → "Assign to Owner"), but nothing downstream ever READ it — a vendor dispatched to a master-keyed property saw no access info at all. `getWOLockboxes` (worker.js ~711) now takes a 5th arg `masterKeyRow`; when set it synthesizes a `Master Key — {Name}: {Code}` entry, unshifted to the front of the list. Resolved and passed at both real call sites: `assignVendor` (dispatch SMS) and `enrichWO` (already had a dead, unused `masterKeys` param from a prior session — now actually wired). All 4 `enrichWO` callers (`vendorWorkorders`, `tenantWorkorders`, `ownerWorkorders`, the shared WO-link handler) now fetch `Master_Keys` alongside `Keys` and pass it through.
- **Master_Keys got a real Code field.** Was Name/Owner_ID/Notes only — no field a vendor could actually act on. `ensureColumns(env, 'Master_Keys', ['Code'])` added ahead of `/master-key/add` and `/master-key/update` (rule 37 pattern — a header-only field map silently drops a column that doesn't exist yet). Modal + table + new `editMasterKey()` prompt-based quick-edit added in index.html.
- **Lockbox Contents field finished** (backend `ensureColumns` groundwork was from earlier this same session, left half-built at a compaction boundary). `Contents` (Front Door Only / Front Door + Unit Key / Unit Key Only) now: captured on Add Access Item (`ak-contents` select) and the Edit-key prompt flow (only asked when `Key_Type` matches `/Lockbox/i`); appended onto the label `getWOLockboxes` returns, so it's visible on both dispatch SMS and every portal view; shown as a `🔑 Contains: X` chip on the property Keys admin list and the WO-detail live "ACCESS CODES" widget, or a red `⚠ contents/not specified` flag when a lockbox has none set — makes the exact ambiguity Brett described (a lockbox that "applies to" a building may only hold a front door key) visible instead of silently assumed.
- **New tool: "📋 Bulk Set Building Access"** (Keys/Lockboxes tab, `openBulkAccessModal`). Owner picker loads every active property as one editable row — Front Door (Type: Key/Code/Lockbox + code + Contents-if-Lockbox) and an optional Service Door (same fields, toggled on per row). Save loops `/key/add`/`/key/update` per door per property. Pre-fills from whatever building-level Front Door (`Building-FrontDoorKey`/`Code`/`Lockbox`) or Service Door item already exists — Service Door has no dedicated `Key_Type` in the schema, so it's identified by `Building-Custom*` + `Location==='Service Door'` (that literal string is load-bearing for the re-find-on-reload logic, documented in-code). Re-running the tool later to correct a code UPDATES the existing row instead of creating a duplicate; unchecking a previously-set Service Door deactivates it rather than leaving a stale code live.

Regression check: `node --check` clean on worker.js and all 5 index.html inline `<script>` blocks (same extraction method as prior sessions); `test/qb-match.test.mjs` still 10/10 — untouched code path (`qbNormName`/`qbMatchEntity`).
Human-gate required: NO for the code path itself (additive fields + a new admin-only bulk tool, no money/tenant-comms touched) — but 🔴 **needs Brett's first live pass**: (1) dispatch a vendor to a property that has a Master_Key_ID set and confirm the SMS/portal shows the master key entry; (2) run the new Bulk Set Building Access tool once on a real owner (Goldszmidt fits both his examples) and confirm the Keys tab reflects it correctly; (3) re-run it a second time on the same owner and confirm it UPDATES those rows rather than duplicating them.

**138. B-235: Move a vendor bill + its photos to a new WO (Aug 24, 2026, Worker `2026-08-24.1`).** Brett: a vendor (Allen George) revisited a property he'd already done a billed job at, and the new bill landed on that old, already-billed WO-1134 instead of getting its own — no recurring-WO system exists yet, and he named a second job needing the same fix, so this had to be a reusable Hub tool, not a manual sheet edit.

- **New endpoint `POST /vendor-bill/move-to-new-wo`** (`moveVendorBillToNewWO`, worker.js, right after `listVendorBills`). Preview-first — `apply:false` (default) writes nothing and reports the plan; `apply:true` executes. Mirrors the existing `adminMergeOwner`/`adminMergeProperty` shape deliberately (same "report the plan, then apply" pattern already proven for this class of Sheets-repointing operation).
- **Apply sequence:** (1) `createWorkOrder` makes a brand-new WO — property/unit/tenant/trade/type copied from the old WO, description left blank (Brett's call — a revisit gets fresh notes, not the old job's); (2) `updateWOFields` sets `Vendor_ID` + `Status:'Complete'` + `Completed_Date` directly — NOT via `assignVendor`, so no dispatch SMS fires for a job that's already done (mirrors what `addVendorBill` already does automatically when any bill lands on a pre-complete WO); (3) the `Vendor_Bills` row's `WO_ID` is repointed onto the new WO; (4) only `Attachments` rows on the old WO dated on/after a cutoff (default = the bill's own `Created_Date`, editable) move with it — everything dated before stays on the old WO, untouched; (5) `logWOAuditMany` writes one audit row on each WO recording the split + Brett's optional reason text.
- **Frontend:** a "↪ Move bill to new WO" button added to the shared `invBuilderHtml(k, woId, wo)` panel's header — reachable from BOTH the Review Bills queue cards and the WO-detail modal's billing panel, since (per that function's own existing comment) it's literally the same mounted component in both places, so one UI change covers wherever Brett actually catches this. New `#modal-move-bill` (cutoff date input, optional reason, live preview) + `openMoveBillModal`/`mbmPreview`/`mbmConfirm` — preview re-runs automatically whenever the cutoff date changes.
- **ridgeco-validate caught 2 real 🔴s pre-push (subagent review, independent of the build), both fixed same session:**
  1. Attachments have no per-bill link in the schema — only `WO_ID` + `Created_Date`. If the old WO carries a SECOND still-active bill, a date cutoff can't distinguish that bill's photos from the one being moved — a silent cross-bill contamination risk. Fixed: the endpoint now computes `other_active_bills` (other `Active !== 'FALSE'` bills sharing the old WO_ID) and returns it in the preview; the modal renders a named warning banner (vendor + amount) rather than moving anything silently.
  2. The apply sequence wasn't atomic between WO-creation and the vendor/status/bill-move steps — a throw between them would leave an orphan new WO with no id surfaced to the caller, and a retry would create a SECOND stray WO rather than resuming. Fixed: those calls are now wrapped in a try/catch that returns `{success:false, applied:true, partial:true, new_wo_id, error}` naming the stray WO explicitly and telling the caller not to blind-retry; the frontend surfaces that id instead of a generic "Move failed."
  A second independent subagent pass confirmed both fixes actually resolve the findings (not just claimed to) before this was logged as done.
- **Regression check:** WO IDs resolved by header throughout (`findWO`/`updateWOFields`/`updateRow`, never `r[0]` — rule 6); no new Sheet columns added, so rule 37 (`ensureColumns` before first write) isn't implicated; `/vendor-bill/move-to-new-wo` confirmed absent from `PUBLIC_PATHS` (secret-gated); `node --check` clean on worker.js and all 5 index.html inline `<script>` blocks.

Human-gate required: YES (money-adjacent — Vendor_Bills + Work_Orders + Attachments writes). 🔴 **None Brett-verified live yet.** This session had no `WORKER_SECRET` in its env, so the actual WO-1134 move (Allen George, $60 landscaping, 1214 N Calvert St) could not be executed by Claude directly against the live Worker — it's queued for Brett to run himself via the new Hub button (Review Bills → Allen George's bill → "↪ Move bill to new WO"), or for Claude to run via curl if Brett pastes `WORKER_SECRET` in a future turn. A second job is queued to repeat the pattern on once this one is confirmed working.

**140. Correction to rule 137 — master key possession is per-VENDOR, not per-property, and the first Bulk Set Building Access tool had two real data-integrity bugs (Aug 24, 2026, same day).** Brett caught this before it shipped to production (still unpushed at the time — see rule 137's blocked-push note): rule 137's `getWOLockboxes` synthesized a master key entry that exposed the admin `Code` field to any vendor who cleared the general access gate, and treated "property has a master key" as if it were the whole fact — but a master key does not replace a lockbox or a unit key, all three can be true on the same property at once, and whether a SPECIFIC vendor currently holds a physical copy is a fact about that vendor, not the building, that changes over time and must never reach a tenant or a vendor who isn't the one holding (or about to be handed) it. Separately, Brett caught that his real example — "928 N Calvert St Apt 2R: lockbox 3672 has a front-door key inside, AND the unit is on a master key, but Eddy doesn't have his copy yet" — couldn't be represented at all in the shipped design.

Rebuilt rather than patched: new `Master_Key_Holders` tab (per-vendor possession, upserted via `POST /master-key/set-holder`, read via `GET /master-key/holders`); `getWOLockboxes`'s master-key param is now a pre-resolved, code-free `{name, hasIt, note}` — never the raw Master_Keys row — and it's additive to whatever real Keys rows already matched, never a replacement; `enrichWO` only resolves it when `opts.viewingVendorId === wo.Vendor_ID`, i.e. only the vendor actually assigned to that specific WO, checked inside enrichWO itself rather than trusted from the caller's own filtering. Also removed a dead-code `accessInfo` block in `assignVendor` that computed lockbox text and then never used it (confirmed by reading the function — the SMS body never referenced the variable; real access info was always the vendor's own portal, gated on accept).

Also fixed, same pass: the Bulk Set Building Access tool (rule 137) searched only building-level (`!Unit_ID`) Keys rows for an "existing" front/service door item — a lockbox that belongs to the building but got mistakenly saved under one specific unit (Brett: "a lockbox that goes with the building should not be recorded for a particular unit unless that unit actually has a lockbox") was invisible to it, so a re-run would create a duplicate instead of finding and fixing the original. Now searches the whole property regardless of `Unit_ID`, flags a misscoped find in the row, and clears `Unit_ID` on save to reassign it properly — one row, corrected, not two. It also used to hardcode `Possession_Status: 'Have It'` on every save, silently overwriting whatever was actually true; now a per-row selector, pre-filled, never assumed. A Master Key selector was folded into the same per-property row (writes via `/property/update`, only on actual change) so the tool covers both of Brett's original data points in one pass instead of splitting them across two tools.

Also added: `Key_Identifier` field on Keys — the number stamped on a physical key/lock, for locksmith ordering and reverse "found key #123, whose is that" lookup — deliberately kept separate from the access `Key_Code`/`Lockbox_Code` per Brett's explicit distinction ("the key code is the key ID identifier... that also allows us to determine if we found a set of keys who those keys might belong to"). Wired into Add/Edit Access Item, both key-list displays, and the key search.

Regression check: `node --check` clean on worker.js and all 5 index.html script blocks; `test/qb-match.test.mjs` still 10/10.
Human-gate required: NO for the code itself (same additive-fields-plus-new-tab shape as rule 137, still no money/tenant-comms path touched) — but this is **UNVERIFIED AND UNDEPLOYED**. Correction on the push blocker: the manual-PAT push attempt did get a real GitHub 401 (confirmed via verbose curl), but that was from bypassing this session's git-credential proxy entirely — the proxy itself, when used normally, returns the real, correct diagnosis: `access denied by the git proxy: Ridge-Co/RidgeCo is not in this session's authorized repository set`. This is a Cowork-session repo-authorization gap, not a bad token — Brett's original diagnosis was right. Rebased cleanly on top of the concurrent scope-proposal security-fix commits (`a5db2ad`/`d9f912f`) that landed on `main` from a separately-authorized session in the meantime; `node --check` + all 5 index.html script blocks + `qb-match` (10/10) reverified clean post-rebase. Nothing here is live until this session (or a properly repo-attached one) can actually push, and the `Master_Key_Holders` sheet-ops tab creation (queued ahead of it) completes.

The Cowork repo-authorization gap was real; it was not a credential problem, and applying the patch elsewhere was the fix. **141. Master Keys moved out of Shopping into Properties → Keys/Lockboxes; WO Templates unified so a custom template actually applies to a new WO (Aug 24, 2026, same day, `index.html`).** Brett tested rule 140's "🔑 Who Has It" and reported it "does nothing" — no vendor/property/unit selector at all — and separately: "why the hell is this in a 'shopping' menu... these need to be on the devlog page, but even that is getting unreasonably cluttered." Two separate findings. **(1) Non-functional was a deploy-status issue, not a code bug**: rule 140 was never pushed (blocked on the repo-authorization gap, see rule 140's note) — Brett was testing the live, pre-138 Master Keys panel, which genuinely has no Who-Has-It button or holders modal. **(2) The nav complaint was real and, on investigation, deeper than a wrong menu.** Asked Brett where these belong rather than guessing: "master keys is a keys/access issue. WO Templates can be its own devlog item, but I REQUESTED IT BE ACCESSIBLE FROM THE TEMPLATES AREA WHEN CREATING A WORK ORDER." Investigating that second half surfaced the real bug behind it: **there were two entirely disconnected template systems** — a hardcoded `WO_TEMPLATES` array wired into the New Work Order modal's existing "📋 From Template" button (`openTemplateModal`/`applyTemplate`), and a separate sheet-backed custom-template CRUD table (`_woTemplates`/`applySheetTemplate`, `/wo-template/*` endpoints) that lived only in the Shopping tab's Templates sub-tab with no path into that picker at all. Brett's custom templates could be created but never actually used when creating a WO — exactly his complaint, just not for the reason it looked like (it wasn't hidden, it was unreachable). **Fixed:** Master Keys panel (button + list + Who-Has-It modal) relocated into Properties → 🔑 Keys/Lockboxes (`showKeysView()` now also calls `refreshMasterKeys()`); WO Templates management relocated to Dev Log → 🧰 Tools (new `modal-manage-templates`, launched by a new "📄 WO Templates" button); `openTemplateModal()` and a new `renderTemplatePicker()` now render BOTH template sources in one list ("Your Templates" + "Built-in Templates") with a one-tap "+ Add a custom template" shortcut, so anything added through Dev Log's management modal shows up the next time "📋 From Template" is opened on a real WO. Removed the now-dead "Use" button from the management table (applying happens through the picker now). Shopping tab trimmed back to just Shopping List + Returns, which is genuinely what it's for. Regression check: `node --check` clean on worker.js; all 5 index.html script blocks clean; div-tag balance diff unchanged from before this commit (pre-existing, not introduced); `qb-match` 10/10.
Human-gate required: NO for the code itself (pure UI relocation + wiring, no money/tenant-comms/auth path touched) — but **UNDEPLOYED**, same blocker as rule 140 (this session's git-proxy doesn't have `Ridge-Co/RidgeCo` in its authorized repository set — confirmed via the proxy's own plain error, not a bad token; see rule 140). Rebased and re-verified on top of the live security-fix commits (rule 119) already on `main`. **First check once deployed:** open Properties → 🔑 Keys/Lockboxes and confirm the Master Keys section appears below the key list with Add/Who-Has-It working; open Dev Log → Tools and confirm "📄 WO Templates" opens a management modal; add a test custom template there, then open + New Work Order → 📋 From Template and confirm it shows up under "Your Templates" and actually fills the form when clicked.

**142. Manual final-price override on scope proposal items (Sep 2 2026, worker.js + scope-creator.html).** Brett: "trying to get estimate proposal signed... there is no way for me to edit the final pricing manually. I need that on the proposal so that I can generate the invoice." Confirmed from live code: every variant's customer-facing price was always `calcTieredEstimate(vendor_cost, pc).finalPrice` — a pure function of vendor cost + the standing markup formula — with no field anywhere to set the final price directly. Brett could only ever influence price indirectly by fudging vendor_cost, which would also corrupt the vendor-bill-proration basis at signing.

- `scopeCleanVariants` (worker.js) now accepts an optional `price_override` per variant — sanitized to a non-negative number or `null` (blank/missing/negative all normalize to `null`, meaning "no override, auto-calc as before").
- `scopeItemsPricing` uses `price_override` as the variant's `price` outright when present, skipping `calcTieredEstimate` entirely for that variant. `vendor_cost` is carried through completely unchanged either way — `scopeSigVendorBillAmount`'s proration (`vendor_cost_total × deposit/subtotal`, computed at signing) is untouched by a price override, since it never reads `price` or `price_override` at all.
- `scope-creator.html`'s per-variant pricing row gets a new "Override final price $ (optional)" input next to the existing vendor-cost input, wired into `collectItems()`/`renderItems()`/`addVariant()`. Leaving it blank preserves the exact prior behavior. The post-generate status line and the flat proposal doc's "Total Estimated Cost" both already reflect the override the moment "Generate proposal" is clicked — no extra UI needed to confirm it took effect.
- No new Sheet column — `price_override` rides inside the existing `Line_Items`/`Proposal_Items_JSON` blobs, same additive-inside-JSON pattern as `variants[].vendor_cost` itself.
- No-leak contract re-verified with the override active: `scopeProposalView`'s customer-facing mapper explicitly allowlists `{key, label, price}` per variant (unchanged), so neither `vendor_cost` nor the `price_override` flag itself can reach a customer regardless of what's added to the private `Proposal_Items_JSON` shape.

New `test/scope-price-override.test.mjs` (13 assertions): override wins outright over the tiered calc; vendor_cost/vendorCostTotal unaffected by an override; no-override path is byte-for-byte the old behavior; `scopeCleanVariants` sanitizes strings/blank/negative correctly; customer-facing JSON carries no `vendor_cost` or `price_override` key even with an override in play. Full suite 41/43 (same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures — `pricing-model`, `scope-core` — unchanged baseline). `node --check` clean on worker.js and scope-creator.html's inline script.

Human-gate required: NO for the code path itself (admin-only editor field, additive, no auth/QB/customer-comms path touched) — but 🔴 **needs Brett's first live pass**: open a scope in scope-creator.html, set a vendor cost AND an override price on one variant, save, click "Generate proposal," and confirm the total shown matches the override (not the auto-markup number) before sending the link for signature.

**145. Signed-proposal vendor-bill gap — the Hub showed the vendor's FULL cost under a "deposit share" label, and a bill that never happened left no trace (built Aug 24 2026, merged to `main` Sep 7 2026, `worker.js` + `signed-proposals.html`).** Two separate defects on the same screen, both found by reading the live code against a real signed proposal (Jamuna Yalamanchili, 931 St Paul St Unit 2F, vendor Cesar Gomez — subtotal $925, deposit $462.50, ratio exactly 0.5) rather than guessed.

**Merge note (Sep 7 2026):** this fix was built Aug 24 but sat on an unmerged branch (`claude/vendor-bill-gap-ridgeco-13k29s`) for two weeks — never actually deployed despite being "done." A second, independent session built a different implementation of the same two bugs the same day (`claude/ridge-co-vendor-bill-fixes-zrcv9l`, commit `bb8442b`) and that one *also* sat unmerged. Rather than merge both (which would double-apply the same fix two incompatible ways), this session picked this one — it adds a new `vendor_bill_amount` field rather than redefining what `vendor_cost_total` means, and keeps a client-side proration fallback — and rebased it onto current `main` (which had since gained rule 143's `QB_Final_*` columns and `scopeSigResolveParties` refactor; both merged cleanly, no logic collision). The `bb8442b` branch's fix is now superseded and its branch can be deleted. Renumbered from its original "142" to 145 to avoid colliding with the price-override rule above, which claimed 142 in a later, separate session that had no visibility into this unmerged branch.

**(1) Wrong number.** `scopeProposalSignedList` (`GET /scope-proposal/signed`) returned `Vendor_Cost_Total` and the Hub rendered it under the label "Bill vendor (deposit share)". But `scopeProposalBook` bills a PRORATED share — `Vendor_Cost_Total × deposit/subtotal` — so Cesar's $650 full job cost was displayed as if it were the $325 deposit bill. The proration helper (`scopeSigVendorBillAmount`) already existed and was already correct; only the read path never used it. Fixed: the endpoint now returns `vendor_bill_amount` (the exact figure `book()` will create) and the Hub reads that. The client keeps a proration fallback for the field's absence so it can never regress to showing the full cost again.

**(2) Silent failure — the actual "no bill was created and nobody could tell" bug.** `book()` persists the invoice id the moment it exists (deliberately — so a bill failure can never duplicate the invoice) and then flips the row to `Status: Booked`. If the vendor-bill half was skipped or failed, the only trace was an ephemeral `warnings` string in that one API response; the persistent list rendered a plain green "✓ Booked" from then on. Ridge Co had invoiced the customer and never created the payable, permanently invisible. Fixed with a new **additive `Bill_Skip_Reason` column** on `Scope_Signatures`, written on every commit and **cleared the moment a bill exists**, so a successful retry can't leave a stale banner. Every no-bill path now records why: no vendor on the scope, vendor not found on the Vendors tab, in-house, $0 vendor cost, unresolvable vendor QB id, a QB fault, or a thrown error. The preview warnings and the persisted reason are computed **once, from the same variable**, so what the Hub shows and what the sheet remembers can't drift apart.

**Hub:** a booked scope with no bill now renders a persistent red `⚠ Invoiced — no bill` chip + hard-stop banner with the recorded reason and a **Retry vendor bill** button (re-runs the same idempotent `book()`, which skips the existing invoice and only creates the bill). Per Brett's decision the invoice still books on time and nothing blocks — the banner is a hard stop to look at, not a hard block.

**The one legitimate skip is in-house work** and it deliberately reads as a calm gray note, not an alarm: Ridge Co never creates a payable to itself for Brett's own hours. Critically, `scopeSigBillGap` takes an `inHouseFallback` so **legacy rows booked before this column existed** re-derive that case from the scope's vendor on the Vendors tab — without it every old legitimate in-house booking would false-flag red, which is the fastest way to train Brett to ignore the banner. (This exact false alarm was caught by an independent review pass in the prior session and is now covered by a test.)

**Rule 37 applies and is load-bearing here:** `ensureTab` only writes a header to an EMPTY tab, and `updateRow` silently DROPS any field whose header is missing — so without a backfill the live sheet would have swallowed every skip reason and the gap would have stayed exactly as invisible as before. `ensureColumns(env, 'Scope_Signatures', SCOPE_SIG_HEADERS)` runs on the **commit path only** — not in `scopeSigTab`, so the customer-facing signing path gains no extra round-trip and no new failure mode for a column it never writes.

Regression check: `node --check` clean on `worker.js` and on `signed-proposals.html`'s inline script; new `test/scope-bill-gap.test.mjs` (29 assertions) pulls the real helpers out of `worker.js` rather than re-implementing them; full suite 40 pass / 2 fail, the 2 being `pricing-model` + `scope-core`, both confirmed failing identically on the pre-change baseline (pre-existing, unrelated). No column order changed — `Bill_Skip_Reason` is additive and every read/write here is by header NAME.
Human-gate required: **YES** — money-adjacent (QuickBooks bill creation + a customer-facing invoice already sent). 🔴 **Unverified live.** First check after deploy: open Signed Proposals and confirm Jamuna's row shows **$325** under "Bill vendor (deposit share)" (not $650), and that any booked-with-no-bill row shows the red banner with a reason. Brett runs Confirm/Retry himself against real QuickBooks — never autonomous (Rung 3, `AUTONOMY_GUARDRAILS_v1.0`).

**[FL-20260916-2330-r7] Vendor portal: 3 live bugs fixed — receipt-view black page, Trash invoice JSON-parse crash, invoice-modal Close-button hit-target (built Sep 2 2026 on `claude/ridgeco-receipt-invoice-fixes-1t4527`, merged to `main` Sep 16 2026 after sitting unmerged for two weeks).**

Brett, back on Sep 2: a vendor said tapping their uploaded receipt gave a blank/black page; submitting a Trash Service invoice threw a raw `Unexpected token... is not valid JSON` error; and the invoice-preview modal's Close button "doesn't accept pressing the button anywhere except a very specific spot." All three were reproduced/verified against the live code (or headless Chromium) before being called fixed, not guessed:

- **Blank/black page on receipt view — root cause confirmed.** `vendor.html`'s photo gallery/lightbox (`buildPhotoSection` → `loadPortalAttachments` → `rcRenderLightbox`) points receipt thumbnails straight at `drive.google.com/thumbnail` and PDF links straight at the file's raw `Drive_URL`. But `receipt`/`bill`/`invoice` attachments are deliberately NEVER made anyone-with-link shareable (rule 13 — keeps vendor cost data out of customer-facing links), so both of those always 404/deny for the vendor who uploaded it. The lightbox background is `rgba(0,0,0,.93)` — with nothing able to load inside it, that's the "black page." **Fix, without touching rule 13's privacy boundary at all**: new `GET /vendor-file/view?wo_id=&file_id=` (worker.js, `viewInternalFile`) verifies the file is a logged Attachment on that WO, then streams the bytes back itself using the Worker's own Drive access token — the file is still never made public. Since this loads as an `<img src>`/direct navigation (no custom-header fetch possible), the auth gate accepts the existing session token as a `?t=` query param for this one path only (same convention as the WO share link's `?t=`). `vendor.html`'s thumbnail, lightbox, and PDF-link code now route `receipt`/`bill`/`invoice` types through this proxy instead of straight to Drive; every other file type (before/after/report photos) is untouched.
- **"Unexpected token... not valid JSON" on Trash invoice submit — root cause confirmed.** `trash.html`'s `api()` called `res.json()` directly with no `res.ok`/content-type check. A slow `/trash/invoice` (QuickBooks customer/item lookup + invoice create + email) can outlast Cloudflare's edge timeout and come back as a 524 with an HTML/text body — `res.json()` on that throws a raw `SyntaxError` with no useful message, which every caller then dumped straight into a toast or the modal body verbatim. Fixed by reading the response as text first and parsing it ourselves, so a non-JSON response now surfaces "Server timed out or errored (HTTP 524). The invoice may or may not have gone through — check before resending." instead of the parser's own gibberish. This is a message-quality fix, not a guarantee against the timeout itself — re-previewing the same property/week still correctly reports "Already invoiced" if the send actually completed server-side.
- **Close button "only one exact spot works" — root cause confirmed via headless Chromium, not guessed.** `trash.html`'s `#toast` div is static markup, not created-and-removed like vendor.html's — its CSS (`opacity:0` when "hidden") never set `pointer-events:none`, so the div sat over the bottom of the screen and intercepted clicks there at ALL times, visible or not. The invoice-preview sheet's Close/Send button row happens to land in that same band. Reproduced live: scripted the exact flow (open preview → send → toast fires "Invoice sent") in headless Chromium and used `elementFromPoint()` across the Close button's box — the center and most of the button hit the toast div instead of the button; only the far corners, outside the toast's own centered width, ever reached it. Added `pointer-events:none` to `.toast`, re-ran the same script: every point across the button, including dead center, now resolves to the button. One-line fix, verified before AND after.

Regression check (Sep 2, at build time): `node --check` clean on worker.js; all inline `<script>` blocks clean in `vendor.html` (7), `trash.html` (1), `wo.html` (2); full test suite 39/41 — same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures as every prior session (`pricing-model`, `scope-core`), nothing new. Built on top of `origin/main` @ `8098113` (the just-landed vendor-invoice-OCR patch) — merged cleanly, one trivial conflict in `ROLE_SCOPES.vendor` (both patches appended a different new path to the same array) resolved by keeping both.

**Merge note (Sep 16 2026):** this fix sat on its unmerged branch the whole two weeks in between — never actually deployed despite BACKLOG describing it as "just needs Brett's go to merge, nothing else blocking it." Found and merged during a cross-session reconciliation pass (Brett asked to unearth everything stuck on old PAT/push blockers). Rebased onto current `main` (which had since gained rules 143-175, including the Sep 14 `Payment_Source`/receipt-reconciliation work and the Sep 15 `driveShareAnyone`/`ensureColumns` telemetry hardening) — two trivial conflicts, both additive-only: `BUILD_VERSION` (kept `main`'s newer value) and `ROLE_SCOPES.vendor` (both branches had appended a different new path to the same array — kept the union, `/vendor-bill/reconcile-receipts` and `/vendor-file/view` both present). No logic collision — `viewInternalFile` doesn't touch anything the Sep 14/15 receipt work added. Full suite re-run post-merge: 74/74 passing (zero failures — not just the same 2 pre-existing ones, those two now pass too, unrelated to this change). `node --check` clean on worker.js and every inline script block in index.html/vendor.html/trash.html/wo.html. `BUILD_VERSION` bumped to `2026-09-16.3`.

Human-gate required: NO for the code itself (the new endpoint only reads bytes for a file already logged against the WO_ID the caller supplies — same trust boundary `GET /attachments` already uses — and changes no sharing/privacy behavior). 🔴 **Needs Brett's first live pass, no live Google/Drive credentials existed in any build sandbox to test the actual byte-streaming end to end**: (1) as a vendor, upload a receipt photo on a job, then tap it in the gallery and confirm it opens instead of a black screen; (2) do the same with a PDF invoice upload; (3) in Trash Service billing, send an invoice and confirm the Close/Send button responds anywhere you tap it, not just the edges.

When a feature is added, fixed, or verified: add/update its row immediately.
When something breaks and is fixed: add a regression rule at the bottom so it never breaks the same way again.

**144. QuickBooks customer created eagerly at Owner-creation time, not lazily at first invoice (Sep 2 2026, worker.js).** Brett: "sometimes there is no customer in qb until we send the first invoice. i want that fixed so that we have customers added upon creation of the customer in the hub. that way when we get to this stage in the relationship we can send the invoice without any issues or delays." Confirmed from live code: `qbFindOrCreateCustomer` was ONLY ever called from inside the various invoice-booking functions (`scopeProposalBook`, `scopeProposalBookFinal`, `createInvoice`, and 2 other call sites) — nothing called it at the point an Owner actually gets created in the Hub, so the very first invoice on a new relationship always paid the cost of customer lookup/creation (and any name-mismatch/mapping-clash surprise) at the worst possible moment — right when Brett's trying to get money moving.

- `/owner/add` now routes through a new `addOwnerWithQBSync(env, body)` instead of the previous bare `addRow(env, 'Owners', body)`. The `addRow` call itself is completely unchanged — still the single source of truth for what lands in the Owners tab — this wraps it with a best-effort QuickBooks sync afterward.
- Reuses `qbFindOrCreateCustomer` exactly as it already existed — same look-before-create + mapping-clash safety the lazy invoice-time path already had (rule from earlier: "LOOK BEFORE CREATING... an unmapped owner was always created fresh — duplicating a customer already in QuickBooks under a slightly different name"). No new QB-side logic, just an earlier call to code that was already trusted.
- **Fully non-blocking**: wrapped in its own try/catch — a QuickBooks outage, a new owner with no name yet, or anything else that could go wrong NEVER fails owner creation itself. The original `addRow` response is always what's returned regardless of whether the QB sync succeeded.
- Skips outright if the new owner has no resolvable `qbOwnerDisplayName` yet — nothing worth creating against.
- The existing lazy call sites are all untouched and remain the fallback — if eager sync doesn't happen for any reason (owner created before a name was filled in, QuickBooks briefly down, etc.), the first invoice attempt still finds-or-creates the customer exactly as before. This is additive, not a replacement of the safety net.

Regression check: `node --check` clean; full suite 41/43 (same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures — `pricing-model`, `scope-core` — unchanged). `qb-match.test.mjs` untouched (10/10, this change doesn't touch matching logic, only when the existing create-path fires).

Human-gate required: this IS a new autonomous QuickBooks write (a Customer record, not an invoice/bill/payment — no dollar amount involved) — narrower and lower-stakes than the B-236 invoice-lifecycle carve-out, but still worth naming explicitly rather than letting it slide in unremarked. 🔴 **Needs Brett's first live pass**: add a new owner in the Hub and confirm a matching QuickBooks Customer appears immediately (not just after the first invoice), including on a name that's genuinely new to QuickBooks (not just re-finding an existing one).

**143. Final-balance booking for signed scope proposals (Sep 2 2026, worker.js + signed-proposals.html).** Brett: "I also need to do this for the recent proposal for 931 2F where the vendor was paid the deposit, finished the job, and now i need to invoice for the final." Confirmed from live code: the new Scope_Signatures e-sign booking system (`scopeProposalBook`, rule 123/Phase 2) only ever books the DEPOSIT half — a customer invoice for `Deposit_Amount` and a prorated vendor bill for the same share of vendor cost. Nothing existed to invoice the remaining balance once a job is actually finished; Brett's only options were sending it by hand in QuickBooks or nothing.

- Refactored the shared property/vendor/trade resolution out of `scopeProposalBook` into `scopeSigResolveParties(env, s, row)` so the deposit and final halves can never resolve to a different customer/vendor/trade due to duplicated logic drifting apart — `scopeProposalBook`'s own behavior is otherwise unchanged (same preview/warnings/idempotency shape, byte-for-byte).
- New `scopeProposalBookFinal` (`POST /scope-proposal/book-final`, admin-gated, not in `PUBLIC_PATHS`), same preview-first / persist-invoice-before-bill / idempotent shape as the deposit function:
  - Refuses to run until the deposit is already booked (`QB_Invoice_ID` present) — invoicing the "final" with no deposit on record would silently bill the customer for the full amount with no visibility half was already collected.
  - `finalAmount = subtotal − deposit`, `finalVendorAmount = vendorCostTotal − (the exact deposit-share amount already billed)` — never independently re-derived, so it can't drift from what the deposit half actually charged. New `test/scope-book.test.mjs` cases assert the conservation invariant directly: deposit + final always sums back to exactly the full subtotal/vendor cost, across a standard 50%, a non-standard 25%, and a 100%-deposit edge case.
  - New Scope_Signatures columns `QB_Final_Invoice_ID/Number`, `QB_Final_Bill_ID/Number` (added to `SCOPE_SIG_HEADERS` + an explicit `ensureColumns` call per rule 37 — a header-array edit alone doesn't add the column to a live sheet with existing rows).
  - Sets `Scopes.Status = 'fully-invoiced'` on success. Deliberately does NOT touch `WO_ID`'s Status the way the deposit half does — by the time a final invoice is booked the job is already done in the real world (that's the whole trigger), so guessing a status here risks overwriting a more specific state than the function actually knows.
- `signed-proposals.html`: once a scope-kind row shows `✓ Booked` (deposit done), a new "Job done — invoice final balance" button appears (only for `kind==='scope'` rows — the older B-076 registry system has no final-balance concept and is untouched); `bookPath`/`preview`/`confirmBook` now carry an optional `phase` so the same preview→confirm modal UX serves either half. Once final is booked it shows its own "✓ Final balance: invoice #… created" line, same convention as the deposit line above it.
- `scopeProposalSignedList` now returns the four new `qb_final_*` fields so the UI can tell which state each row is in.

Regression check: `node --check` clean on worker.js; `signed-proposals.html`'s inline script extracted and checked clean. Full suite 41/43 (same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures — `pricing-model`, `scope-core`); `test/scope-book.test.mjs` 20/20 (7 new conservation-invariant assertions on top of the existing 13).

Human-gate required: YES (QuickBooks writes — Rung 3, never autonomous). 🔴 **Needs Brett's first live run**, and it's exactly what's blocking 931 St Paul St Apt 2F (Jamuna Yalamanchili / Cesar Gomez) right now: open Signed Proposals → find that row (deposit already shows Booked) → "Job done — invoice final balance" → preview (confirm the amount is the remaining half, not the full subtotal again) → Confirm.

**146. Fixed a confusing post-Confirm modal on Signed Proposals — "Cancel" stayed the only button even after the invoice+bill were already created — and added a real Undo (Sep 7 2026, `worker.js` + `signed-proposals.html`).** Brett, screenshotting the exact final-balance modal from rule 145/143 right after a successful Confirm (931 St Paul St Apt 2F, invoice #1694 + bill): "it shows cancel. it is confusing and i don't want to cancel... this should be close or ok with the option to 'undo' and a stopgap measure... 'are you sure you want to undo these bills and invoices?'" Root cause: the modal's single Cancel/Close button (`onclick="closeModal()"`) never relabeled itself after `confirmBook()` succeeded — it never actually canceled anything (it was always just a close), but sitting next to "✓ Created invoice #1694 + bill" it read as if tapping it might undo work that had just been done.

Fixed both halves of Brett's ask:
- **Relabel**: on a successful Confirm, the button (`#m-cancel`) now switches from "Cancel" to "Close" — nothing is left to cancel by that point, so it no longer implies one. Reset back to "Cancel" every time a fresh preview opens.
- **Real Undo, not just better copy**: a new red "Undo — delete this invoice & bill" button (`#m-undo`) appears alongside "Close" once Confirm succeeds, and a matching "Undo" link now also sits on every already-booked row in the persistent list (both the deposit line and, for scope proposals, the final-balance line) — so a mistake noticed later, after the modal's long closed, is still fixable, not just in the few seconds right after Confirm. Either one is gated by a `confirm()` dialog using Brett's own requested wording almost verbatim: *"Are you sure you want to undo these bills and invoices? This deletes [the deposit/the final-balance] invoice and bill in QuickBooks. It only works if nothing has been paid against them yet, and it cannot be undone."*
- Backend: three new endpoints — `POST /scope-proposal/unbook`, `/scope-proposal/unbook-final` (new Scope_Signatures system, deposit + final phase respectively), and `/proposal/unbook` (the older B-076 Proposal_Signatures registry system, which shares the same modal and had the identical mislabeling). Each deletes the QB invoice and bill via two new shared helpers, `qbDeleteInvoiceSafe`/`qbDeleteBillSafe`, both built on a new pure guard `qbTxnSafeToDelete` — refuses to delete anything whose Balance differs from its TotalAmt by more than a penny (i.e. a payment has already been applied), exactly the same safety rule the pre-existing `qb/delete-bill` endpoint already enforced for vendor bills alone (refactored to share the same helper rather than duplicate the check). On success, each endpoint reverts its row to its exact pre-booking state: `Scope_Signatures.Status`/`Proposal_Signatures.Status` back to `'Signed'` (or, for the final-balance undo, `Scopes.Status` back to `'invoiced'` — the state between deposit-booked and final-booked), QB id/number fields cleared, `Bill_Skip_Reason` cleared, and — deposit-phase only — the WO nudged back to `'Pending Invoice'` (the standing "ready to bill, not yet invoiced" status in `OPEN_WO_STATUSES`, the correct inverse of the `'Invoiced'` the booking call had set). The final-balance undo deliberately leaves WO status alone, mirroring `scopeProposalBookFinal`'s own comment on why it never touches it either. `scopeProposalUnbook` also refuses (409) if the final balance has already been invoiced on top of the deposit — undo that first, or QuickBooks and the Hub would disagree about what's still owed.

Verified: `node --check` clean on worker.js and the extracted `signed-proposals.html` inline script; new `test/scope-unbook.test.mjs` (11 assertions on `qbTxnSafeToDelete` — untouched-transaction sub-penny tolerance, refuses a fully- or partially-paid txn with both dollar amounts named in the error, never throws on missing/garbage balance or total). Full suite 52/54 (same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures — `pricing-model`, `scope-core`). Additionally verified with a real headless-Chromium (Playwright) run against the actual file, mocking the Worker's fetch responses — not just read by eye: reproduced Brett's exact screenshot scenario (final-balance Confirm on the Jamuna/Cesar row) and confirmed the Cancel→Close relabel, the Undo button's appearance, the confirm() dialog's exact wording, the unbook endpoint firing with the right id, and the modal closing afterward; separately confirmed the persistent list-level Undo link (final-balance line, deposit line untouched) fires the same way without the modal ever being reopened. `BUILD_VERSION` bumped to `2026-09-07.1`.

Human-gate required: YES (QuickBooks deletes — Rung 3, never autonomous, same gate as every booking call it undoes). No live QuickBooks credentials were available to this session, so the actual delete calls themselves are unverified against real QuickBooks data — **first live check for Brett**: book a low-stakes test deposit or final balance, confirm the new "Undo" appears and reads correctly, tap it, confirm the browser dialog wording, confirm, and check in QuickBooks that the invoice and bill are actually gone and the Signed Proposals row goes back to its pre-booking state. Separately worth testing the refusal path once real money has moved: undoing a booking that's already been paid against should come back with a clear "has a payment against it" error, not silently fail or silently succeed.

**147. Real "Send estimate" email — B-210's follow-on build, wiring the live Gmail send into the scope-proposal flow (Sep 7 2026, `worker.js` + `scope-creator.html`; new `test/scope-proposal-send.test.mjs`).** Earlier this same day, a separate session confirmed Gmail OAuth for `ridgecomaintenance@gmail.com` fully live end-to-end (`gmailSendEmail`, real test send, `message_id` confirmed) but flagged that it was only ever wired to ONE call site (the AR Report send) — `scope-creator.html`'s `copyProposal()` still only copied the proposal link to the clipboard, no send path existed. Brett: "resume ridgeco — build the send-estimate email."

- New `POST /scope/proposal/send {scope_id}` (admin-gated, mirrors `arReportSend`'s shape — not added to `PUBLIC_PATHS`, confirmed by grep before shipping, same WORKER_SECRET gate as `/scope/proposal/link`). Resolves the CONFIRMED PAYOR only — `Property.Owner_ID` → `Owners.Email` — never the Realtor/PM referral source (`Properties.Source_Email`), matching exactly what Section 6 "Confirm the payor" (`renderOwnerGate`, "Realtor ≠ payor") already enforces client-side. Re-checks both gates server-side (proposal text generated, owner email on file) rather than trusting client state — same defensive posture as every other customer-facing send in this file.
- **`scopeProposalResolveRecipient(prop, owners)`** — the recipient-resolution logic extracted as its own pure function (mirrors `qbResolveBillTo`'s shape) specifically so it's unit-tested without mocking Sheets/Gmail.
- Mints/reuses the SAME idempotent `Link_Rev`-scoped token `/scope/proposal/link` already uses via `scopeProposalLinkToken` — sending never mints a second, different link.
- **`buildScopeProposalEmailHtml()`** — house-style HTML mirroring `buildArReportEmailHtml`'s look (greeting + property address + a "View & Sign Estimate" button to `scope-proposal.html`). Deliberately carries NO pricing/vendor_cost data in the email body — only `ownerName`/`addr`/`url` — consistent with the existing customer-boundary privacy rule (rule 119's vendor-cost/note-field stripping).
- Stamps `Sent_Date`/`Sent_To`/`Send_Count` onto the `Scopes` row (additive, `ensureColumns`-guarded per rule 37) and logs every attempt — success AND failure — to a new self-provisioning `Proposal_Send_Log` tab, same convention as `AR_Report_Log`.
- `scope-creator.html`: new "📧 Send estimate to owner" button in Section 7, alongside the existing Save/Copy/Get-link row — `copyProposal()`/`getProposalLink()` are left fully in place, untouched, for anyone who still wants to hand-deliver a link (text message, etc.) rather than email it. `sendEstimateEmail()` reuses the existing client-side `ownerBillingStatus()` (the same helper the step-6 gate already runs) purely so the `confirm()` dialog can show the real recipient address before sending — worded differently if the estimate was already sent before ("Send it again?"). A persistent `renderSendStatus()` note ("✅ Sent to X on Y · sent Nx total") is hooked into `renderEditor()`'s render lifecycle right next to `loadSignatureIfSigned()`.

Regression check: `node --check` clean on `worker.js` and `scope-creator.html`'s inline script. New `test/scope-proposal-send.test.mjs` — 16 assertions covering `scopeProposalResolveRecipient` (normal case, no-Owner_ID, dangling Owner_ID, phone-only-no-email owner, whitespace-only email, null property, and — explicitly — that `Properties.Source_Email` never leaks in as the recipient) plus `buildScopeProposalEmailHtml`'s content (owner name, address, real link, CTA label, "Hi there," fallback). Full suite 44/46 (same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures — `pricing-model`, `scope-core` — no new regressions).
