Human-gate required: this is a real customer-facing send, gated behind Brett's own tap in the Hub (never autonomous) — same posture as every other customer email in this file. 🔴 **Needs Brett's first live pass**: on a real scope with the proposal already generated and a payor confirmed in step 6, tap "Send estimate to owner," confirm the dialog shows the right email, send it, and check the email actually lands looking right and the link opens `scope-proposal.html` correctly. Separately worth a second live check for the "already sent" resend path — confirm the persistent note appears after a successful send and the confirm-dialog wording changes on a second tap.

**155. Receipt Reconciler: materials-based descriptions, bill-on-match, business-expense receipts with no WO, and QB-email forwarding for reconciliation (Sep 10 2026, worker.js + receipt-reconciler.html).** Brett: the description bug from the unshipped Aug 31/Sep 2 fix (rule 140, `PASTE-THIS-TO-PUSH-receipt-reconciler-duplicate-checker.md`) was still live in production — confirmed by cloning `origin/main` fresh and reading the actual deployed code, not trusting the doc. That patch was apparently never pasted through claude.ai/code. This build re-does that fix plus three new asks from the same conversation.

- **Description bug, actually fixed this time**: `receiptExtract`'s prompt now returns `items_summary` — short generalized categories ("paint", "primer", "batteries") alongside the existing verbatim `items` — and `receipt-reconciler.html`'s description field defaults from `items_summary.join(', ')` instead of `suggested_wo.desc`. Falls back to the old (broken) default only for receipts scanned before this shipped, since they have no `items_summary` on file. `Receipt_Recon_Queue` gained an `Items_Summary` column (`ensureColumns`-guarded).
- **Bill-on-match**: new `appendReceiptToInvoiceReview(env, {wo_id, receipt_id, amount})`, called from `receiptReconConfirm` whenever a WO-tied receipt is confirmed. Finds the most-recently-approved active `Invoice_Review` row for that WO (never creates a second one — same "hand back the existing row" precedent `approveInvoiceReview` already uses) and appends the receipt to `Own_Material_IDs`, bumping both `Own_Materials` and `Customer_Total` by the exact receipt amount so `buildInvoiceLines`' rebuilt-total-must-match-recorded-total invariant (enforced in `qbRepairInvoice`) stays true. If the invoice hasn't been sent yet, nothing further happens — it rides along naturally at `qb/send-invoice` time (confirmed by reading that path: it reads `Own_Material_IDs` fresh at send, not at approval). If the invoice was ALREADY sent (`QB_Invoice_ID` set), sets new `Invoice_Review.Repair_Flagged`/`Repair_Reason` columns instead of touching QuickBooks directly. `qbRepairable`'s day-window skip (`when < cutoff`) now has a `&& !repairFlagged` escape hatch so a flagged row surfaces regardless of how old the original send was, and gets an explicit `'materials added since send'` issue so it's never silently filtered out by the existing description-diff check alone. `qbRepairInvoice` clears `Repair_Flagged` on a successful `apply:true`. Deliberately reuses `qbRepairInvoice`'s existing paid-invoice guard (refuses when `Balance !== TotalAmt`) rather than adding a second one — never touches a paid invoice, same as before.
- **Business-expense receipts, no WO required**: `addReceipt` no longer requires `wo_id` — only `amount`. New `Receipts` columns (`ensureColumns`-guarded): `Property_ID`, `Category`, `Source_File_ID`, `Source_File_URL`, `QB_Email_Sent`, `QB_Email_Sent_Date`. `receiptReconConfirm` accepts `no_wo:true` (skips the WO-exists check and the wo_id requirement entirely) plus an optional `property_id`. `receipt-reconciler.html`: the `'company'` category (previously hard-excluded — Skip was the only option) now renders its own lightweight form (optional Property, Amount/Store/Date/Description, no WO) with a "Confirm as business expense" button (`confirmBusinessExpense()`). `'refund'`/`'customer_paid'` are unchanged — still excluded/Skip-only.
- **QB email forwarding for reconciliation**: Brett wants every row that ever lands in the Receipts tab — WO-tied or not, already-existing or brand new — forwarded to QuickBooks' own receipts-capture inbox so it can be matched during bank/CC reconciliation (separate from, and in addition to, whatever else happens with it in the Hub). New `gmailSendEmailWithAttachment()` (multipart/mixed MIME, base64 attachment wrapped at 76 chars/line per RFC 2045; falls back to the existing `gmailSendEmail` when there's no attachment). New `sendReceiptsToQBEmail(env, {limit})` — reads `Config.qb_receipts_email` (set via the existing generic `/config/set`, no new config endpoint needed), sweeps `Receipts` rows where `QB_Email_Sent !== 'TRUE'`, resolves each one's source file (`Source_File_ID` directly, or `_recoverReceiptSourceFile()`'s best-effort match back to its `Receipt_Recon_Queue` origin row by WO+amount for older rows that predate this build), sends with the image attached where recoverable (text-only otherwise — never skipped for lack of an image), paced 400ms apart, marks `QB_Email_Sent` on success, leaves failures unmarked so a retry sweep picks them up. Bounded per call (default 25, max 200) since a Worker invocation has a real time limit — a large backlog clears over several sweeps, not one call. New `POST /receipts/send-to-qb-email`; wired into the existing daily digest cron alongside `purgeConfirmedDuplicateReceipts`. Running it once (or letting the cron run) is also the full backfill Brett asked for — every existing Receipts row, including ones already reconciled to a WO from before this build, starts with `QB_Email_Sent` unset and gets swept the same way new ones do.

Regression check: `node --check` clean on `worker.js`; extracted the one inline `<script>` block in `receipt-reconciler.html`, clean. Full suite 50/50 (same 2 pre-existing unrelated failures noted in every prior RidgeCo doc — `pricing-model`, `scope-core` — not touched by this build, and not present as failures in this run's own count since the suite reports 50/50 clean). `BUILD_VERSION` bumped to `2026-09-10.1`.

**NOT verified — no live Gmail/Drive/QuickBooks credentials in the build sandbox**: the actual MIME/attachment send to Gmail, the Drive download for older-row file recovery, and the qb/repairable widen-query change against a real already-sent invoice. Brett still needs to supply `qb_receipts_email` (from QuickBooks' Receipts → Forward from email page) and confirm `ridgecomaintenance@gmail.com` is the pre-authorized sending address on that same QB page — QuickBooks silently bounces forwarded receipts from an unregistered sender. 🔴 **Needs Brett's first live pass, in this order**: (1) set `qb_receipts_email` via `/config/set` and authorize the Gmail sender in QuickBooks, (2) confirm a receipt scanned through the Reconciler now shows a real materials description by default, (3) confirm a WO business-expense receipt with `no_wo:true` posts and appears with `Category:'company'`, (4) run `/receipts/send-to-qb-email` once and check a forwarded receipt actually lands in QuickBooks' Receipts inbox with its image attached, (5) on a WO whose invoice was already sent, confirm a new receipt, verify `Repair_Flagged` gets set, then run the existing Repairable Invoices check and push it — confirming the SAME QuickBooks invoice updates rather than a new one being created.

Human-gate required: YES for anything QuickBooks-facing (Rung 3 — repairing/pushing an updated invoice is still Brett's own explicit `apply:true`, never automatic). The QB-email forwarding itself is not a QuickBooks API write (it's an email with an attachment) so it runs unattended off the daily cron, same posture as `receiptReconScan`.

**155a. Live-pass follow-up to rule 155 (Sep 10 2026): fixed a real Cloudflare subrequest-limit bug in `sendReceiptsToQBEmail`, caught by actually running it against production.** Brett provided `qb_receipts_email` and a working `WORKER_SECRET`. Live-verified in order: `/health` (Gmail fully configured), added 1864 Kerns School Rd (Milam Ridge) via `/property/add` — Property ID 85, no `Owner_ID`/`QBO_Customer_ID` — confirmed by reading it back. Set `Config.qb_receipts_email` via the existing `/config/set`, confirmed by reading it back. A 2-receipt test batch sent clean (1 with its image attached, 1 text-only — the recovery match genuinely doesn't always find a file, expected for a row with no queue origin). A follow-up 25-receipt batch (the rule-155 default) failed partway through: 15 sent, then 10 failed with `Too many subrequests by single Worker invocation` starting at receipt #22. Root cause: each receipt costs ~3-4 subrequests (driveDownload + gmail send + `updateRow`'s own read-then-write), plus ~5 for the initial config/tab reads — a batch of 25 could need 75-100+, well past this account's apparent per-invocation budget. Nothing was lost or double-sent (failures leave `QB_Email_Sent` unset, same as designed, so they'll pick up on the next call) — but the 25 default was wrong. Fixed: default dropped to 8, hard cap to 10 (comfortably under budget even worst-case, all-attachments). `node --check` clean, full suite still 50/50 (unrelated to this change). `BUILD_VERSION` → `2026-09-10.2`. Backlog resumed afterward in 8-10-sized batches — see CURRENT.md for the running total.

Human-gate required: no new QuickBooks writes here (still just email forwarding), but this is the first live confirmation the actual Gmail-send/attachment path works end-to-end in production, not just against `node --check` and offline tests.

**156. Configurable multi-milestone payment schedules for signed Scope Proposals — replaces the fixed 50% deposit/50% final split (Sep 14 2026, worker.js + scope-creator.html + scope-proposal.html + signed-proposals.html; new test/payment-schedule.test.mjs, test/manual-verify-payment-schedule.mjs, test/manual-verify-milestone-billing-ui.mjs).** Brett: needs presets (1/3, 1/4) plus full custom schedules — e.g. 50% upfront + 25% at a progress milestone he triggers himself + 25% at completion — with the vendor side always matching the customer's milestone against the VENDOR's own original estimate (so Ridge Co's markup stays separate at every draw, not just at the end), grouped billing when several milestones are ready at once ("jobs sometimes run faster than I can physically check"), and a Maryland home-improvement-law deposit cap (Md. Bus. Reg. §8-617(b): 1/3 of contract price max, collected before/at signing) enforced as a warning, never a hard block — his explicit call, since materials-heavy small/midsize jobs often need more up front than a pure-labor big renovation would.

- **New `Payment_Milestones` tab** — one row per milestone per signature (`Scope_ID`, `Signature_ID`, `Label`, `Percent`, `Trigger` [upfront/manual/completion], `Sequence`, `Customer_Amount`, `Vendor_Amount`, `Status` [pending/billed], `QB_Invoice_ID/Number`, `QB_Bill_ID/Number`, `Billed_Date`). Additive and fully backward-compatible by design: a `Scope_Signatures` row signed BEFORE this shipped has zero milestone rows and keeps using the existing `scopeProposalBook`/`scopeProposalBookFinal` 2-stage path completely untouched — `signed-proposals.html` branches per-row on whether `milestones.length` is nonzero, so nothing already in flight changes behavior.
- **New `Scopes.Payment_Schedule_JSON`** (`ensureColumns`-guarded) — an array of `{label, percent, trigger}`, editable per scope via a new Payment Schedule picker in `scope-creator.html` Section 7 (presets: 1/3 default, 1/4, Custom row-builder with add/remove/live-total). Default when never set: `[{Deposit, 33.34%, upfront}, {Final, 66.66%, completion}]` — replacing the OLD hardcoded 50/50 split system-wide, not just as an option.
- **Pure helpers** (unit-tested in isolation via the same `grab()`/`new Function()` technique as `test/scope-book.test.mjs`): `scopeDefaultPaymentSchedule`, `scopeValidatePaymentSchedule` (percentages must sum to 100 within 0.1 rounding tolerance — the last entry absorbs drift under that, anything further off is REJECTED rather than silently renormalized, since that would change what a customer is agreeing to pay without them seeing it; upfront-trigger total over `MAX_UPFRONT_DEPOSIT_PCT` — Config-overridable, defaults to 33.34 — is a warning, never a rejection), `scopeComputeMilestoneAmounts` (last milestone absorbs the rounding remainder so amounts always foot EXACTLY to the subtotal/vendor-cost-total, never a cent short or over).
- `scopeProposal` (customer proposal text generator) and `scopeProposalView` (the public e-sign page's data source) both now build the real schedule instead of a hardcoded "Required 50% Deposit" line — the proposal DOCUMENT ITSELF, and the live/signed customer-facing page, both show the actual configured milestones with real dollar amounts.
- `scopeProposalSign` expands the scope's schedule into individual `Payment_Milestones` rows at signing time (customer_amount off the signed subtotal, vendor_amount off the vendor's own signed cost total — same % applied independently to each side's own base, which is what keeps Ridge Co's markup separate at every draw per Brett's explicit requirement). A milestone-row creation failure never undoes the signature itself (already-committed, wrapped in try/catch) — the empty milestones array is itself the visible signal rather than a silent swallow.
- **New `scopeProposalBillMilestones` (`POST /scope-proposal/bill-milestones`, admin)** — generalizes `scopeProposalBook`'s exact safety shape (preview-first, invoice persisted before bill is attempted so a bill failure can never duplicate the invoice on retry, bill failure never hides/rolls back an already-created invoice) to bill ONE OR SEVERAL pending milestones together as a single grouped QB invoice + single grouped QB vendor bill (one Line item per milestone in each, so itemization stays visible even when grouped) — this is Brett's "combine in one bill" requirement. Rejects outright (never silently partial-processes) if any requested milestone belongs to a different signature or isn't `pending`.
- `scopeProposalSignedList` now attaches each signature's `milestones` array (sorted by `Sequence`) to its row.
- `signed-proposals.html`: milestone rows render as a checklist (label/%/$/status chip per milestone, already-billed rows disabled so they can't be double-billed) with a "Bill selected (N)" button that enables/updates live as you check pending milestones, reusing the existing preview→confirm modal (dispatched via a new `modalMode`/`confirmModalAction` so the one shared modal serves both the legacy book/book-final flow and the new milestone flow without duplicating modal markup). **Known gap, flagged not hidden: no Undo yet for a grouped milestone bill** (unlike the legacy deposit/final flow, which has one) — `#m-undo` deliberately stays hidden after a milestone confirm rather than being wired to something that doesn't exist.
- `scope-proposal.html` (customer-facing signing page): replaced the hardcoded "50% deposit" total line, payment-terms bullet, and agree-checkbox wording with the real schedule, rendered milestone-by-milestone with due-timing labels (due now / due at completion / billed once reached). Recomputes live off `currentTotals()` exactly as the old deposit math did, so picking a different item variant (Repair vs Replace, etc.) before signing correctly re-prices every milestone's dollar amount, not just the total. The already-signed/locked view renders the LOCKED schedule from the signature's actual `Payment_Milestones` rows (falls back to the live default schedule only for a pre-existing signature that predates this build and has none).

Verified: `node --check` clean on `worker.js`; all three touched HTML files' inline `<script>` blocks extracted and checked clean (scope-creator.html, scope-proposal.html, signed-proposals.html — 1 block each). New `test/payment-schedule.test.mjs` — 24 assertions on the pure helpers directly (Brett's own 50/25/25 example validates with a warning not a rejection; a 1/3 preset with hand-rounded percents normalizes correctly; a subtotal that doesn't divide evenly by 3 still foots exactly with zero rounding drift on both the customer and vendor sides; an unrecognized/missing trigger defaults to `manual`, never silently `upfront`). Full existing suite re-run clean at 51/51 (previously-noted `pricing-model`/`scope-core` failures are no longer present in this baseline — appear to have been fixed in an intervening session; not touched by this build either way). Two new real-browser (Playwright, mocked Worker responses) verifications, same technique as rule 146's: `test/manual-verify-payment-schedule.mjs` (13 checks — schedule renders with correct labels/%/$ on the customer page, live variant-toggle recalculation is correct for every milestone not just the total, the old "Required 50% deposit" text is confirmed GONE from actual rendered output, and the locked/signed view correctly renders the persisted milestone amounts) and `test/manual-verify-milestone-billing-ui.mjs` (13 checks — milestone checklist renders correct status per row, an already-billed milestone's checkbox is correctly disabled, the Bill-selected button's enable state and count track selection accurately, the preview modal shows the right customer/vendor split, and — the one that matters most — the actual commit call sends exactly the right `signature_id`/`milestone_ids` payload and nothing else).

**NOT verified — no live Google Sheets/QuickBooks credentials in the build sandbox**: creating the `Payment_Milestones` tab live, an actual signature producing real milestone rows, and a real grouped QuickBooks invoice+bill from `/scope-proposal/bill-milestones`. **🔴 Needs Brett's first live pass, in this order**: (1) open an existing or new scope in `scope-creator.html`, confirm the Payment Schedule picker loads the default (33.34%/66.66%) and that switching presets + saving works and reads back correctly; (2) generate a proposal and confirm the proposal text and the customer-facing link both show the real schedule, not "50% deposit"; (3) sign a test proposal and confirm `Payment_Milestones` rows actually get created on the live Sheet with correct dollar amounts; (4) on Signed Proposals, check one milestone and Bill it, confirm the QuickBooks invoice + vendor bill actually land with the right amounts and the vendor's bill reflects THEIR estimate, not the customer's marked-up price; (5) check two pending milestones together and confirm they land as ONE grouped invoice with two line items, not two separate invoices.

Human-gate required: YES (QuickBooks invoice/bill creation — Rung 3, never autonomous, same posture as `scopeProposalBook`/`scopeProposalBookFinal`). The MD deposit-cap warning is informational only per Brett's explicit decision — it does not block saving a schedule or billing a milestone, and this is NOT legal advice; Brett is confirming the specific contract language and the 1/3 cap's applicability to his mostly-landlord customer base separately.

**Still open, explicitly out of scope for this build (Brett's own phased plan):** vendor draw requests with required photo evidence (email now, SMS-ready channel), and the Owner Change / Vendor Change order system (contingency-clause language, per-change decide-to-charge). Both scoped and agreed in conversation, neither started.

**158. Invoice descriptions now come from what was actually logged, not a generic WO field — plus a private/customer-facing split on every notes field (Sep 14 2026, worker.js + index.html + vendor.html + wo.html; new test/labor-description.test.mjs).** Brett: logging hours with real per-entry detail ("diagnose" one day, "return with parts and complete the repairs" two hours later) never reached the customer invoice — the only text that ever showed up was a single WO-level field he had to hand-retype every time. Root-caused from live code, not guessed: `buildInvoiceLines` never read `Time_Entries.Notes` or `Vendor_Bills.Notes` at all — the sole source was `wo.Invoice_Memo || wo.Description`, populated by nothing except a manual textarea edit in Invoice Review. Same gap on the vendor side — vendors already had a Notes field on their bill submission (index.html, vendor.html, wo.html share-link) that was equally disconnected from anything a customer ever saw.

- **Two-field split, everywhere a note gets typed**: a new `Invoice_Description` column (customer-facing — "Describe what was done in as much detail as possible") sits alongside the existing `Notes` column (now clearly labeled private/internal, e.g. "why it took 5 hours instead of 2") on both `Time_Entries` and `Vendor_Bills`. Additive only, `ensureColumns`-guarded per rule 37 — no existing column touched. Applied to every place hours or a bill get logged: the Hub's own Log Time form and vendor-bill panel (index.html), the vendor PIN portal's time log and Submit Bill modal (vendor.html), and the no-login WO-share-link bill form (wo.html).
- **New `buildLaborDescription(billRow, timeEntries, wo)`** — the actual fix. Compiles the labor line's description from every time entry linked to THAT bill (`Bill_ID` match, date-ordered: `"8/12 — Diagnose; 8/14 — Return with parts and complete the repairs"`), with the bill's own `Invoice_Description` leading when set (the flat-rate / no-time-entry case — trash service, a lump-sum vendor bill). Falls back to the old `wo.Invoice_Memo || wo.Description` ONLY when neither exists, so nothing already sent regresses. Wired into all four places that build an invoice line (`qbSendInvoice`, `qbSendCombinedInvoice`, `qbRepairable`, `qbRepairInvoice`) — each now fetches `Time_Entries` alongside its other tabs and passes the WO's entries through.
- **Decision locked with Brett**: one combined labor line (not a line per time entry) — avoids a real markup-leak risk on marked-up vendor bills (splitting hours × the vendor's own rate per line would expose vendor economics the existing code deliberately hides), and matches how materials lines already stay separate.
- **A Spanish-speaking vendor's Invoice_Description translates straight to English** in `woSharedBill` (customer-facing, unlike Notes/Truck_Desc which keep the existing bilingual `[ES]/[EN]` internal-record pattern — a customer should never see Spanish text they didn't ask for).
- **Invoice Review pre-fill**: the memo box (`inv-memo-`) now defaults to the same compiled description instead of the old WO-level text, via a new `invComposeMemo`/`invRefreshMemoDefault` pair that only fills the box when it's still empty or holds its own last auto-fill (same "isOurs" guard the price-suggestion box already uses) — a hand-typed memo is never overwritten. Still fully editable before send, unchanged from before (Brett's explicit requirement). Known limitation: the front-end pre-fill only composes from time entries already linked to a real `Vendor_Bills` row; the rarer "tick hours on top with no formal bill" path still shows the old default in the box (still editable, nothing lost — the backend `buildInvoiceLines` is unaffected by this front-end-only gap since it recomputes independently at send time).
- Also visible now in the Hub's Time Tracking list on the WO: each entry shows its Invoice Description (📝, customer-facing) and its private Notes (italic) as two distinct lines, not one blended note.

Regression check: `node --check` clean on worker.js and all 14 inline `<script>` blocks across index.html/vendor.html/wo.html. Two existing tests (`combined-invoice.test.mjs`, `invoice-hours.test.mjs`) eval-extract `buildInvoiceLines` in isolation via the `grab()`/`new Function()` technique and broke when it gained a dependency on the new helper — fixed by grabbing `buildLaborDescription` alongside it, same pattern used everywhere else in this suite. New `test/labor-description.test.mjs` (8 assertions) tests `buildLaborDescription` directly: chronological compilation, bill-description-leads-when-set, both combined, wrong-bill exclusion, inactive-entry exclusion, blank-description entries skipped (not rendered as an empty segment), and the two fallback paths (wo.Invoice_Memo preferred over wo.Description; old jobs with neither field regress to nothing, unchanged from before). Full suite: **59/59 passing** — the previously-noted `pricing-model`/`scope-core` baseline failures are confirmed gone as of this session (not touched by this build either way; already fixed by whatever landed between rule 156 and this one). `BUILD_VERSION` → `2026-09-14.3` (`.2` was already taken by rule 157's Twilio SMS build on this same base).

**NOT verified — no live Google Sheets/QuickBooks credentials in the build sandbox**: the actual `Invoice_Description` column creation on the live Sheet, a real compiled description reaching a real QuickBooks invoice line, and the Invoice Review memo pre-fill against real bill/time-entry data in the browser. 🔴 **Needs Brett's first live pass, in this order**: (1) log two time entries on a real job with different Invoice Descriptions, turn them into a bill, and confirm the Invoice Review memo box pre-fills with both, compiled and dated; (2) send that invoice and confirm the QuickBooks line description actually shows the compiled text, not the old WO description; (3) as a vendor (vendor.html or the wo.html share link), submit a bill with an Invoice Description and confirm it shows up for Brett to review before send; (4) confirm a job with NEITHER field filled in still books exactly as before (regression check on a real job, not just the offline fallback tests).

Human-gate required: no new QuickBooks writes here (still the same existing send/repair gates, untouched — Rung 3, never autonomous). This build only changes what TEXT lands in an invoice line that a human still has to tap Confirm/Approve to actually send.

**162. Work Order Void/Hide (distinct from Cancelled) + a duplicate-submission guard on WO creation (Sep 14 2026, worker.js + index.html; new test/wo-void.test.mjs, extended test/dupe-guard.test.mjs, fixed test/turnover.test.mjs).** Brett hit WO-1192 live: a double-tap on Create Work Order produced two rows sharing the SAME auto-incremented WO number, and every WO lookup in the app (`findWO`, `updateWOFields`' rowIndex, `updateRow`) resolves by ID with a plain first-match `.find()`/`.findIndex()` — so no button could ever reach the second row again, no matter which card Brett tapped Cancel on. Root-caused from the live code AND the live Sheet data (fetched via `/workorders` with the stored `WORKER_SECRET`), not guessed: one row had gotten a vendor assigned and an SMS sent before being set to Cancelled; the other, with no vendor, sat permanently stuck at New. Confirmed vendor #1 on that job is Brett's own in-house record, so no outside vendor was actually disturbed. Brett fixed the live duplicate row by hand (this build does not touch production data); the ask from there was two-fold: stop this from happening again, and add a real Void/Hide mechanism distinct from Cancelled — Cancelled means a real job that was issued and then called off; Void means a work order that should never have existed at all (a duplicate create, or one job folded into another) — the record stays, it's just gone from every list/search by default, restorable, never mixed into the existing "closed" bucket (Complete/Invoiced/Paid/Cancelled).

- **Duplicate-submission guard**: `createWorkOrder` now calls the existing `findRecentDuplicate` helper (already used by Receipts/Time_Entries/Vendor_Bills/Estimates, never wired to Work_Orders before this) — same property/unit/tenant/trade/description/type within 30 seconds is treated as the original request landing twice, and hands back the existing WO's id instead of appending a twin. This is what actually prevents a repeat of WO-1192 at the source; it does NOT retroactively fix an existing same-ID collision (voiding, like every other lookup, still resolves by ID first-match).
- **New `Work_Orders` columns** (additive, `ensureColumns`-guarded): `Voided` (TRUE/FALSE, independent of `Status` — a voided WO keeps whatever status it had), `Void_Reason` (Duplicate/Combined/Other), `Void_Reason_Detail`, `Void_Combined_Into_WO_ID`, `Voided_By`, `Voided_Date`.
- **`POST /wo/void`** — validates the reason enum, requires `combined_into_wo_id` when reason is Combined, logs to `WO_Audit` (field `Voided`, `FALSE`→`TRUE`, notes = reason/detail/target) same as every other status change. When Combined, copies this WO's `Notes` onto the surviving WO using the same timestamped-prefix convention `appendWONotes` already uses (`[date — combined from WO-XXXX] ...`) — Brett's call: notes move, nothing else (vendor bills/receipts/time entries stay attached to their original WO_ID, no data migrates).
- **`POST /wo/unvoid`** — flips `Voided` back to `FALSE`; the reason/detail/target/by/date fields are left in place as history (voided on X for Y, restored on Z) rather than cleared.
- **Both admin-only by construction** — not added to any entry in `ROLE_SCOPES` (vendor/tenant/owner), so a PIN-session token can't reach them; only the `WORKER_SECRET` admin gate can.
- **Filtering, everywhere Work_Orders gets listed**: `/workorders` excludes `Voided=TRUE` by default (`?include_voided=1` / `?voided_only=1` for the Hub's own dedicated filter), and `/vendor-workorders`, `/tenant-workorders`, `/owner-workorders`, `/nearby-wos` all exclude voided rows unconditionally — regardless of `include_closed`, since Voided isn't a closed status, it's removed from view entirely.
- **index.html**: new "Voided" option on the Work Orders status filter — deliberately excluded from "All Statuses" too, only reachable by name, per Brett's own spec ("searched or filtered but only if looking for that specific status"). Dashboard counts exclude voided WOs. `woCard()` shows a "VOIDED — reason" badge. WO detail screen gets a "🗑 Void / Hide" button (opens a reason picker: Duplicate / Combined-with-picker / Other-with-textarea) or a "↩ Restore" button when already voided. The Combined picker is a client-side filter against already-loaded `state.workorders` scoped to the same Property_ID/Unit_ID — deliberately NOT `/nearby-wos`, which is the opposite scope (other NEARBY properties, for job bundling), corrected from an earlier assumption stated to Brett mid-build. Four other WO-picker dropdowns in the admin app (property list open-count, unit page open WOs, move-in/out panel, bundling opportunities) also now exclude voided rows, and all four places that load `state.workorders` request the full set (`?include_voided=1`) so the client-side toggle has real data to work with regardless of which load path ran.

Regression check: `node --check` clean on worker.js and all 5 inline `<script>` blocks in index.html. This originally shipped as rule 159, but that number turned out to already be claimed (twice, by two different renumbering passes) by the notify-toggle/bulk-checkbox/Twilio-status work from a separate concurrent session — renumbered to 162 (the next free number after rule 161) once that collision was found. Rebased three times mid-build across the day's concurrent pushes: rule 158's invoice-description compiling, rule 158/158a's (→159/159a's) notify-toggle work, and rule 160/161's Twilio-diagnostic + whole-property-SMS fixes. `createWorkOrder` never had a real logical conflict with any of them — each session's changes sit at a different point in the function; the actual conflicts were `BUILD_VERSION` line collisions (bumped past whatever the incoming tip claimed each time) and two content conflicts on the third rebase (`context/CURRENT.md`'s status log, and `woCard()` in index.html where the concurrent session's repositioned bulk-select checkbox and this build's new "VOIDED — reason" badge sit on adjacent lines of the same returned string) — both resolved by keeping both sides' changes. `test/turnover.test.mjs` sandbox-extracts `createWorkOrder` verbatim and broke when it gained the `findRecentDuplicate` dependency — fixed by grabbing that function alongside it, same technique used throughout this suite. Extended `test/dupe-guard.test.mjs` with the WO-1192 scenario itself (same job caught within the window; different job, different property, and outside the window all correctly NOT blocked). New `test/wo-void.test.mjs` (extracts `woVoid`/`woUnvoid`/`getWorkOrdersList` verbatim against a fake Sheets backend, 25 assertions): reason validation, Combined requiring a target id, Notes get copied onto the surviving WO with the combined-from prefix, restore preserves void history, and the three list modes (default-excludes/include_voided/voided_only) all return the right set. Full suite (`node --test test/*.test.mjs`), re-verified after all three rebases: **62/62 passing**, zero failures.

**NOT verified — no live Google Sheets credentials in the build sandbox**: creating the six new columns on the live Sheet, an actual double-tap create against the live Worker, and a real Void→Restore round trip in the browser. 🔴 **Needs Brett's first live pass, in this order**: (1) rapid-double-tap Create Work Order on a test job and confirm only ONE row appears (no more WO-1192s); (2) Void a real work order with each of the three reasons — confirm Duplicate/Other save cleanly, and confirm Combined's WO picker only lists other work orders at that same property/unit and that its Notes actually land on the target WO with the combined-from prefix; (3) confirm a voided WO disappears from the main list, from "All Statuses", from the vendor/tenant/owner portals (if reachable for that job), and from the Log Time / Add Receipt / Assign Vendor pickers, and only reappears when "Voided" is explicitly selected; (4) Restore a voided WO and confirm it reappears everywhere it should, with its void history still visible if you look at the audit log.

Human-gate required: no QuickBooks/payment writes here at all — Void/Unvoid only touch Work_Orders and WO_Audit. Still worth a live pass before trusting it on a real duplicate, same as any new write path.

**171. Property Structure management — Unit_Count auto-compute + Units add/rename/remove + tenant unit reassignment (Sep 14 2026, index.html only; no worker.js changes needed — /property/update, /unit/add, /unit/update, /tenant/update were already generic fields-passthrough).** Brett hit this live on 1109 Battery Ave (Property 84): converted it from single-family to multi-unit via the Type dropdown (which already worked), then typed a new Unit_Count directly into the spreadsheet because the Edit Property modal never exposed that field for editing at all — but nothing created matching Units rows, and the one existing tenant (Reagan, ID 102) stayed linked at the property level with no Unit_ID and no UI path to move her into a real unit. Root-caused from live code, not guessed: `submitEditProperty()`'s fields object never included `Unit_Count` (creation-only); `/unit/add` already existed on the backend but was only ever called from the two bulk CSV importers (data-importer.html, patch-importer.html), never from the main Hub; the only tenant-movement UI anywhere was Schedule Move-Out/Turnover, which deactivates a tenant rather than relinking an active one. Confirmed live via the stored `WORKER_SECRET` (read-only): Property 84 is Type=multi, Unit_Count=2, zero `Units` rows, tenant 102 linked at property level with blank `Unit_ID` — this build fixes the tool; the Battery Ave record itself was fixed live Sep 16 (Brett supplied "Apt 1"/"Apt 2" + confirmed Reagan is in Apt 2; Unit 55/56 created and linked via `/unit/add`+`/tenant/update`) — full detail [FL-20260916-2150-t8].

- **Unit_Count**: auto-computed (count of active `Units` rows) by default for `multi`/`condo` property types, shown read-only with a "Set count manually" override that reveals a number input. If the manual value stops matching the real unit count, a visible warning shows immediately instead of the two silently drifting apart — the exact failure mode that motivated this build.
- **Units management** (new section in the Edit Property modal, shown only for multi/condo): lists active units with their current tenant or "vacant"; Add Unit (free-text label — no forced naming convention, so "Apt 1" vs "1st Floor" stays Brett's call per property); Rename; Remove.
- **Remove-unit hard block** (Brett's call): refuses and names the specific blocker if an active tenant, an open work order, or an active key is still attached to that unit.
- **Tenant → Unit reassignment**: new Unit dropdown in the Edit Tenant modal, scoped to the tenant's own property's units. Changing it prompts a plain-language confirm, then relinks both sides of the bidirectional link (`Tenants.Unit_ID`, and `Units.Tenant_ID` on both the old and new unit) using the same clear-old/set-new sequencing already proven in the existing move-in flow (`submitMoveIn`). **Same-property only for v1** — cross-property moves are explicitly labeled "not supported here yet" in the UI rather than silently failing; logged as B-237 for a future build.

Verification: `node --check` clean on all 5 inline `<script>` blocks in index.html. Full suite `node --test test/*.test.mjs`: 65/65 passing, zero regressions (worker.js untouched — every endpoint this build calls was already a generic fields-passthrough). Also wrote a scripted jsdom-based logic test in the build sandbox (not added to the committed suite — would introduce jsdom as a new devDependency this repo doesn't otherwise use; flagging for Brett to decide whether to formalize it) covering unit-count auto-compute/override/warning, add/rename/remove-unit including all three remove guards, and the full tenant-reassignment flow (unit unchanged = exactly 1 api call; unit changed = exactly 3 calls in the right order — tenant update, clear old unit, set new unit; cancelled confirm = 0 calls) — 14/14 passing, after catching and fixing two bugs in the test harness itself (not the shipped code) along the way.

**NOT verified — no live Google Sheets write access from the build sandbox beyond the diagnostic `WORKER_SECRET` reads logged above**: an actual Add/Rename/Remove Unit against the live Sheet, and a real tenant-reassignment round trip in the browser. 🔴 Needs Brett's first live pass: (1) open Edit Property on a multi/condo property and confirm the unit count + list render correctly; (2) add a real unit, confirm it shows up in Units and in the Edit Tenant dropdown for that property; (3) reassign a real tenant to a different unit and confirm both the tenant record and both units' `Tenant_ID` end up correct; (4) try removing a unit that still has a tenant/open WO/key attached and confirm it's actually blocked with a clear message.

Human-gate: no QuickBooks/payment writes anywhere in this build — Properties/Units/Tenants only, same rung as any other structural data edit. Still worth the live pass above before trusting it on a real property.

**176. Receipt "View" link blank/broken after a vendor submits a bill (Sep 16 2026, vendor.html + index.html; no worker.js changes needed — `/vendor-file/view` already existed from rule 142).** Brett reported live: Alex Busey said submitting receipts "removes permission to view them"; Brett confirmed the same links were blank on his own end too. Root-caused against the real WO-1091 (2930 St Paul Apt 3) Vendor_Bills row, not guessed: receipt/bill/invoice files are deliberately never made Drive-shareable (rule 13), and rule 142 (Sep 15) already fixed the photo-gallery lightbox to route through the authenticated `/vendor-file/view` proxy instead of linking straight to Drive — but the **bill-summary "View" link**, which only renders *after* a bill is submitted (`vendor.html`'s `loadVendorBillSummary`, `index.html`'s WO-detail vendor-bills panel and `renderReceiptsJsonBreakdown` on Review Bills), was never touched by that fix. All three still built an `<a href>` straight to the raw, never-shared `Vendor_Bills.Receipts_JSON[].url` — which is exactly why it read as "submitting removes permission": nothing was ever revoked, the link simply hadn't been clicked until the post-submit summary card rendered it for the first time.

- `vendor.html`: new `driveIdFromDriveUrl(u)` helper (pulls the Drive file id out of a stored `.../file/d/<id>/view` URL) so `loadVendorBillSummary`'s receipt lines can route through the existing `internalFileUrl(woId, fileId)` proxy instead of `r.url` directly.
- `index.html`: new admin-side equivalent `internalReceiptUrl(woId, driveUrl)` (same id-extraction, but builds the `?t=` query param off `AUTH_TOKEN` instead of a vendor session token) — fixed both live render sites (the WO-detail vendor-bills panel and `renderReceiptsJsonBreakdown`, used on Review Bills). A third, byte-identical occurrence in `voidVendorBill`'s legacy re-render path was left untouched — it sits after an unconditional `return;` and is dead code (see rule 162's area), not worth touching for this fix.
- `BUILD_VERSION` → `2026-09-16.7` so both portals' auto-refresh picks this up without a manual reload.

Verified: `node --check` clean on all 7 `vendor.html` and all 5 `index.html` inline `<script>` blocks. Live-verified against the real WO-1091 data that triggered the report — all 3 of Alex Busey's receipt photos (`file_type=receipt`) return real image bytes (confirmed JPEGs, Samsung Galaxy S23, ~4-4.6MB each) through `/vendor-file/view` with the admin token; nothing was lost on the Drive side, this was purely the link never being routed through the proxy. `/version` polled and confirmed `2026-09-16.7` live ~25s after push.

Human-gate required: no — read-only rendering fix, no new write path, no QuickBooks/money logic touched.

**[FL-20260916-2330-r7-follow-up] Receipt-reconciler duplicate checker — rebuilt from scratch (Sep 16 2026, worker.js + receipt-reconciler.html; new test/receipt-duplicate-checker.test.mjs).** The Sep 2 original (documented in `ridgeco-receipt-reconciler-unit-fix.md`'s "Fix 2") never existed anywhere in git history — confirmed via `git log --all`, nothing — it only ever shipped as a delivered paste-ready file Brett apparently never pasted into claude.ai/code. The unit-search half of that same Sep 2 session was separately rebuilt Sep 14 (rule 173); this closes the other half, matching the original 3-decision design exactly: check all 3 layers Brett named (other Receipts at the property, QuickBooks Bills/Expenses, QuickBooks invoice lines), on-demand only (real cost-metered QB reads, never automatic), keep confirmed duplicates forever (already true via the existing 180-day-retention `duplicate_confirmed` status, untouched by this build).

**Layer 1 — property-wide, not just same-WO.** New pure `receiptDuplicatesAtProperty(receipt, allReceipts, workorders)` — wider than the existing `receiptIsDuplicate` (same-WO-only). Catches the real gap that check misses: a receipt entered against the wrong WO at a property running several jobs at once. Refuses to scan without a resolved property + date (unsafe to guess). A candidate's property resolves via its own WO's `Property_ID` when the Receipts row itself predates that column.

**Layer 2 — QuickBooks Bills/Expenses.** `qbDuplicateBillsNear`/`qbDuplicateExpensesNear` — a 3-day date window at the exact amount, not scoped by vendor (Bill.VendorRef needs a resolved QBO vendor id this check has to work without).

**Layer 3 — QuickBooks customer invoice lines (the pre-Hub-bookkeeping case).** `qbAllInvoicesRaw` pulls a broad, list-only Invoice query ONCE per batch call (mirrors `arInvoices`' own "SELECT * / filter in code" convention — naming `Line` directly can fault QBO's parser); pure `qbInvoiceCandidatesByDate` narrows to a date window (45 days); `qbInvoiceLineDuplicates` then opens each date-proximate candidate INDIVIDUALLY (the actual "real API cost" step the original design flagged) and scans its real `Line` array for a matching `SalesItemLineDetail` amount — capped at 5 opens per receipt.

**Orchestration + endpoints.** `receiptCheckDuplicatesOne(env, row, opts)` combines all 3 into one flat evidence array; `opts.token`/`opts.allInvoices` let a batch caller share one QB token + one Invoice pull across every row instead of refetching per row. New `POST /receipt-recon/check-duplicates` (single) and `/receipt-recon/check-duplicates-bulk` (capped at 5 ids/call — same "click again to continue" shape as the existing Drive-sharing repair tool, not a one-shot sweep). Evidence persists onto the queue row (`Duplicate_Evidence_JSON`, `Duplicate_Checked_Date`, both additive via `ensureColumns`) so it survives a page refresh. `receiptReconConfirmDuplicate` now accepts an optional `reason` from the matched evidence — Brett's own "there may be reason to reference it later" ask — instead of always writing the generic note.

**Frontend.** `receipt-reconciler.html` gets a "🔍 Check duplicates" button on every pending row (any category, not just the ones the old same-WO flag already caught) and, once a check has run, an evidence block — one card per signal with its own "It's this one" button. The reason text is looked up from a JS-side `rowEvidence` cache by index rather than threaded through the `onclick` string directly, since a QuickBooks vendor name or invoice-line description can contain quote characters that would break a naive inline literal.

New `test/receipt-duplicate-checker.test.mjs` (11 assertions) covers the two pure helpers directly — `receiptCheckDuplicatesOne` and the QB-calling functions all need live QuickBooks credentials no build sandbox has, same as every other QB-touching build in this repo. Full suite 77/77 post-build (was 76/76 before this feature, +1 new test file). `node --check` clean on worker.js and receipt-reconciler.html's inline script.

**Caught and fixed a real bug before calling this done**: an intermediate patch used the `receiptSuggestCore` function's own declaration line as unique `old_str` anchor context but omitted it from the replacement, silently deleting that line and leaving its body orphaned underneath the new code. The full suite caught this immediately (75/76 the moment it landed, not the expected 76/76) — fixed and re-verified clean (76/76, then 77/77 once the test file landed) on a fresh independent clone before moving on, not just trusted.

Human-gate required: NO for the code path itself (every new endpoint is read-only against QuickBooks — no Bill/Expense/Invoice write anywhere in this build; the only write is the existing, unchanged `receiptReconConfirmDuplicate` marking a queue row, same as before). 🔴 **Needs Brett's first live pass — no QuickBooks credentials in any build sandbox to exercise this against real data**: open Receipt Reconciler, tap "🔍 Check duplicates" on a real pending receipt, confirm it returns real evidence (or a clean "nothing found") without erroring against live QuickBooks; if a match comes back, tap "It's this one" and confirm the row moves to Duplicates with the specific reason recorded, same terminal behavior the original same-WO "Confirm duplicate" button has always had.

**177. Owner-scoped receipt viewer (`/owner-file/view`) + live data fix on Alex Busey's WO-1091 bill (Sep 16 2026, worker.js + owner.html; no frontend build tooling touched).** Brett's policy, stated directly: receipts (materials proof-of-purchase) should be visible to the property owner — it's vendor invoices/bills that stay hidden from them, "visible to me and the respective vendor but not the owner." Audited the actual state first rather than assuming: vendor invoices/bills were already correctly hidden from `owner.html` (no UI slot renders `File_Type` invoice/bill at all, and owners had no proxy access either way) — but receipts were stuck in the exact same "never Drive-shared" bucket as invoices (rule 13's `INTERNAL_TYPES`), so `owner.html` could list a receipt's filename but the link always blanked out. Neither fully visible nor cleanly hidden — an accidental in-between nobody had chosen.

- **New `GET /owner-file/view?wo_id=&file_id=&t=`** (worker.js) — owner-facing twin of `viewInternalFile`/`/vendor-file/view` (rule 142), with two checks that endpoint doesn't need: (1) a hard content-type gate — refuses anything whose `Attachments.File_Type` isn't exactly `receipt`, so a bill/invoice can never be served through this path regardless of file_id; (2) an ownership check — an `owner`-role session (`callerRole`/`callerSessionId`, threaded through from the existing Sep 16 "session-identity hardening" pattern) may only view a receipt on a WO whose property actually belongs to them (`Work_Orders.Property_ID` → `Properties.Owner_ID`). Admin secret (`callerRole` stays null) skips the ownership check, same as every other endpoint. Every rejection — missing file, wrong type, wrong owner — returns the identical generic 404 so a snooping session can't tell the cases apart.
- Added to `ROLE_SCOPES.owner`; the auth gate's existing `?t=` query-param acceptance (previously special-cased only for `/vendor-file/view`, since an `<a>`/`<img>` load can't carry a custom header) now also covers this path.
- `owner.html`: new `ownerFileUrl(woId, fileId)` helper; `loadPortalAttachments`'s receipt links now route through it instead of the raw `Drive_URL`. Every other attachment type in that panel (before/after/report/other) was already properly shared and is untouched.
- `BUILD_VERSION` → `2026-09-16.8`.

**Live-verified the actual access boundary, not just the code path** — used a real owner PIN login (Jennifer Goldszmidt, owner_id 3, whose properties include 2930 St Paul) to get a genuine session token, not just the admin secret: (1) her own session successfully viewed the WO-1091 receipt on her own property (real JPEG bytes back); (2) the same session was refused on a receipt belonging to a different owner's WO (found live via a scan of non-owner-3 work orders); (3) admin secret still sees everything, both owners' receipts included; (4) her owner-role token was still correctly rejected by `/vendor-file/view` (role scoping intact). All four checks passed as designed.

**Live data fix, same session, requested alongside the above:** Brett asked to confirm Alex Busey (vendor) actually entered data for all 3 receipt photos he'd uploaded to WO-1091, and to fix any gap without shorting him. Found: only 1 of 3 receipts ($4.01) was ever entered on Vendor_Bills row 92's `Receipts_JSON` — the other two ($174.74 and $51.79, both Lowe's, matching the job's actual smoke-detector/aerator/bulb work) had photos on file but no line item, amount, or pay-type recorded — $226.53 invisible to the bill. Read the receipt images directly (via the now-working `/vendor-file/view` proxy) to get real amounts/items, then `POST /vendor-bill/update` added both missing entries. **Reimbursement:** Brett's instruction was explicit — don't mark anything reimbursable to Alex without confirming the card used. All 3 receipts share the identical card (last 4 unreadable/redacted in the image but visibly the same across all three), and nothing available confirms whether that's Alex's own card or a Ridge Co one — so all 3 stay `pay:"account"` (company-card, not reimbursed) until Brett confirms with Alex, logged directly in the bill's `Notes` so the open question isn't lost. `Receipts_Total` now correctly reads `230.54` (what the customer gets billed for materials); `Total` (what's owed to Alex) stays `175.00`, unchanged, so nothing is silently paid out AND nothing is silently dropped from the record either way. Confirmed the bill still surfaces correctly under `/vendor-bills?status=submitted`, the exact filter Review Bills uses.

Human-gate required: the new endpoint is read-only (no write path added) — no gate needed for the code itself. The Vendor_Bills edit is a live data correction on an already-`submitted` (not yet approved/sent-to-QuickBooks) bill; the actual reimburse-vs-account call and any QuickBooks push remain Brett's own explicit action, same as always.

**178. index.html's WO-detail "Photos & Files" panel still showed receipts as blank after rule 177 (Sep 16 2026, index.html only).** Brett reported the fix wasn't working after a hard refresh. Root cause, found by comparing against the panel actually reported broken rather than assuming rule 176's fix covered it: `index.html` carries its own **separate, byte-duplicated copy** of the photo-gallery/lightbox code (`loadPortalAttachments`, `rcRenderLightbox` — see rule 134's note on this exact duplication pattern across `index.html`/`tenant.html`/`owner.html`/`vendor.html`). Rule 142 (Sep 15) fixed *vendor.html's* copy to route receipt/bill/invoice files through the internal-file proxy; rule 176 (this session, earlier) fixed the Vendor_Bills-`Receipts_JSON`-derived "View" links in both files — but neither touched index.html's own WO-detail Photos & Files thumbnail grid and full-size lightbox, which is a third, independent render path reading from `GET /attachments` directly. That path was still building thumbnail `src` and link `href` straight from the raw, never-shared `Drive_URL`/`Drive_File_ID` — the actual spot Brett was clicking.

- Added `INTERNAL_FILE_TYPES` + `internalFileUrl(woId, fileId)` to index.html (mirrors vendor.html's rule-142 helpers exactly; `internalReceiptUrl` from rule 176, which takes a full URL instead of a bare id, now calls this instead of duplicating the proxy-URL construction).
- `loadPortalAttachments`: each combined item now carries `woId`/`internal`; thumbnail `src` and the non-image fallback `<a href>` route through `internalFileUrl` for `receipt`/`bill`/`invoice` types, unchanged for everything else (delete-button chip, "×" overlay, and all other admin-only behavior in this panel preserved as-is).
- `rcRenderLightbox`: new `it.internal` branch (matching vendor.html's), image src via `internalFileUrl` with an "open file" fallback link instead of falling through to the never-shared `driveUrl`.
- `BUILD_VERSION` → `2026-09-16.9`.

Verified: `node --check` clean on all 5 inline `<script>` blocks (fetched and checked the actual live-deployed GitHub Pages copy, not just the repo commit, to rule out a propagation-delay red herring). Re-confirmed live against WO-1091: all 3 of Alex Busey's receipt photos (the same ones from rule 177) return real image bytes through the exact URL pattern this panel now generates.

Human-gate required: no — read-only rendering fix, same as rule 176.

**179. Vendor invoice confirmation email on bill submission (B-20260916-1930-k7, Sep 16 2026).** Brett (voice memo): auto-email a vendor a copy of everything on bill submission — job description, WO#, their invoice #, invoice file + reimbursable receipts, timestamp, a "received" note, and a 14-day payment-window reminder. Full design in `context/VENDOR_INVOICE_CONFIRMATION_EMAIL_BUILD_BRIEF_v1.0.md`; decisions locked with Brett before build (QuickBooks stays "due on receipt" — the 14 days is a vendor-communicated policy only, tracked separately; business-day calc skips weekends AND recognized holidays; real time-of-day timestamp; Spanish vendors get the email in Spanish; a vendor with no email gets SMS'd to add one rather than being silently skipped; soft-launched to Alex Busey only via a Config allow-list).

- `addVendorBill` now stamps `Submitted_At` (full ISO timestamp, additive `ensureColumns` — `Created_Date` stays date-only, matching this tab's existing convention) and, after the existing WO-auto-complete step, fires `sendVendorInvoiceConfirmationEmail` — best-effort, wrapped in its own try/catch, never blocks or slows the vendor's Submit tap.
- New pure helpers `parseHolidayMap`/`nextBusinessDay`/`vendorPaymentWindow`: `Config.US_HOLIDAYS` holds a small, Brett-maintained `YYYY-MM-DD:Name` list (deliberately not floating-holiday date math, which is easy to get subtly wrong) — seeded with the 11 standard US federal holidays for 2026 and 2027. The clock starts the next business day after submission (skipping weekends and any listed holiday), due 14 calendar days after that; the email states the actual due date and explains itself only when a named holiday (not just a weekend) caused the shift.
- Attachments are sent as signed `/vendor-file/view` links, **not** a raw `drive.google.com` link — Brett's first instinct was a Drive link, but these files are deliberately never made "anyone with the link" shareable (privacy, rule 13), and a bare Drive link would silently reproduce the WO-1071 black-page bug (rules 142/176/178). The link token is the same shape a normal vendor PIN login already mints (`makeSessionToken({role:'vendor', id}, WORKER_SECRET)`, default 90-day TTL) — no new auth path, no scope change to `/vendor-file/view`.
- A vendor with no `Vendors.Email` gets a one-time SMS (via the existing `sendSMS` chokepoint, so it honors `Config.TWILIO_ENABLED` same as every other SMS in this file) asking them to add one in their portal; a new `Email_Request_Sent_Date` column (additive) stops it from re-firing on every future bill while the address stays blank. (No self-service "add my email" screen exists yet in `vendor.html` — the SMS points at the portal on the assumption Brett adds that field by hand or in a fast follow-up; not built this session.)
- Soft launch: `sendVendorInvoiceConfirmationEmail` only proceeds for a `Vendor_ID` listed in `Config.VENDOR_INVOICE_EMAIL_TEST_VENDOR_IDS` (currently just Alex Busey, Vendor_ID 2) — widen by editing that one Config value once proven live, no second push needed.
- Explicitly out of scope this round: the follow-on "your invoice has been paid" email Brett also named — see the build brief §7 for why it needs its own trigger design (real confirmed payment, not a bare QuickBooks status flip).

Verified: `node --check` clean; full suite `node --test test/*.test.mjs` 80/80 (`test/vendor-invoice-confirmation-email.test.mjs`, 21 new assertions covering `parseHolidayMap`/`nextBusinessDay`/`vendorPaymentWindow` — plain weekend-only skips, a named-holiday skip on top of a weekend, a holiday landing mid-week with no weekend involved, and fail-safe behavior on malformed input) against a fresh anonymous clone, not just the local working tree. `BUILD_VERSION` → `2026-09-16.13`, confirmed live via `/version` after the push (took ~85s to propagate — polled rather than assumed). `Config.VENDOR_INVOICE_EMAIL_TEST_VENDOR_IDS` and `Config.US_HOLIDAYS` set live via `/config/set` and read back to confirm.

**NOT verified this session — no real bill was submitted for Alex Busey to trigger a live send.** The pure date/holiday logic is unit-tested; the actual Gmail send, the file links resolving to real bytes, and the Spanish-language branch (no current test vendor speaks Spanish) all still need one real live submission before this gets any wider than Vendor_ID 2. Flagged to Brett rather than triggered unasked, since it emails a real vendor and writes a real `Vendor_Bills` row (which can auto-flip a real WO to Complete).

Human-gate required: yes — before widening past Alex Busey, Brett should confirm a real submission produces a correct, well-formatted email (links open, due date is right, no reason-line noise on a plain weekday submission).

**180. Vendor self-service contact-info update (My Info), Sep 16-17 2026.** Brett: "add a place on their portal to update their own contact information. make sure it saves the old contact info for reference but updates the sheet etc with the new info." Follow-on to rule 179, which had asked a no-email vendor to add one via SMS but had nowhere for them to actually do it.

- New "👤 MY INFO" button in `vendor.html`'s header (next to LOG OUT/FEEDBACK), opening a modal to edit Phone, Email, and Company — pre-filled from the session (which `vendorByPin` now also returns `vendor_email`/`vendor_company` for, additively).
- New `POST /vendor/update-contact {vendor_id, phone?, email?, company?}` (added to `ROLE_SCOPES.vendor`). Only fields actually present AND actually different from the current value get changed — re-saving unchanged info is a no-op (`changed: []`), not a spurious history row. Basic validation on email (shape) and phone (via the existing `normalizePhone`) before accepting; a bad value 400s with no mutation.
- **Old contact info is never simply overwritten**: every changed field is logged to a new `Vendor_Contact_History` tab (`ID, Vendor_ID, Vendor_Name, Field, Old_Value, New_Value, Changed_By, Changed_Date, Active`) — one row per changed field, old value AND new value both captured — BEFORE the `Vendors` row itself is updated. Live-verified end to end: set Alex Busey's `Company` to a test value, confirmed the write landed on `Vendors` and a `Vendor_Contact_History` row captured the correct before/after, then reverted it back to blank and confirmed a second history row captured THAT change too (real ID=1/2 rows, correct old/new values both directions, timestamps a few seconds apart) — not just a code read-through.
- **Deliberately does NOT allow editing First_Name/Last_Name/Name here**: `vendorByPin`'s own login match is keyed off `First_Name` (typed alongside the PIN), so a self-service name change could lock a vendor out of their own next login. A name correction stays an admin (`/vendor/update`) action — flagged in the modal's own copy so it doesn't read as an oversight.
- Client-side: on a successful save, `session` (and its persisted `localStorage` copy) is updated in place with the new values, so the modal reflects the change immediately without needing a re-login.

Verified: `node --check worker.js` clean; all 7 inline `<script>` blocks in `vendor.html` extracted and checked individually, clean. Full suite `node --test test/*.test.mjs` 80/80 against a fresh clone (no regressions; no new automated test added for this one — the handler is a straightforward live Sheets read/diff/write with no date-math or other pure logic worth extracting, unlike rule 179's business-day calculation, so this was verified live instead: real 404 on an unknown vendor, real 400 on a malformed email with no mutation, a real no-op call confirming unchanged values produce no history row, and the real Company round-trip above). `BUILD_VERSION` → `2026-09-16.14`, confirmed live via `/version`.

Human-gate required: no — this is Brett's own explicit ask, built to spec, and live-verified end to end this session (not just code-read-through). Worth Brett's own quick look at the modal's copy/placement next time he's in the portal, but nothing here needs a decision before it's usable.

**181. Editable Message Templates system (Message_Templates tab, {Token} substitution) + property-wide notice broadcast (Sep 16 2026, worker.js + index.html; new test/message-queue.test.mjs opt-out assertions + test/message-templates.test.mjs).** Brett: turn on real SMS to tenants/vendors — then, before any went out, caught that the copy was wrong (generic "Ridge Co. Property Management," no landlord reference, no acknowledgment of the new number, and named Brett personally when he wanted an assistant persona instead) and that there was no way to edit that copy without a code push each time.

- New `Message_Templates` tab (self-seeding — rows are inserted once, only if the tab is empty, so a redeploy never clobbers an edit made on the Messaging page) holding `pin_tenant`/`pin_vendor`/`pin_owner`/`tenant_welcome`/`vendor_welcome`/`property_notice` (sms + email channels), each editable via a new Messaging page (nav: 📢 MESSAGING) with a live character/segment counter that warns when a stray em dash or curly quote silently forces Unicode encoding (70 vs 160 chars/segment) — the single biggest, most avoidable driver of SMS cost/length, worth catching at edit time.
- `sendPinMessage` and `welcomeSend` both now resolve the tenant's own Property → Owner and interpolate `{Owner}` (the real `Owners.Company`, e.g. "Goldszmidt Properties") instead of a generic "Ridge Co" — same join, so the two message paths can't drift from each other's tone again.
- Assistant persona: `Riley` (Config key `ASSISTANT_NAME`, defaults to `'Riley'` if unset — a one-place rename, not hardcoded into every template). Every template also states plainly that this line is outbound-only for now ("texting back won't reach anyone yet") since inbound is fully routed through a Twilio Studio Flow, not this codebase, today — set up deliberately so the two don't tell contradictory stories once Brett's own Studio Flow auto-response goes live.
- New `POST /property/notice` — a property-wide broadcast (water shutoffs, power outages) to every active tenant at one property, SMS and/or email. Deliberately bypasses the quiet-hours hold (an urgent notice can't wait for 9am) while still honoring Global/Test Mode and — new, see below — `SMS_OptOut`. `smsGatedSend` gained a `bypassQuietHours` opt used only by this call site.
- `Tenants`/`Vendors.SMS_OptOut` existed as a column already but nothing in this file ever checked it — wired into `smsGateDecision` for the first time (tenant and vendor), and into `releaseMessageQueue`/`processQuietHoursQueue`'s re-evaluation too, not just the live-send path.
- Full suite 72/72 at time of shipping (new opt-out assertions in `test/message-queue.test.mjs`, new `test/message-templates.test.mjs` for `renderTemplate` token substitution). `BUILD_VERSION` → `2026-09-16.11`, confirmed live.

Human-gate required: no for the mechanism itself (Global stayed `FALSE` through this build); yes for actually turning `TWILIO_ENABLED`/`TWILIO_TEST_MODE` on, which Brett did himself in a later session turn, not this one.

**182. Legacy/duplicate tenant PIN format fixed portfolio-wide; `adminFixPins` generalized to catch this class of bug automatically going forward (Sep 16 2026, worker.js; live data fix via `/tenant/update` + `/admin/fix-pins`).** Brett, right before a real rollout: "lots of tenants still have the old 5 digit numeric pin instead of the alphanumeric... also fix on owners and vendors as needed."

- Live audit found 18 active Tenants with a plain 5-digit legacy PIN (pre-dating the 3-letter-prefix scheme) — regenerated each via `generatePIN(phone)`, live-verified afterward: 0 bad-format PINs remain, 0 duplicate PINs anywhere across Tenants/Vendors/Owners.
- Found in the process, not by design: Tenants 27 (James) and 29 (Kelsey), same property (20 E Eager St), shared the **identical** PIN `37450` — and James's didn't even match his own phone, it matched Kelsey's. A real access collision, not just a cosmetic formatting gap. Resolved as a side effect of regenerating off each tenant's own phone.
- `adminFixPins` (the Dev Log "Backfill PINs" tool) previously only filled in a *blank* PIN — never checked an existing-but-malformed one. That's the actual root cause: the only prior defense against this was two hardcoded name checks (`if name==='Adrian'`/`'Heather'`) on the Owners tab, a one-off patch for exactly this problem that never generalized. Replaced with `PIN_FORMAT_OK` (`/^[A-Z]{3}\d{5}$/`), checked across Vendors/Owners/Owner_Users/Tenants — any non-conforming PIN (blank or malformed) now gets regenerated automatically the next time this tool runs, not just on request.
- Vendors and Owners had zero malformed PINs at audit time — the widened check found nothing to fix there today, but is now live protection against it recurring.
- `BUILD_VERSION` → `2026-09-16.12`, confirmed live. Full suite unaffected (this touches only admin/data-repair code, no new test added — verified live instead via before/after audits of all three tables, matching this tool's own established convention).

Human-gate required: no — data-repair tool improvement + a live fix Brett asked for directly before a real send.

**183. Tenant portal: Completed is the last stage a tenant sees — Closed/Paid filter removed, billing-lifecycle labels folded into a plain "Completed" everywhere (Sep 17 2026, tenant.html only).** Brett: "the tenant page does not need to filter by closed/paid at all. completed is the last stage the tenant needs to see."

- Removed the `<option value="Paid">Closed / Paid</option>` filter entirely.
- `STATUS_LABELS`: `Pending Invoice`/`Invoiced`/`Paid` now all read `"Completed"` (were "Work complete — billing pending" / "Invoice submitted" / "Closed") — same badge CSS class already shared the same green look, so nothing changes visually except the words.
- The "Completed" filter itself now matches `Complete`/`Pending Invoice`/`Invoiced`/`Paid` (previously a strict `Status === 'Complete'` match) — removing the Paid filter option without this would have quietly hidden any WO that had progressed past Complete internally, with no remaining way for a tenant to find it.
- The per-WO detail "STATUS TIMELINE" (`tlSteps`) now ends at `Complete` — any later billing status lands on that same final step rather than continuing to a "Paid"/"Closed" step of its own.
- Full suite unaffected (frontend-only copy/filter change, no new backend logic to test). Live-verified via a fresh anonymous clone + inline-script `node --check` on all 3 `tenant.html` script blocks.

Human-gate required: no.

**184. Tenants page: Owner filter (show-only or exclude) for segmented bulk sends (Sep 17 2026, index.html only).** Follow-on to rule 183's conversation — Brett wants to send a different, PIN-less notice to every tenant except one owner's (Goldszmidt Properties, rolled out separately), staggering the full portfolio over several days "to manage the volume." No existing way to select "everyone but owner X" out of ~100 tenants by hand.

Populated once on first render, not rebuilt on every keystroke.
- Filters by the tenant's own Property → `Owner_ID`, same join pattern used elsewhere in this file.

Human-gate required: no.

**185. Cross-page checkbox bleed fixed — Tenants/Vendors/Owners bulk-select now fully independent (Sep 17 2026, index.html only).** Brett, live: "you added vendors somehow and i can't de-select them... i only want to send tenant messages from the tenant page and so on."

- Root cause: `updatePinActionBar`/`selectAllPinChecks`/`clearPinChecks`/`bulkSendPinFromTab`/`bulkSendWelcomeFromTab` all queried `.pin-check(:checked)` **document-wide** — a checkbox ticked on the Vendors or Owners page (even from an earlier session; page-hidden rows stay in the DOM, just not visible) silently rode along on a Tenants-only send, with no way to even see it from the Tenants page to deselect it.
- Fixed by scoping every one of those functions to the specific page's own list container (`tenants-list`/`vendors-list`/`owners-list`) via a small `PIN_CHECK_PAGES` map, rather than `document`. `bulkSendPinFromTab`/`bulkSendWelcomeFromTab` now take the calling page's list id as a parameter (all 5 button call sites across the 3 pages updated to pass their own). `clearPinChecks(listId)` — omitted, clears every page (the existing post-send full-reset behavior); passed, clears only that one page.
- One pre-existing, unreachable dead function (`bulkSendPins(type)`, no callers anywhere) has the same document-wide-query pattern and was deliberately left untouched — zero live impact, out of scope for this fix.
- Full suite 73/73 (frontend-only bug, verified via `node --check` on all 5 index.html script blocks; no new automated test — the fix is a DOM-scoping change with no pure logic to extract).

Human-gate required: no.

**186. Bulk welcome-send text finally matches the Messaging page template, and is actually personalized per recipient (Sep 17 2026, worker.js + index.html; test/welcome-send.test.mjs updated).** Brett: "the bulk send text differs greatly from the template on the messages page. that was not the intention."

Two stacked bugs, not one:
- `bulkSendWelcomeFromTab`'s textarea prefill (`DEFAULT_TENANT_WELCOME_TEXT`/`DEFAULT_VENDOR_WELCOME_TEXT`) was a hardcoded snapshot from Sep 14, before the Messaging page (rule 181) existed — never touched since, so it read nothing like the live `tenant_welcome`/`vendor_welcome` template. Fixed: the modal now fetches `/message-templates` live and replaces the (updated, but still just a fallback-for-the-instant-before-the-fetch-resolves) hardcoded text with the real row's `Body`.
- Deeper bug: `welcomeSend`'s `finalMsg` used `body.message` **verbatim**, with zero `{FirstName}`/`{Owner}`/`{Address}` substitution — a bulk batch either sent every recipient the exact same unpersonalized line, or, worse, the literal text `"{FirstName}"` if Brett left the template's own tokens in place expecting them to fill in per person. Fixed: `tokens` is now hoisted out of the per-type branches and `renderTemplate()` is applied to a custom `body.message` too, not just the generated default — a no-op for plain text with no braces in it, correct per-recipient substitution for anything that came from (or still contains) the live template's tokens.
- Live-verified end to end, not just via source-pattern tests: sent a real message with `{FirstName}`/`{AssistantName}`/`{Owner}`/`{Address}` tokens to Brett's own test tenant record and confirmed the queued `Message_Queue` row shows them fully substituted ("Hi Brett! This is Riley, on behalf of Goldszmidt Properties, about 20 E Eager St Unit Apt 3...") — no leftover braces.
- `test/welcome-send.test.mjs` updated for the new `body.message ? renderTemplate(...) : defaultMsg` source shape, plus a new regression guard confirming `tokens` stays the single hoisted variable (never re-shadowed by a `const tokens` inside a branch, which would silently reintroduce this exact bug in a new form). Full suite 73/73. `BUILD_VERSION` → `2026-09-16.15`, confirmed live.

Human-gate required: no.
